# Native canvas program: every ruling, in the order made

The decision log of the native-canvas program (Plans 1-8, 2026-10-29 to 2026-10-31), copied from the SDD ledgers before they were deleted. Each line is `Ruling: <what was decided> — <why> — <what it costs if wrong>`. "(delegate, Pn.Tm)" marks a ruling a controller-delegate agent made during the parallel run (program rule R-DELEGATE); the others are the controller's, including the pre-flight rulings that amended each plan before it ran. Where the spec or a plan and a ruling disagree, the ruling is what was built.

The `.superpowers/sdd` ledgers this log mentions were the run's git-ignored working files and have been removed.

## Program-wide (run rules and cross-plan rulings)

- before deleting a plan workspace, copy its `Ruling:` lines below. After Plan 8: finishing-a-development-branch.
- Ruling: R-LANES — tasks run in 5 parallel git-worktree lanes (.claude/worktrees/lane-1..5, branches lane-1..5), each with its own target/; a task commits on its lane branch; after its review passes, an integrator rebases the lane onto main, re-runs the gates and fast-forwards main, one integration at a time — the user asked for parallel agents (max 5); the briefs' "commit on main" becomes "commit on your lane branch", with the same end state on main — costs: rebase conflicts in shared files, resolved mechanically per native-canvas-analysis/dag.md "Shared files that merge mechanically".
- Ruling: R-ROOT (2026-10-30 17:55; OVERRIDES step 4 of the integrator prompt) — the main checkout `<repo>` belongs to the user's own work now: it is checked out on the user's branch feat/terminal-panes (commit 815de4c, terminal split-panes spec and plans), not on main, and may hold their uncommitted edits. Nobody in this run may stash, clean, reset, check out, merge into, add, commit or delete anything in the main checkout, or touch the branch feat/terminal-panes. Integrators advance main WITHOUT the main checkout: `B=$(git -C <repo> branch --show-current)`; if B is not main, run `git -C <lane> fetch . <lane-branch>:main` (a fast-forward-only update of the main ref that touches no working tree; it refuses a non-fast-forward); only if B is main, run `git -C <repo> merge --ff-only <lane-branch>` (and if that refuses because local changes or untracked files would be overwritten, return FAILED naming the paths and touch nothing). If the update refuses because main moved, rebase again. Main may gain commits that aren't from this run; rebase onto them as usual. To read main's code — and the spec and plan files too, whose paths in prompts point into the main checkout — use `git show main:<path>` (e.g. `git -C <lane> show main:docs/superpowers/plans/<plan>.md`) or your lane, never the main checkout's files (they are the user's branch and miss everything this run lands after 504abec, such as later live-check notes); a live check edits the plan file in its own lane — costs: nothing for main; the user's branch is left alone and merges main when they choose.
- Ruling: R-DELEGATE — during the parallel run, ⚠️ items, plan-conflict findings, implementer blockers and breaker adjudications are decided by a controller-delegate agent (opus) holding the spec, plans and ledgers; each writes "- Ruling: (delegate, Pn.Tm) ..." to its plan's ledger, and the controller reviews every delegate ruling before finishing — the run can't pause on the controller without idling the other lanes — costs: a wrong delegate ruling is reworked later.
- Ruling: R-REQUEUE (2026-10-30 15:00) — a scheduler bug (a dangling else in the workflow script) re-queues a task's PLAN-CONFLICT items as fix-loop findings even when the delegate dismissed them. A fixer handed a finding that a "(delegate, <task>) PLAN-CONFLICT … → dismiss" ruling covers changes no code for it and says so in its fix report, quoting the ruling; the re-reviewer marks such a finding ADDRESSED (dismissed by ruling) and never re-opens it — audit at 15:00: P7.T1, P2.T3 and P7.T5 met this and their fixers already kept the dismissals; the script is fixed for later runs — costs: one wasted fix round per affected task in the running run.
- Ruling: R-LIVE — live checks run unattended. The agent drives the app only over the control socket (open_page, state, layout, canvas ops incl. get/command/pointer/key/type once they exist) and checks results with `get` and window screenshots. Steps that need real hands (trackpad or mouse feel, real IME composition, cursor shapes, judging smoothness, a real old board, a Finder drag) go into the plan file under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)", with exact steps and what to look for, committed with the live-check notes; they don't fail the task — the user isn't at the keyboard during the run and synthetic input can't reach the window (memory visual-verify-sawit) — costs: those items stay unverified until the user runs them; a defect found then costs a fix after the run.
- Ruling: R-LAUNCH — every live check launches and stops the app per Plan 1 C4/C6/C7 (this overrides Plan 3 C17's pgrep stop): from its lane, S=<the run's scratch folder>/live-<task>; mkdir -p $S/home $S/proj; `SAWIT_HOME=$S/home SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS=1 RUST_LOG=sawit=debug <lane>/target/debug/sawit --foreground "$S/proj" 2>"$S/sawit.log" &` then PID=$! (drop SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS once Plan 8 Task 1 has removed the flag). The socket is $S/home/sawit.sock, or for a long path the /tmp/sawit-$USER-<hash>.sock fallback: find it with `lsof -p $PID -a -U`, never by trying every /tmp/sawit-*.sock (that drives the user's own app). Messages: bytes `sawit-control/1\n` + one JSON object, then shut down the write half and read the reply. Stop with `/bin/kill $PID` only. Memory notes: <the project's Claude Code memory folder>/visual-verify-sawit.md and shell-kill-alias.md — costs nothing.
- Ruling: R-TEXTEDIT (controller, 2026-10-31 06:10) — one root cause, three final-review findings: a command that clears ds.text_edit (select.none, page.select, page.delete, deleting the node being typed in, …) leaves FigCraft's "Edit text" interaction open, so the next Move-tool click cancels it and the typed text is lost with no undo step (Plan 6 final C1: right-click and ≡/context-menu commands; Plan 4 final residual: layers-panel page/eye/padlock/drop actions; the command op during a user's edit). The fix is ONE BoardView helper — end an open text edit (guard::run("ui.textEnd", || textedit::end(session)), toast on error) before any command that isn't a text.* or clipboard id — called by every UI command path (BoardView::choose, run_menu, on_right_down before its selection rule, the layers panel's actions) and by the command op (ops.rs command arm and command.batch steps, which end the edit in the session directly). FINAL.P6's fix wave creates the helper with its C1 fix; FINAL.P7's fix wave routes the layers panel and the command op through it and folds any separate guard into it, so one path remains; each adds a test that typed text survives the next click — why: the spec's never-lose-data intent (D5, D15) and a reasonable user's expectation; one helper stops a fourth path from reopening the gap — costs: a few call sites and tests across two fix waves.
- Ruling: R-LOAD — P1.T7 measures render times while other lanes compile, so it records the load average (`sysctl -n vm.loadavg`) beside its numbers and labels them "under build load"; P8.T4 (which runs last, on a quiet machine, with the release build) is the authoritative budget measurement — parallel lanes make an early quiet measurement impossible — costs: Plan 1's numbers are only indicative.
- Ruling: R-EHFRAME — the `ld: __eh_frame section too large (max 16MB)` linker warning that appears in `cargo test -p sawit` once FigCraft links into the app (found by P1.T4; app binary will follow at P1.T6) is fixed ONCE, by P1.T5: add `[profile.dev.package.<crate>] opt-level = 3` entries to the root Cargo.toml for the FigCraft render-path crates and tiny-skia (use `cargo tree -p sawit -e normal` to name them: figcraft-render/-engine/-text/-geom/-document as present, tiny-skia, tiny-skia-path, and the font crates FigCraft rasterises with), with a comment in the style of the existing syntect block ("hot CPU path: debug renders"), until `cargo test -p sawit` and `cargo build -p sawit` link with no __eh_frame warning; record the test binary's __eh_frame size before/after (`otool -l <bin> | grep -A3 __eh_frame`) in the report. Until it lands, other tasks' reviews treat that one warning as known (R-EHFRAME), not as their own finding — it follows the repo's existing pattern for hot debug paths, also makes Plan 1 Task 7's debug render timings meaningful, and is one config change instead of a warning every app task trips over — costs: one-time rebuild of those crates at opt-level 3 in every lane and slower clean debug builds; if opt-level 3 doesn't get the binaries under 16 MB, P1.T5 reports DONE_WITH_CONCERNS with the measured sizes rather than silencing the lint.
- Ruling: R-EHFRAME (amended 2026-10-30, accepting delegate ruling P1.T5) — P1.T5's 10 opt-level-3 entries stay (sawit test binary 14.26 MiB, fast debug renders), but once P1.T6 wires the canvas in, the debug app link has ~17.3 MiB of __eh_frame even with every FigCraft crate at opt-level 3. From P1.T6 on, the linker warning "__eh_frame section too large (max 16MB) to encode dwarf unwind offsets in compact unwind table …" in any debug link (app or test binary) is KNOWN under R-EHFRAME in every task: it is no task's finding, and no task changes profiles, linker flags or lint levels for it (any other linker message is still a finding). P8.T4 records the debug app's __eh_frame size beside its release numbers; the user chooses later whether to set opt-level for all dependencies in dev (option b) — the warning only slows exception unwinding in debug builds (release has 2.23 MiB), and option b means a full dependency rebuild in 5 lanes with the disk tight — costs: one known warning line in debug links until the user decides.
- Ruling: P7-X3 (controller; overrides the Plan 1 final review delegate's "no change" on M1) — P7.T8 adds a write-target guard: (1) ops::refused (the command op, and command.batch steps at any depth) refuses a path-taking engine command — file.export, file.exportSvg and file.exportFramesPdf with `path`; file.exportLayers and data.fill with `dir`; any other id at rev 0971d09 that writes to a caller-given path — when its target is a .figcraft or .excalidraw file or one of their .bak, .lossy.bak or .tmp siblings (for a `dir`, when the files it would write match those names), answering {ok:false, error:"<id> would overwrite a canvas file"}; (2) sawit-mcp's canvas_export (crates/mcp/src/app.rs) refuses the same targets for output_path on the app and headless paths. Tests: an export aimed at .nyawit/canvas.figcraft is refused and the file is byte-identical afterwards; the same export to a .png/.svg path still writes. P7.T8's files gain crates/mcp/src/app.rs — why: the spec's never-lose-data intent (D5, Review Focus) and a reasonable user's expectation; offline (P7.T5's headless path) nothing holds another copy, so one mistaken agent export destroys a board, and the guard keeps every legitimate export — costs: ~15 lines and two tests; an agent can no longer write image bytes to a path named *.figcraft.
- 4. Only after nothing is lost: remove lanes/worktrees, drop superseded stashes and parked branches, delete the SDD workspaces after collecting every `Ruling:` line (SDD finish), then finishing-a-development-branch.

## Plan 1 (foundation)

- Ruling: R2 — crates/board/src/lib.rs declares only modules whose files exist; T1 adds `pub mod doc` (+ re-exports), T2/T3/T6 each add their own `pub mod` and include lib.rs in their `git add` — declaring missing modules does not compile — costs nothing if wrong (same end state).
- Ruling: R3 — the plan's test/interface code names FigCraft API as understood; where rev 0971d09 spells a method/field differently, implementers use the real API and keep every assertion and value, reporting each deviation — the spec pins the rev, the plan argues intent — costs a reviewer round if a deviation changes meaning.
- Ruling: R4 — Task 0 Step 1's commit is skipped: spec and plans are already committed (15cf5f5, 0f5df5e) and the tree is clean — costs nothing.
- Ruling: R5 — baseline numbers (Task 0) and render times (Task 7) are written into the plan file and committed as `docs:` commits, because Plan 8 reads them from there — costs a doc commit if the user wanted them elsewhere.
- Ruling: R6 — Task 0 (preflight, commands only, no code) is run by the controller and gets no task review — there is no code to review — costs nothing.
- Ruling: R7 — `open_canvas` keeps its `Option` and returns `Option<CanvasTab>`; "returns CanvasTab" in the plan names the payload — today's callers use `.ok_or(..)` — costs a trivial signature change if wrong.
- Ruling: R8 — commit trailers name the model that wrote the commit (a Sonnet implementer's commit says "Claude Sonnet 5.5"), not the plan's fixed "Claude Opus 5.5" line — the trailer exists for truthful AI attribution and a Sonnet commit labelled Opus would be false — costs a cosmetic trailer rewrite before any push if the user wants one fixed line.
- Ruling: R9 — (from Task 1's Important finding) Task 2 adds `store::open_with_issues(path) -> Result<(BoardDoc, Vec<String>), BoardError>` (`open` delegates); when the load reports issues it copies the original file to `<path>.bak` before returning, so the first autosave can't silently drop what the loader left out; Task 5 shows the issues as a toast, as FigCraft's own file.open does — the spec's never-lose-data intent (D5 backup, Review Focus 1) covers partially damaged files — costs one extra function and a .bak the user may not need.
- Ruling: R9a — amends R9: Task 2 makes `from_bytes_with_issues` use `load_bytes_with_versions` and append a note for each thing a Sawit save would drop (non-empty version history, branches, images left in the file), so the issue list is a complete "a save here loses something" signal that triggers R9's backup; doc comments say so — the re-review showed healthy history/branches vanish with an empty list — costs a few lines in doc.rs if wrong.
- Ruling: R10 — accept Task 2's documented deviations: `nyawit::ensure` runs only when `.nyawit/` is missing (a user-deleted .gitignore stays deleted, as nyawit.rs:9 "written once" documents); an emptying save or a lossy open is refused (error, nothing overwritten) when its .bak can't be written — both keep the never-lose-data guarantees true on failure — costs a .gitignore not re-created in a half-made .nyawit (minor 3 below).
- Ruling: P1-C1 — Frame.view is the snapped View that Retained::render returns, as the Task 3 note says, and the image is that view's size. renders_a_job asserts that the image size equals (frame.view.width, frame.view.height), that frame.view covers the requested view (origin <= (-100,-100), 401x301 here), and that the key matches. — why: The plan's own steps mandate Retained, and placement() is only right for the snapped view. Keeping 400x300 would force a crop that misplaces frames at fractional origins. — costs if wrong: Only a test edit. The alternative, a crop, costs a copy per frame and sub-pixel misplacement.
- Ruling: P1-C2 — Replace 'count <= 3' with 'keys arrive in increasing order and the last is 3'. Add a deterministic test of the latest-wins slot itself: make the Mutex<Option<Job>>+Condvar a small private type with put/take, and check that three puts with no taker leave exactly key 3 pending. — why: Reviewers flag assertions that can't fail. The slot test proves 'replaced, never queued' without depending on timing. — costs if wrong: A few test lines.
- Ruling: P1-C3 — Task 4 adds `log = "0.4"` to crates/app/Cargo.toml. Cargo.lock already has log 0.4.34, which sawit-board uses. — why: worker.rs doesn't compile without it. It is the dependency the plan forgot. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P1-C4 — Task 4 adds `env_logger = { version = "0.11", default-features = false }` to crates/app. At the top of main() it installs the logger only when RUST_LOG is set (env_logger::try_init(), error ignored). T7 launches `SAWIT_HOME=$S/home SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS=1 RUST_LOG=sawit=debug target/debug/sawit --foreground "$S/proj" 2>"$S/sawit.log" &`, records PID=$!, and reads the `canvas render ... ms` lines from $S/sawit.log. — why: The spec's per-frame 'debug log of render time' and T7 Step 4 need an installed logger and captured stderr. Gating on RUST_LOG leaves normal runs unchanged. — costs if wrong: One small new dependency (env_logger and env_filter; not in Cargo.lock yet, so one crates.io fetch). A ~20-line hand-written stderr logger in main.rs could replace it. Plan 8's release-size comparison includes it.
- Ruling: P1-C5 — Task 4 starts crates/app/src/board/mod.rs with `#![allow(dead_code)]` and a comment saying Task 6 removes it. Task 5 keeps it. Task 6 deletes it and uses or removes anything still unused (e.g. Frame.millis), so T6's commit passes clippy without the allow. — why: Keeps the plan's task split and the per-commit gates. mod.rs is already in all three tasks' file lists, so no new file is touched. — costs if wrong: If T6 misses it, the allow outlives T6. T6's review must check that it is gone.
- Ruling: P1-C6 — Send the bytes `sawit-control/1\n` + {"cmd":"open_page","page":"canvas","project":"<$S/proj>"} to $S/home/sawit.sock. The scratch root is short, so the socket has no /tmp fallback and no socket search is needed. Then send {"cmd":"canvas","op":{"op":"get"}}. — why: Command is serde tag "cmd" with snake_case variants (open_page). "page" is rejected. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P1-C7 — Stop the instance with /bin/kill $PID from the --foreground launch in C4. As a fallback, use pgrep -f "sawit --foreground $S/proj". — why: This stops exactly this instance. The memory recipe forbids broad matches that could kill the user's app. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P1-C8 — Accept the skip. T6's RED step is its four ops tests plus a refusal-list test. T7 Step 2 adds the check: after open_page, `state` shows an active tab of kind "canvas", and `get` answers ok:true with elements of type "other" whose ids are numeric strings. Only the native path produces that. — why: The plan already allows moving the check to T7. This makes the check concrete. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P1-C9 — T6 sets canvas_open to true when any `canvases` or `boards` entry is alive. — why: app_state should describe what is open. It is one line in a file T6 already edits. — costs if wrong: One line. Otherwise Plan 8 corrects it when `canvases` is removed.
- Ruling: R-EHFRAME — the `ld: __eh_frame section too large (max 16MB)` linker warning that appears in `cargo test -p sawit` once FigCraft links into the app (found by P1.T4; app binary will follow at P1.T6) is fixed ONCE, by P1.T5: add `[profile.dev.package.<crate>] opt-level = 3` entries to the root Cargo.toml for the FigCraft render-path crates and tiny-skia (use `cargo tree -p sawit -e normal` to name them: figcraft-render/-engine/-text/-geom/-document as present, tiny-skia, tiny-skia-path, and the font crates FigCraft rasterises with), with a comment in the style of the existing syntect block ("hot CPU path: debug renders"), until `cargo test -p sawit` and `cargo build -p sawit` link with no __eh_frame warning; record the test binary's __eh_frame size before/after (`otool -l <bin> | grep -A3 __eh_frame`) in the report. Until it lands, other tasks' reviews treat that one warning as known (R-EHFRAME), not as their own finding — it follows the repo's existing pattern for hot debug paths, also makes Plan 1 Task 7's debug render timings meaningful, and is one config change instead of a warning every app task trips over — costs: one-time rebuild of those crates at opt-level 3 in every lane and slower clean debug builds; if opt-level 3 doesn't get the binaries under 16 MB, P1.T5 reports DONE_WITH_CONCERNS with the measured sizes rather than silencing the lint.
- Ruling: (delegate, P1.T4) ⚠️ T5's bounds fill takes raw page_rgb (task-5-brief.md:36) while this worker paints a non-sRGB page in sRGB (worker.rs:196-201) → fix: T4 moves the page-colour conversion (worker.rs:199-200) into one helper beside view::page_rgb (crates/board/src/view.rs:137-147), screen_rgb(profile, rgb) -> [u8; 3] (rgb unchanged for sRGB, else conversion_to(Srgb).apply as FigCraft's doc_c32), which screen_bgra calls, and rewords the docs of page_rgb (view.rs:137 calls it 'the colour to fill the canvas with') and Job::page_rgb (worker.rs:25-27) to say the bytes are in the document's profile; this amends T5: fill the bounds with screen_rgb(doc.color_profile, page_rgb) and give dots() that colour too, while Job.page_rgb and Options.background stay in the document's profile (the worker converts) — FigCraft fills behind its texture with doc_c32 of the page background and gives its dot grid the same colour (ui-egui canvas.rs:225-235, :269; theme.rs:455-458); Display P3 boards are reachable (file.colorProfile converts every colour, engine cmd/file/save.rs:134-142 and doc colors.rs:117-126, and T6's command op doesn't refuse it; third-party .figcraft files in Plan 7), and a coloured P3 page would show a seam wherever the frame doesn't cover the canvas (first paint, pans past the overscan, zoom-out); T5's implementer reads its brief, rulings.md and the code but not this ledger, so the helper and its docs carry the amendment (controller: copy the T5 half into rulings.md before P1.T5 starts) — costs one fix round on Plan 1's critical path and about 20 lines; if P3 boards never matter, a harmless helper.
- Ruling: (delegate, P1.T4) ⚠️ spec Risks 'The worker never holds more than two frames' (spec:300-301) vs the unbounded frame channel between the render thread and the UI → dismiss: T4's side holds and the UI side is already in T5's brief — the thread holds one Frame at most (worker.rs:141-150) and the slot one Job, and each frame answers one request made on the UI thread that also drains the channel; frames waiting in the channel are CPU-side images outside GPUI's sprite atlas (only paint_image inserts, gpui-pre window.rs:5071; drop_image of a never-painted image is a no-op, platform.rs:1853-1857); the UnboundedReceiver is the plan's Task 4 interface and matches FigCraft's own unbounded done channel that poll() drains whole (ui-egui render_worker.rs:335-341); task-5-brief.md:38-40 already has the cx.spawn loop take each frame, replace the current one and drop_image the replaced one at the next paint, which T5's review checks against its brief and Plan 1's final review re-checks through this ruling — costs if wrong: frames (about 23 MB each at 2624x2224) wait in RAM while T5's loop lags; the atlas stays bounded by T5's drop_image.
- Ruling: (delegate, P1.T5) ⚠️ Spec D5 (autosave 300 ms after the document changes) holds only while the tab paints: autosave starts from prepaint (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:363) and is private (:214) → dismiss: not a T5 gap; carried to P1.T6 as a requirement: P1.T6's run_op calls self.autosave(cx) after every op, successful or not (it does nothing while the document Arc is the saved or pending one), beside its cx.notify(); P1.T6 adds a board::tests test in which a command op (board.sticky) sent through run_op to a BoardView opened without a window (the tests' open helper) is in the file after advance_clock(SAVE_DELAY), with no paint in between; any trigger that needs no paint and passes that test is fine (e.g. a cx.observe_self(|this, cx| this.autosave(cx)) subscription kept in a field); P1.T6's review checks the call and the test — T5 places autosave where its brief puts it (after any frame whose doc is a new Arc), and at ab94fca nothing in production edits a board (BoardView is built only in tests; the only production entry is board::init, workspace.rs:146), so every change T5 can make is followed by a paint; T6's run_op is the first way an edit arrives without input in the window: Command::Canvas brings the canvas tab forward (control.rs:319-321, workspace.rs:1965), but gpui-pre-macos 0.3.10 stops the display link while the window is covered, minimised, hidden, on another Space or the display sleeps (window.rs:2989-3003; :817-824 won't restart it then), so an agent's edit to a covered Sawit window gets no prepaint until the window shows again; Plans 2 and 7 add their ops to ops::run behind run_op, so one call there covers every control-socket edit, and the test stops later tasks from dropping it — if T6 misses it, edits made over the control socket while the window is covered stay unsaved until it draws again, the tab closes or the app quits, and a crash in between loses them; adding it later is one line and one test.
- Ruling: (delegate, P1.T5) ⚠️ R-EHFRAME will trip at T6: with BoardView wired into main the app has 21.0 MiB of __eh_frame (lane-1 target/debug/deps/sawit-92f3148f08c6e4ca), still 17.3 MiB with 21 more FigCraft-only crates at opt-level 3 (deps/sawit-d72dc156938f66e7) → dismiss: option (a); T5's 10 opt-level-3 entries stay as committed (they keep the sawit test binary at 14.26 MiB and the debug render path fast for P1.T7's timings) and none are added; from P1.T6 on, the linker warning "__eh_frame section too large (max 16MB) to encode dwarf unwind offsets in compact unwind table, performance of exception handling might be affected" in any debug link (the app or a test binary) is known under R-EHFRAME in every later task (P1.T6, P1.T7, P2.T2, P3.T3, P4.T2, P5.T4, P6.T2, P7.T5, P8.T1 and every other task of Plans 2-8): it is no task's finding, and no task changes profiles, linker flags or lint levels for it (any other linker message is still a finding); P8.T4, after P8.T1 has removed wry, records the debug app's __eh_frame (otool -l target/debug/sawit | grep -A3 __eh_frame) beside its release numbers, so the user can choose option (b) then, measured on a quiet machine; controller: extend the program ledger's R-EHFRAME line to match — the linker's own text (ld-1267) limits the effect to exception-handling speed: unwinding stays correct and only panics and backtraces in debug builds slow down; no gate fails on it (clippy -D warnings checks without linking; rustc 1.99.1's linker-messages lint is warn-level, and no linking build in the repo uses -D warnings: release.yml runs scripts/bundle-macos.sh, a plain cargo build --release -p sawit); shipped builds are far below the limit (target/release/sawit, the Task 0 fat-LTO baseline, has 2.23 MiB); (c) still leaves 17.3 MiB and adds about 2 min to every lane's clean build; (b) is unmeasured and rebuilds every dependency in 5 lanes and main while each lane's target/debug/deps already holds 8.4-15 GB and the disk has 25 GiB free, so it could fill the disk mid-run, and it slows every clean debug build of the user's app — one known, speed-only warning line in debug builds until P8.T4; if the controller wants it gone sooner, (b) is one Cargo.toml block plus a full dependency rebuild per target dir, best done while the lanes are idle.
- Ruling: (delegate, P1.T5) ⚠️ The app-link half of R-EHFRAME's stop condition at ab94fca (report: app 0x00e24348, no warning) can't be confirmed from disk: lane-1's app binaries hold the experiment builds, and the integrator's gates don't run cargo build -p sawit → dismiss: no extra build for the integrator or anyone else — at ab94fca nothing outside crates/app/src/board uses FigCraft or sawit_board, and inside it only board::init is reachable from production (workspace.rs:146; BoardView is built only in tests), so the app gains only figcraft-text's font scan and the font crates under it, all at opt-level 3, over the report's 14.19 MiB BASE app; main has gained only crates/board files since 4f31938, so the rebased app reaches the same code; the report's 14.14 MiB fits, and its numbers match all four binaries still on disk (0x00e43ce8, 0x011536dc, 0x0150497c, 0x01153730); under this task's option-(a) ruling the same line is known from P1.T6, the next task on lane 1, so a check now would decide nothing — if ab94fca's app link does warn, it is the same known, speed-only line until T6 lands; nothing ships from a debug build.
- Ruling: (delegate, P1.T6) ⚠️ The workspace wiring has no automated test (open_canvas's native branch workspace.rs:2005-2013, the existing-tab downcast :1996-2003, Command::Canvas's native arm control.rs:320-331, canvas_open control.rs:101-102, boards.retain workspace.rs:2068) → dismiss: not a T6 gap; - Ruling: (delegate, P1.T6) PLAN-CONFLICT crates/board/src/ops.rs:17-30 and :92 (with crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:221-235): the command op's twelve-id refusal list doesn't stop a document switch — command.batch runs any id as a step, and file.recover, branch.create, branch.open, version.duplicate and doc.switch aren't listed — while BoardView autosaves the active document over canvas.figcraft, with a .bak only when it is empty → fix (this widens the controller's "Carried to Task 6" ruling; its twelve ids and their reply stay): (1) the twelve ids are refused as command.batch steps too, nested batches included, with the same "<step id> is handled by Sawit", before anything runs; (2) an effect guard keyed on DocState.uid: command.batch runs inside Session::grouped under its own undo label (figcraft_engine::params::string(&params, "label"), else "Batch") and fails the group when the uids or the active uid changed, so the engine's restore (session/undo.rs:331-351) puts back the documents, the active one and the canvas document; every other id runs with plain execute and, if the uids or the active uid changed, the new documents are dropped and the canvas's uid is made active again; the reply is {ok:false, error:"<id> would open or switch documents; the canvas keeps its one document"}; (3) RED-first tests, listed in the fixer's finding; note for P7.T2: P7-C11's single predicate may also leave file.recover, version.duplicate and branch.open out of `commands`, since the guard refuses them whenever they succeed — why: the spec binds one document per canvas tab (Non-goals: "several documents in one tab") and D5 saves that document with a .bak only before an emptying save; BoardDoc::doc() is the active document (crates/board/src/doc.rs:115) and queue_save writes it 300 ms after the op (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:221), so one op could silently replace the user's canvas (an agent's batch [file.newBoard, board.sticky], or file.recover of any .figcraft); the ruling's stated purpose, no switching, opening, closing or writing documents behind the store, is the requirement and the id list only its means; checked in FigCraft 0971d09: file.recover, version.duplicate and branch.create push and activate a tab (cmd/file.rs:182-190, :295-309, sharing/branches.rs:314), doc.switch sets active (cmd/file.rs:311-317), command.batch runs its steps through execute and keeps their effects when it succeeds (cmd/edit.rs:463-487, session/undo.rs:193-201), and a failed group restoring docs, closed tabs and active is the engine's own tested behaviour (batch_rollback_tests.rs:38-60); FigCraft guards its plugin calls the same way, grouped plus a post-execute check (extensions/plugins.rs:222-236); a labelled group around every command would be wrong because edit.undo and edit.redo are history:false and the group would re-record them as new undo steps and clear redo (session/undo.rs:270-288, doc_state.rs:243-249), and the reviewer's count-and-active check misses a close followed by an open (same count and index), which (1) blocks and the uid compare would catch; outside a batch nothing removes or replaces the canvas tab (remove_tabs only for file.close, cmd/file.rs:415, and branch tabs, sharing/branches.rs:197; file.revert, cmd/file/open.rs:121, needs a path that only file.save and file.saveAs set, cmd/file/save.rs:188, both refused even as steps by (1)), Sawit never polls live or background sessions so nothing switches a document after the op returns, and menus and keys can't reach these ids (the catalog has only branch.create and collab.join, both hidden, and file.revert, refused without a path); fixing it in T6 keeps the hole out of Plan 7's canvas_command, which hands this op to every agent — costs if wrong: one fix round on lane 1 before P1.T7 (about 60 lines and 5 tests in ops.rs); if the guard ever trips on a legitimate command, that op answers ok:false and changes nothing, and the fix is in the guard.

the picture is derived: FigCraft re-renders it on its next save from the document Sawit keeps whole, a frame marked Set as thumbnail included (the mark is plugin_data figcraft.thumbnail inside document.json; engine cmd/file/save.rs:7-37), and reads a missing one as optional (format thumbnail.rs:1-4; archive_preview :149 then gives the page colour and no picture), so nothing the user made is lost, which is the line P7-C1 draws (keep history and branches, refuse lost images); writing it puts a whole-page render in every save, which FigCraft runs on a thread of its own (ui-egui app/files.rs:226-262) while Sawit encodes in queue_save on the UI thread (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:224-238 on main), so doing it right means moving encoding onto store.rs's writer thread, a change to the newest-save-wins core for a picture only FigCraft's home screen shows — costs if wrong: FigCraft's recent files show a Sawit-saved file as its page colour with no picture until FigCraft saves it again; the follow-up is figcraft_engine::cmd::file::thumbnail(&doc) (public: engine lib.rs:17, cmd.rs:9, cmd/file.rs:42) passed to save_bytes_with_history and encoded on the writer thread, about 30-50 lines and one test in doc.rs and store.rs.

## Plan 2 (editing)

- Ruling: P2-C1 — In route_key's text-editing branch, Space without cmd or ctrl returns Unhandled, like a single printable character, so the IME path inserts it. Add route_key('Space', none) -> Unhandled to typing_is_left_to_the_ime_while_editing. — why: Keeps the plan's rule that typing goes through the IME path, and FigCraft's behaviour (egui inserts the Text event). The space bar is the one text key with a multi-letter name. — costs if wrong: None found: KEYS has no plain-Space binding (Shift+Space and Alt+Space are Canvas-only).
- Ruling: P2-C2 — Resolve in [Root, Workspace, Board] and [Root, Workspace, Board BoardText], as terminal/view.rs's tests do. Assert both that no action other than NoAction matches and !pending for every key, as no_binding_takes_a_shell_key_from_the_terminal does. Load bindings in app order (gpui_kit::init, workspace::init, board::init), and keep board's bind_keys after the workspace's in workspace::init. — why: The spec requires Root's Tab, ⇧Tab and ⌘C to reach the canvas, and Step 3 counts on this test to find them. Asserting !pending keeps ⌘K (app.actions) immediate. — costs if wrong: Test-only. Without it: Tab, ⇧Tab and ⌘C stay dead on the canvas, and ⌘K waits for the chord timeout.
- Ruling: P2-C3 — Keep resize_dir and its tests in radians. cursor_style converts: Cursor::Resize(a) -> resize_dir(a.to_radians()), and a non-finite angle gives Arrow. Add a cursor_style test: Resize(0.0) -> ResizeLeftRight, Resize(90.0) -> ResizeUpDown, Resize(45.0) -> ResizeUpLeftDownRight, Resize(135.0) -> ResizeUpRightDownLeft. — why: One call site. T2's tests stay as written, and the buckets are the same as ui-egui's cursors::resize_icon. — costs if wrong: Wrong resize cursors on every handle, which only shows by eye.
- Ruling: P2-C4 — The test sends Move (50,40), Move (100,80), then Up (100,80), as FigCraft's own drag helpers do (canvas_fixes_tests.rs), and keeps the 100×80 and one-undo-step assertions. drag_released_outside_ends_gesture keeps its Up at (5000,5000) and asserts 1 layer and !busy(). — why: Test data only: the engine and the GPUI path, where moves arrive up to the release point, are right. — costs if wrong: If Up's own position was meant to count, input::pointer would need a synthetic Move first. The spec asks for nothing like that.
- Ruling: P2-C5 — handle_requests calls figcraft_engine::board::edit_text(&mut session, id) for board.editText. route_key runs board.nextSticky with {"edit": true}, as ui-egui's menus::invoke does. Add next_sticky_keeps_typing to T2: while editing a sticky, route_key('Enter', shift+cmd), then handle_requests -> editing the new sticky's text. — why: Matches FigCraft's UI and the spec's ⇧⌘↩ row (canvas while editing text): typing goes on in the new sticky. It also gives handle_requests a real test. — costs if wrong: ⇧⌘↩ ends typing instead, and the editText branch is dead code that errors when reached.
- Ruling: P2-C6 — Tests start editing with figcraft_engine::board::edit_text(&mut session, sticky) or textedit::begin(board::text_of(doc, sticky), None, false). This is wording only, under recorded ruling R3 (real API, same assertions). — why: The intent is editing the sticky's text. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P2-C7 — policy.rs gets CLIPBOARD = [edit.copy, edit.cut, edit.paste, edit.pasteOver, edit.pasteReplace]. Outside text editing, route_key returns Ui(id) for them, and on_key handles those ids itself. Copy and cut: execute, after_copy, write the marker. Paste: read the system clipboard, before_paste, paste_source, paste. Add clipboard::marker() -> Option<String>. Other Ui ids keep the 'Coming soon' toast. — why: No signature change to route_key or KeyOutcome, which T4, the key op and Plan 3 and 6 tests use, and the platform clipboard stays in the app. — costs if wrong: A new KeyOutcome variant would also work, at the cost of changing T4's match and the key op. With this ruling, the key op answers handled:true for ⌘C without copying; agents use the command op.
- Ruling: P2-C8 — In T6, while ds.text_edit is set, copy and cut put textedit::selected_text on the system clipboard (cut then calls textedit::insert('')), and paste inserts the system text with textedit::insert, all through guard::run. Add a test: while editing a sticky with 'hello' selected, copy -> the system text is 'hello'; paste 'x' -> the sticky reads 'x'. — why: T6 owns clipboard routing, and this is what FigCraft and every text field do. — costs if wrong: Users can't paste text into stickies, and ⌘V adds layers while they type.
- Ruling: P2-C9 — Keep the engine test and add an app test through the EntityInputHandler: mark 'ni', then mark '' -> no text and no preedit; mark 'ni', then a pointer Down outside the text (the engine ends the edit, tools/click.rs), then unmark_text -> no text. unmark_text commits only while the same edit (ImeEditOwner) still has a preedit. — why: Review Focus 3 is about the IME path, which the plan's test doesn't reach. — costs if wrong: Preedit text could leak into the document on cancel or click-away, unseen until the manual IME check.
- Ruling: P2-C10 — The pointer and key ops push what handle_requests leaves back onto session.ui_requests. BoardView drains through one helper after every pointer event, key and run_op. — why: The spec says the ops take the same path as GPUI events. One drain point keeps Plans 4-6's panel requests working from agents. — costs if wrong: Plan 6 Task 4's live check fails, and agent double-clicks lose canvas.renameLabel.
- Ruling: P2-C11 — Load bindings as the terminal tests do (gpui_kit::init, crate::workspace::init, crate::board::init). Bind cli's three global keys (cmd-q, cmd-shift-n, cmd-o) in the test helper. Assert that each SAWIT_WINS key resolves to the same non-empty action in [Root, Workspace, Board] as in [Root, Workspace]. workspace.rs isn't edited for the test, only for C2's ordering if needed. — why: Test-only, and it follows the existing pattern. — costs if wrong: A compile error, or a check for ⌘O that can't fail.
- Ruling: P2-C12 — T6 adds base64 = "0.22" to crates/board/Cargo.toml (0.22.1 is already in Cargo.lock through FigCraft) and stages crates/board/Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock and crates/board/src/lib.rs. — why: It doesn't compile otherwise. — costs if wrong: None. Cargo.lock edits serialize with other lanes that touch it.
- Ruling: P2-C13 — Port FigCraft's crates/mcp/src/headless.rs Headless::key at the pinned rev. Run commands with BoardDoc::execute: no clearing, no live_poll or live_sync. Not found, a hidden id and an unknown key name return Ok(Unhandled). Wrap tool_key, escape, pen_end and textedit::key in guard::run. — why: These are the plan's own three changes and Global Constraints, made explicit. — costs if wrong: A failing test or a review round.
- Ruling: P2-C14 — Add crates/board/src/lib.rs and crates/app/src/board/mod.rs to Task 3's files. No code change. — why: File overlap is computed from the lists. — costs if wrong: Two lanes edit board/mod.rs at once without the scheduler knowing, and a rebase conflicts.
- Ruling: P2-C15 — Outline the selection box only for a multi-selection. One selected layer gets its own outline, the handles and the size label. Also port ui-egui's handle rules: connectors get round ends, and locked layers and boxes under SMALL_BOX_PX get no handles. — why: This matches the port source and the test. — costs if wrong: A failing test, or doubled outlines.
- Ruling: P2-C16 — paste keeps its Err for the test. After before_paste, the app does nothing for a Canvas paste when session.clipboard.nodes is empty, and doesn't call paste. — why: Review Focus 5: a paste with nothing to paste must not show a toast. — costs if wrong: A toast on ⌘V with nothing copied.
- Ruling: P2-C17 — Rule 3 uses figcraft_engine::looks_like_svg(text), and svg_text_imports also pastes an SVG that starts with an <?xml ...?> prolog. — why: Same intent, for SVG as other apps copy it. — costs if wrong: SVG copied from other apps pastes as a text layer.
- Ruling: P2-C18 — While editing a new sticky, {op:'type', text:'hi'} -> ok:true, and the sticky reads 'hi'. Without an edit -> ok:false, error 'not editing text'. — why: It pins the spec's type contract. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P2-C19 — As recorded ruling R5: T7 writes the answers into the Plan 2 file and commits them as a docs: commit. Step 3 is read as 'place a shape'; nothing else changes. — why: Keeps the evidence, following R5's precedent. — costs if wrong: The manual-check answers get lost.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T1) ⚠️ C5 spans tasks: route_key leaves board.editText in ui_requests for T2’s handle_requests → dismiss: no T1 gap; T1’s half is done (board.nextSticky runs with {"edit": true} and leaves board.editText for the view, pinned by next_sticky_asks_to_edit_the_new_sticky), and handle_requests calling board::edit_text plus the test next_sticky_keeps_typing are P2.T2’s, to check at T2’s review — why: rulings.md P2-C5 gives that half to T2 and dag.json lists C5 in P2.T2’s notes; no keyboard reaches route_key before T2 lands, because on_key comes with P2.T4, which depends on P2.T2 — costs if wrong: ⇧⌘↩ would make the next sticky without typing in it, and board.editText would reach the view unhandled.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T1) ⚠️ C7 names policy.rs and route_key, but P2.T6 owns it → dismiss: T1 stays without CLIPBOARD; P2.T6 adds CLIPBOARD to policy.rs, makes route_key return Ui(id) for those ids outside text editing (C7) and copies, cuts and pastes text while editing (C8), to check at T6’s review — why: dag.json lists policy.rs and input.rs in P2.T6’s files with notes "C7, C8", and C7 applied now would turn ⌘C/⌘X/⌘V into T4’s "Coming soon" toast until T6; meanwhile they run the engine’s tab-local layer copy, cut and paste outside text editing, and return Unhandled while editing under the text-editing ruling below; nothing ships before cutover (D6) — costs if wrong: if T6 misses it, ⌘C/⌘X/⌘V never reach the system clipboard or another tab, and paste into a sticky does nothing.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T1) PLAN-CONFLICT input.rs:125-142: while text is edited, ⌘ keys that aren’t text shortcuts fall through to canvas commands → fix: the text branch runs only what FigCraft’s UI runs while editing (ui-egui shortcuts.rs:808-862): Text and Both shortcuts, the typing rule and named text keys as now; ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z end the edit (textedit::end through guard::run), then run edit.undo or edit.redo; ⌘A runs textedit::key("SelectAll"); ⌘↩ that is no text shortcut ends the edit; any other single character with ⌘ returns Unhandled without reaching tool_key or the Canvas lookup (the clipboard ids stay P2.T6’s, C7/C8) — why: D7 makes FigCraft’s keymap the source of truth, and its Canvas context means "not while editing text" (keymap.rs:81-82); FigCraft’s own test text_keys_while_editing has ⌘A select the text (ui-egui shortcuts_tests.rs:134-152); Headless::key is FigCraft’s agent path, not its keyboard path; verbatim, ⌘A selects sibling layers with the edit open, ⌘↩ types a newline (textedit.rs:418), ⌘D, ⌘G and ⌘E act on layers mid-typing, and ⌘Z drops the open edit unrecorded and undoes the step before it (session/undo.rs:114-131), where ending first makes the edit its own undo step (textedit.rs:109); Review Focus 2 asks for the right keys while editing too — costs if wrong: about 20 lines and 4 tests to revert; while typing, canvas-only ⌘ keys (zoom, ⌘\, ⌘/) do nothing, as in FigCraft’s UI, so there the key op differs from FigCraft’s MCP key tool.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T1) PLAN-CONFLICT input.rs:179-186: a digit with nothing selected runs object.opacityKeys, which refuses → fix: add !board.session.selection().is_empty() to the digit arm’s guard so the key falls through to Ok(Unhandled); tests: on an empty board route_key("5", none) is Ok(Unhandled), and with a sticky selected it is Ok(Handled) with opacity 0.5; FigCraft’s two-digit chain (4 then 5: 45 %) stays unported, as in Headless::key — why: FigCraft’s UI leaves the key alone with nothing selected (ui-egui shortcuts.rs:611-613); number_keys_opacity defaults on (prefs.rs:120), so verbatim every digit on an empty canvas would toast "nothing is selected (pass ids)" (automation/params.rs:158-160); C13 already returns Ok(Unhandled) for a key the canvas has no use for — costs if wrong: none found.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T1) PLAN-CONFLICT input.rs:61-69: GPUI reports a shifted digit as its shifted character with Shift cleared, so ⇧0, ⇧2, ⇧⌘7 and ⇧⌘8 come back Unhandled → fix: a US digit-row table at the top of route_key turns ) @ # $ % ^ & * ( into 0 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 9 with Shift, before the text branch, tool_key and the lookups (FigCraft’s normalize already maps ! + ? < > { } | : " _); key_name, gpui_keystroke and their brief values stay; tests: at zoom 2, route_key(")") is Handled and zoom becomes 1; with a selection "@" is Handled; while editing text "*" and "&" with ⌘ are Handled and ")" is Unhandled (the IME types it); carry to P2.T7: ⇧2 and ⇧⌘8 on a real keyboard join Plan 2’s hand checks — why: D7 and the conflict policy need every non-Sawit key to reach the canvas, which a hand check alone can’t give; GPUI 0.3.10 clears Shift on these (gpui-pre-macos events.rs:469-479), its Keystroke carries no physical key (gpui-pre keystroke.rs:18-33), and FigCraft’s normalize has no entry for them (keymap.rs:399-436); gpui_keystroke’s shift-<symbol> form never matches a real event (gpui-pre keystroke.rs:82-112), but T4 needs no NoAction for it: no Root, Workspace or global binding uses a shifted symbol (vendor/gpui-base root.rs:44-49, workspace.rs:106-140, cli.rs:269-271), so the real events reach on_key_down — costs if wrong: on layouts where these symbols are unshifted (French AZERTY), ) zooms to 100 %, @ zooms to the selection and ⌘& or ⌘* toggles a list, as FigCraft’s normalize already does for !.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T3) ⚠️ D15 scope for per-frame engine reads: overlay() runs in every prepaint without guard::run (mod.rs:364), so selection_box, selection_frame, locked_in, box_sides_px, selected_connector_ends, components::*, vectoredit::overlay and figcraft_render::node_outline run unguarded on the UI thread → fix: prepaint builds the overlay as let shapes = figcraft_engine::guard::run("ui.overlay", || Ok(sawit_board::overlay::overlay(&board.session))).unwrap_or_default(); so a panic costs that frame's overlay and the names' hit areas in tool.labels, and guard::run logs it; prepaint shows no toast and calls no notify for it (a toast there needs a repaint, which would panic again on every frame); no new test (nothing makes overlay() panic on purpose); set_ui_dark and apply_view_prefs (mod.rs:310-311, from Plan 1) stay as they are, since they set fields and walk no geometry (engine session/new.rs:44-85). CARRY, the same rule for every per-frame read: P2.T5 keeps the caret, text selection and preedit underline inside overlay(), so this guard covers their layout_node and textedit reads, and runs textedit::preedit_doc for the render job in prepaint through guard::run, falling back to None (the document without the composition); P3.T3 (toolbar::active, the context bar), P4.T2 (layer rows), P5.T4 and P5.T5 (design-panel models) and P6.T2 (menu checked and enabled states) call each sawit-board model that render or prepaint builds through one guard::run("ui.<model>", ...) at the app call site, falling back to the empty or hidden model, with no toast — why: D15 and the plan's Global Constraints send every engine call other than execute through guard::run, for FigCraft's never-crash rule, and FigCraft applies that rule to each UI frame (ui-egui windows.rs:126-140, called at :618 and :712: catch_unwind, a toast, an emergency save); Sawit already guards the render worker's read of the same document and only logs there (worker.rs:178-186); overlay() walks every frame-like layer on the page and each selected layer through engine and render geometry on every frame, and GPUI draws inside gpui-pre-macos's extern "C" display_layer and step callbacks (window.rs:3324, :3345) with no catch_unwind, where a panic can't unwind, so it aborts Sawit with every tab, and a panic caused by the saved document comes back each time that canvas is shown — costs if wrong: one line per call site and a catch_unwind that costs nothing until something panics; while a read keeps panicking, the canvas paints without its overlay instead of quitting, and the log gets one error line per repaint.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T3) ⚠️ Plan 4's row hover highlight draws through this hover outline, which shows only with the Move or Scale tool, prefs.highlight_on_hover on and nothing measured (overlay.rs:300-310) → dismiss: no P2.T3 gap; the gate stays as ported and a row hover doesn't bypass it. CARRY to P4.T2: a row hover sets ds.hover and notifies (plan 4), and needs nothing more from the overlay. CARRY to P4.T3: its 'hover highlight' hand check reads 'with the Move tool (V), hover the row of an unselected layer: the canvas outlines that layer 1.5 px in blue (purple in a component); a selected layer gets no extra outline' — why: FigCraft does the same through the same mechanism: its layers panel sets ds.hover on a row hover (ui-egui panels/layers.rs:1231-1235) and its overlay applies this gate (overlays.rs:47-55); the gate never hides a canvas hover, because the engine works out ds.hover only for the Move and Scale tools and sets it to None for every other tool (engine canvas/tools/pointer.rs:209-264); the spec's Layers panel line names ds.hover as the mechanism, Move is the default tool, and highlight_on_hover defaults on (engine prefs.rs:119) — costs if wrong: with another tool active, a row hover shows no outline on the canvas; lifting the gate for a row hover is a small P4 change (overlay() would need to know that the hover came from a row).
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T3) ⚠️ nothing in the overlay has been looked at: the app test checks only tool.labels, and P2.T7 Step 3 screenshots one selected shape → dismiss: no P2.T3 gap; nothing enters the fix loop. CARRY to P2.T7, over the socket after Step 3's shape: (a) node.create a frame and a section where the window shows them, then select.none, and screenshot: the frame's grey name sits just above its top-left corner and the section's name on a grey pill inside its top-left corner; (b) view.zoom100 and a screenshot, then pointer down and up at (frame x + 4, frame y - 11), the middle of the name's line at 100 %: the next screenshot shows the frame selected (outline, 4 handles, size label) with its name in blue; (c) tool.move, then pointer down on empty canvas and a move 120 by 80 away with no up: the screenshot shows the marquee (pale blue fill, blue outline); then pointer up. Plan 2's hand check 'drag a shape' adds: dragged level with another shape, red-orange snap guides show, with a distance pill where a gap is measured — why: P2.T3 met its brief (no live step, and the spec's app tests take no pixels), and its lane can't show the overlay: the workspace opens the native canvas only from Plan 1's Task 6 (lane-1 d09a76a, not on main) and the pointer op comes with P2.T2; 13 sawit-board tests pin the overlay's geometry, and painted_frame_names_go_back_to_the_engine runs the paint path through real text shaping; R-LIVE puts what the socket can drive into the live check and only steps that need real hands into the hand checks, so pressing a name goes to (b), not to the hand checks — costs if wrong: a defect that only the paint shows (a name, pill or readout misplaced, a dash or the marquee fill wrong) stays unseen until P2.T7 or the user's hand checks.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T3) PLAN-CONFLICT overlay.rs:324-328 and :401: the selection colour is all-or-nothing, so when any selected layer is in a component, every selected outline, the box, the handle strokes and the size pill are COMPONENT, where the brief says COMPONENT for components and instances and a SELECTION size pill → dismiss: ratify Decision 3 as built: the selection takes COMPONENT when any selected layer is a component set or is in a component or an instance (outline_color, overlay.rs:768-782), else SELECTION, for its outlines, its box, its handle strokes and its size pill; the hover outline keeps its own layer's colour, and a slot drop target stays COMPONENT; P2.T5 and later edits of overlay.rs keep this rule — why: the plan makes overlay() a port of ui-egui's overlays.rs, which does exactly this (selection_overlay's one colour, overlays.rs:246, and transform_box paints the size pill in it, :417); D4 fixes only the three colour values; a selection with no component layer gets the brief's colours exactly, and the tests pin SELECTION there; a per-layer rule leaves a mixed selection's box and handles without a colour, and a blue pill under purple outlines clashes — costs if wrong: in a mixed selection the ordinary layers' outlines, the handles and the size pill show purple instead of blue; per-layer colours are two small edits (a colour per id in the outline loop, SELECTION for the pill), and nothing else depends on the rule.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T3) PLAN-CONFLICT overlay.rs:389-393: one selected Line's size label reads its length only ('100'), where the brief's item 5 says '{w} × {h}' ('100 × 0') → dismiss: ratify Decision 4 as built: one selected NodeKind::Line (the Line and Arrow tools make them) reads its length in whole px, a connector shows no size label, and every other selection reads '{w} × {h}'; its source is ui-egui, not C15, which rules on handles only — why: the plan makes overlay() a port of ui-egui's overlays.rs, which labels a line this way (overlays.rs:263); a line's height is 0, because the Line tool and node.create set only its width, the length (engine canvas/tools/draw.rs:77, cmd/create.rs:282), and selection_frame gives one layer's own width and height (canvas/tools/handles.rs:99-104), so '× 0' would add a constant that tells nothing; the brief's own size test is a 100 × 80 rectangle, which reads as the brief asks — costs if wrong: one branch to drop (overlay.rs:389-393); no test depends on it.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T2) ⚠️ Integration: the rebase onto main conflicts in crates/app/src/board/mod.rs (`mod events;` beside `mod overlay_paint;`, and the paint closure) → dismiss: no P2.T2 gap, nothing enters the fix loop; the integrator rebases under R-LANES. Main has moved since the report, to ba95652 (Plan 7's Task 2), so the rebase now stops in two files. mod.rs has the report's 2 hunks: keep both mod lines, and make the paint closure `move |bounds, painting, window, cx| { events::track_drags(&drags, window); if let Some(painting) = painting { paint::paint(bounds, painting, window, cx); } }`, so the drag listeners register on frames without a painting too (lane mod.rs:539). crates/board/src/ops.rs has 4 hunks, all keep-both: the module doc lists pointer beside get, add, update, delete, clear, replace, command and commands; the imports are the union of both lists; run's doc keeps both bullets; run's match keeps both arms. There is also one clash git doesn't show: both sides add a test helper `fn design() -> BoardDoc` to ops.rs's tests (main :1201, lane :909), a duplicate definition (E0428). The reconcile commit keeps main's and drops the lane's: both give an empty "Design" document in Session::with_document, and main's goes through save and load. Then the gates run: fmt, clippy, and cargo test -p sawit-board and -p sawit — why: rebasing is the integrator's step (R-LANES), and its prompt covers both kinds: keep both sides for mod lines, match arms and appended tests (dag.md "Shared files that merge mechanically", ops.rs and mod.rs rows), and fix "a name both sides added" in one minimal reconcile commit. Checked with git merge-tree --write-tree main lane-1 at ba95652: only these two files conflict, the merged mod.rs has no duplicate item, and the lane's pointer op needs only failure, input::page_pointer_event, input::pointer and leave_for_the_view, all present after the merge — costs if wrong: an integration attempt that stops on a conflict and goes to the Opus integrator, or a gate failure the integrator fixes; nothing reaches main unchecked.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T2) ⚠️ Hand checks (R-LIVE): Plan 2 Task 7 Step 4 (plan :423-429) lacks the three checks the report carries → dismiss: no P2.T2 gap (its brief has no live step, and a plan file is edited only by its plan's live check, dag.md :129). CARRY to P2.T7: write these into the Plan 2 file under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)", beside Step 4's items. (1) Cursors over the canvas: R → a crosshair. T → a crosshair over empty canvas and an I-beam over a text layer (make one with T, a click, a word, Escape). V with a rectangle selected → ↔ on its left and right edges, ↕ on its top and bottom edges, the ↖↘ diagonal on its top-left and bottom-right corners, the ↗↙ diagonal on its top-right and bottom-left corners (P2-C3: only shows by eye), and a crosshair just outside a corner (rotate). H → an open hand, and a closed hand while the button is held. (2) R, then press on the canvas, drag out past the window's edge and release outside the window: one rectangle, selected, with the Move tool back, and moving back over the canvas draws nothing; one ⌘Z removes the whole rectangle (Review Focus 1). (3) Q and a click place a sticky; type a, Escape, then V; double-click the sticky: its text shows selected, and typing x replaces it (without the edit, x would pick the Connector tool) — why: R-LIVE sends cursor shapes and real hands to the hand checks, and synthetic input can't reach the window (memory visual-verify-sawit). The app tests reach these paths only through simulated events (a_drag_out_of_the_window_ends_as_one_undo_step mod.rs:1110, a_double_click_types_in_a_sticky :1175, resize_cursors_take_degrees events.rs:196). The expected cursors are the engine's: ToolId::cursor (tools.rs:298-310), the Text tool's I-beam over a text (pointer.rs:188-191), the Hand tool's Grab and Grabbing (tools.rs:300, pointer.rs:133), and handle_cursor (handles.rs:253-281: corners at 45° and 135° modulo 180°, edges at 90° and 180°; Rotate is Crosshair in the brief's table). A double-click enters a text with all of it selected (click.rs:81-95, board::edit_text) — costs if wrong: if P2.T7 leaves them out, a wrong cursor mapping or a difference only real events show (macOS click counts, a release outside the window) stays unseen until the user meets it.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T2) ⚠️ The other halves of the one drain: keys are P2.T4's, and P3.T3's BoardView::choose (P3-C13: toolbar flyouts, context bar, P5.T4's apply_edit, P6.T2's run_menu) isn't on the cross-plan ruling's list ("pointer event, key and run_op") → dismiss: no P2.T2 gap. Its half is in: send_pointer calls handle_ui_requests (events.rs:108), and so does run_op (mod.rs:194), pinned by an_ops_requests_reach_the_view (mod.rs:1255). CARRY to P2.T4: on_key ends with self.handle_ui_requests(Vec::new(), cx) for every outcome, through the same one dispatcher (Plan 2 Task 4: "After every key, the view drains session.ui_requests"). A GPUI test sends ⇧⌘↩ through on_key while a sticky is typed in: typing goes on in the new sticky, and session.ui_requests is empty. P2.T4's review checks it. CARRY to P3.T3: choose ends with the same drain, in the order of FigCraft's FigApp::run (ui-egui app/commands.rs:15-26): BoardDoc::execute, then self.handle_ui_requests(Vec::new(), cx), then the toast for an error, then cx.notify(), so a session.message toast never replaces the error's. Test: on a board with one sticky selected, choose("board.nextSticky", {"edit": true}) → 2 stickies, typing in the new one, session.ui_requests empty. P6.T2's run_menu keeps its "then drain" (a second drain finds nothing). Controller: extend the program ledger's one-drain line to "pointer event, key, run_op and choose" — why: choose is the port of FigApp::run, which handles UI requests after every command it runs. The spec's menu policy sends view.layers, view.design, app.actions and help.shortcuts through execute as ui_requests (spec :117-121; engine cmd/view.rs:311-313, :446-447). Other commands ask the view too: object.rename without a name → layers.rename (cmd/edit.rs:254-257), select.area without a box → selectArea (cmd/edit/select.rs:39-44), and board.nextSticky {edit: true} → board.editText (boards/board/items.rs:167-171). P3.T3's brief says nothing about requests (task-3-brief.md :39) — costs if wrong: one call per choose, which finds nothing when no request is queued. Without it, a request from the chrome waits for the next pointer move or key: a toggle or menu item that runs view.layers or view.design acts on the next hover, and board.editText starts typing only after the next key has already run as a shortcut.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T2) ⚠️ No plan covers Space+drag or middle-button panning. FigCraft does both in its UI layer (ui-egui canvas/input.rs:74, :127-142), so the port doesn't bring them, while the Excalidraw canvas pans on Space+drag → dismiss: no P2.T2 gap, but a plan carries it. CARRY to P2.T4, which creates on_key; P2.T2's events.rs is on main when it runs, and events.rs joins its files (no task that can run beside P2.T4 edits it; dag.md :129). Port both pans. (a) A plain Space (no modifiers), while the canvas has focus and no text is edited, arms a pan: on_key takes it before route_key and stops it, and a repeat changes nothing. Space's key-up (on_key_up), or the canvas or the window losing focus, disarms it. Armed, a left press on the canvas starts a pan instead of a tool press. While text is edited, Space stays the IME's (P2-C1). (b) A middle-button press on the canvas starts a pan with any tool. A pan's moves and release come through track_drags' window listeners, as a drag's do. Each move calls sawit_board::view::pan with the pointer's travel since the last one (the content follows the pointer, as with on_scroll), and the release, on or off the canvas or the window, ends it. The engine gets no pointer event from a pan (no gesture, no undo step, tool and selection unchanged), and no pan starts during a tool drag. The cursor is the open hand while armed and the closed hand while panning (ui-egui cursors.rs:69-75). Tests (#[gpui_kit::test]): a middle-button drag by (60, 40) leaves the page point that was under the press under the release, and adds no layer. With the Rectangle tool, Space down then a left drag pans and draws nothing, and after Space up the same drag draws one rectangle. While a sticky's text is edited, Space arms nothing and goes on to route_key (Unhandled, for the IME). CARRY to P2.T7's hand checks: hold Space over the canvas → an open hand; drag → the canvas follows under a closed hand; release Space → the tool's cursor is back; with a mouse, a middle-button drag pans with the Rectangle tool on; while typing in a sticky, Space types a space — why: the spec deletes Excalidraw "when the native canvas covers what the old one did" and names the hand-drawn look "the one Excalidraw trait this replacement does not carry over" (spec :10-11, :20-21). Sawit's Excalidraw canvas pans on Space+drag and on a wheel-button drag today: these are Excalidraw's defaults, and web/canvas/src/main.tsx:299-317 intercepts only ⇧⌘↩ and ⌘/⌃ app keys. D11 asks for FigJam/Figma familiarity, and FigCraft, the port source, does both. Plain Space is no engine key: KEYS has only Alt+Space and Shift+Space (keymap.rs:292, :340), and tool_key, vector edit and the shape builder ignore it. No Root, Workspace or global binding takes space (vendor/gpui-base/src/root.rs:44-49), so on_key gets it, and gpui-pre 0.3.10 has MouseButton::Middle, on_key_up and on_blur. P2.T4 rather than P2.T2's fix loop, because the arming belongs in on_key, which P2.T4 creates beside P2-C1's typing rule, and P2.T2 is approved and can integrate now — costs if wrong: about 100 lines and three tests in P2.T4, which the controller can strike before P2.T4 starts, or revert. Without a carrier, Plan 8's cutover takes away the Space+drag and wheel-button panning that Sawit's canvas has today.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T4) ⚠️ mod.rs:311-314 with :81: ⌘K while text is edited runs text.link with {}, the engine refuses it (url is required) and on_key toasts the refusal, reachable now that Board binds cmd-k → dismiss: no P2.T4 gap and no code change in any task; text.link is not made UI-handled and ⌘K is not left Unhandled while editing: ⌘K and ⇧⌘U in a text edit (text.link’s two Text-context keys, engine keymap.rs:246) keep running text.link through execute, on_key keeps toasting the engine’s reason (“invalid parameters for `text.link`: `url` must be a string”; the edit stays open and nothing changes), and the key op keeps answering {ok:false, error} with it. CARRY to P6.T4: the C18 follow-up note it writes into the Plan 6 notes (P6-C18, extended by the P6.T1 delegate ruling) also names text.link’s keyboard path, ⌘K and ⇧⌘U while text is edited, so the follow-up’s link dialog opens from those keys as well as from the Text menu and the palette, as FigCraft’s does (ui-egui shortcuts.rs:894-901 sends a Text shortcut through menus::invoke, which opens MenuInput::Link for text.link, menus.rs:172); P6.T4’s hand check doesn’t count this toast as a failure — why: the spec gives ⌘K to the canvas for app.actions and text.link (spec :86), keeps the UI-handled ids to what Sawit carries out itself (import, export, save, copy as; :111-116), runs everything else through execute (:117), and D15 makes an engine refusal a toast; Plan 2 defines UI_COMMANDS as the spec’s UI-handled ids and Ui(id) as is_ui’s outcome (plan :60, :88), so a UI-handled text.link would list an id Sawit doesn’t carry out and say “Coming soon” for a dialog no plan builds, and the key op would answer handled:true for nothing (P2-C7’s cost) where the engine’s reason now tells an agent to send url through the command op; leaving ⌘K Unhandled while editing would drop a FigCraft binding (D7) and fail silently, where Plan 6’s Review Focus 2 has a command the engine refuses show its reason; P6-C18 already accepted this same toast for text.link from the Text menu and the palette, so keys and menus stay alike; the code is P2.T1’s route_key on main (input.rs:259-262 → run → execute at :246), which P2.T4 only makes reachable by binding cmd-k as the spec’s table requires — costs if wrong: until the input-dialog follow-up, ⌘K and ⇧⌘U in a sticky or a text layer show a technical 3 s toast and make no link (nothing ships before cutover, D6); switching to “Coming soon” later is a few lines in input.rs’s run plus a test, and P6.T3’s run_ui then needs an arm for text.link.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T4) ⚠️ events.rs:143-155: the open hand while Space is held, the closed hand while panning, a real middle button, a real Space hold and Space typing a space in a sticky can only be checked by eye → dismiss: no P2.T4 gap, nothing enters the fix loop; the P2.T2 delegate ruling on the pans (rulings.md:41, progress.md:132) already carries all five to P2.T7’s hand checks (hold Space over the canvas → an open hand; drag → the canvas follows under a closed hand; release Space → the tool’s cursor is back; with a mouse, a middle-button drag pans with the Rectangle tool on; while typing in a sticky, Space types a space), and the code gives what they expect: cursor() is ClosedHand while a pan is on and OpenHand while Space is held (events.rs:143-153), hold_space leaves Space to the input method while text is edited (events.rs:91-106), and a middle press pans with any tool unless a tool drag is on (events.rs:64-74). P2.T7 writes them into the Plan 2 file under “Hand checks (pending — needs the user)” beside Step 4 (plan :423-429 doesn’t list them yet), and P2.T7’s review confirms they are there. ADD to that carry’s middle-button check: drag on past the window’s edge and release outside the window → the pan ends there: back over the canvas the Rectangle tool’s crosshair shows, not the closed hand — why: R-LIVE sends cursor shapes and real hands to the hand checks, and synthetic input can’t reach the window (memory visual-verify-sawit); P2.T4’s tests reach the pans only through simulated events (mod.rs:1699, :1720, :1742, :1765) and release every pan on the canvas (review Minor 2), so only a real release shows that the middle button’s otherMouseUp outside the window reaches the window listener that ends the pan (track_drags events.rs:249-269, release :178-185; gpui-pre-macos window.rs:143 registers otherMouseUp:), as the carried ruling requires (“the release, on or off the canvas or the window, ends it”); a pan that misses its release keeps the closed hand (cursor() while a pan is on), so the crosshair tells the two apart — costs if wrong: if P2.T7 leaves them out, a swapped hand cursor, a dead middle button, Space lost in a sticky or a pan that outlives its release stays unseen until the user meets it (a lost pan also follows other drags in the window, review Minor 1); the added clause costs the user one more drag.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T6) ⚠️ mod.rs:373-377: ⌘X in vector edit mode runs edit.cut and cuts the whole layer, where FigCraft's keyboard removes the selected points and heals the path (report Concern 2) → fix: route_key handles it (crates/board/src/input.rs:188-192, a P2.T6 file) before the Canvas shortcut becomes Ui("edit.cut"): while ds.vector_edit is set, ⌘X runs vector.deleteHeal when any VectorEdit::parts() part has points selected, else vector.edit.delete when any has segments selected, else nothing, through BoardDoc::execute, and returns Ok(KeyOutcome::Handled), so nothing is copied and the system clipboard is left alone (a port of ui-egui shortcuts.rs:195-207 cut()); route_key's doc lists it among the places where it does as FigCraft's keyboard does; input::tests port FigCraft's vector_keys_tests.rs:43-75 on an open three-point path A-B-C (vector.create, vector.edit.begin, vector.edit.select {vertices:[1]}): ⌘X is Handled and keeps the layer with 2 points joined by 1 segment; with no point selected it keeps all 3; points selected in a second edited layer (vectoredit::begin_many) go from that layer only; outside vector edit ⌘X stays Ui("edit.cut") (clipboard_keys_are_left_to_the_view). The menus' Cut (run_clipboard, Plan 6 C4) keeps cutting the layer, as FigCraft's menu Cut does (ui-egui menus.rs:196-212) — why: D7 and the P2.T1 delegate ruling make route_key follow FigCraft's keyboard where Headless::key (its agent path) differs, and FigCraft pins this case with tests, Cmd/Ctrl+X among them (vector_keys_tests.rs:43-50, "Ctrl+X on points keeps the layer"); vectoredit::key passes ⌘X on (engine canvas/vectoredit.rs:724-777 returns false with cmd), so today ⌘X with points selected takes the whole shape off the canvas; in route_key rather than the cut arm it stays on the keyboard path, the key op does what the key does (spec Testing: the ops run GPUI's path; C7 let the key op skip only what touches the system clipboard, and this doesn't), and it is tested headless; no task after P2.T6 has input.rs in its files (dag.json), so nothing later would carry it — costs if wrong: about 15 lines and three tests to revert, after which ⌘X in vector edit mode cuts the layer again as Headless::key does; one fix round on P2.T6, which has slack before P6.T2, and P2.T7 also waits on P2.T5's review.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T6) ⚠️ clipboard.rs:216-233: a text paste or cut changes the edited text outside the input method, and Sawit has no counterpart of FigCraft's note_ime_application_change (ui-egui shortcuts.rs:733-741, :790-803, :1023) → dismiss: no P2.T6 gap, nothing enters the fix loop, and P2.T5 needs no change for it. CARRY to P2.T7's hand checks, beside Step 4's "type with a Chinese IME": in a sticky being edited, with Pinyin, type ni (underlined, candidates open), then press ⌘V with hello copied in TextEdit → hello is in the sticky, no underlined ni stays once nothing is being composed, and typing on composes and commits normally after it; write down where the composition's own text went, if anywhere — why: the engine ends a composition on every text edit (edit_if sets te.preedit = None, engine canvas/textedit.rs:190-191), and paste_text (textedit::insert → insert_model → edit) and cut_text (textedit::key Delete → edit) both go through it, so ⌘V or ⌘X during a composition leaves no preedit in the document (P2-C9's rule); P2.T5 as committed (lane-5 8409c18) keys off that preedit: marked_text_range reads it (text_input.rs:80-87), unmark commits only a preedit still there in the same edit (commit_composition :282-295), and the shown composition is rebuilt once the preedit changes (Composition::refresh, crates/board/src/overlay.rs:209-232), so afterwards the platform sees no marked text and an unmark adds nothing; what stays different from FigCraft is the input method's own later events, which FigCraft drops as interrupted (shortcuts.rs:700-709), and gpui-pre 0.3.10 offers no call to discard the platform's marked text (only Window::invalidate_character_coordinates), so only a real input method shows what it does then — costs if wrong: an input method that keeps composing through the paste can commit its text after the pasted text, or show its candidates at the old place until the next key; found by hand, it costs a fix in text_input.rs after the run.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T6) ⚠️ the real system clipboard can't be driven over the socket: the key op answers handled:true for ⌘C without copying (C7's cost), and synthetic input can't reach the window → dismiss: no P2.T6 gap, nothing enters the fix loop. CARRY to P2.T7: write these into the Plan 2 file under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)", beside Step 4's items, with what to look for: (a) select a sticky, ⌘C, then ⌘V in TextEdit: it types sawit-canvas:N (N counts the copies since launch); (b) copy a word in TextEdit, click empty canvas, ⌘V: a text layer reading that word, its top-left in the middle of the view, no toast; (c) ⌃⇧⌘4 and drag over part of the screen (the capture goes to the clipboard), then ⌘V on the canvas: an image layer showing it; (d) copy an SVG file's source that starts with <?xml from a text editor, then ⌘V on the canvas: its shapes come in, in a frame, not as a text layer; (e) ⌘C a sticky in one canvas and ⌘V in another (the canvas of a second Sawit window on another folder, or a second canvas tab once Plan 7's .figcraft file tabs are in): the sticky pastes there, and ⇧⌘V pastes it again; (f) while typing in a sticky, select a word and ⌘C, then ⌘V in TextEdit: it types that word, not a marker; (g) copy text in TextEdit, then ⌘V in a sticky being edited: the text is typed at the caret and no layer appears; (h) ⌘C a sticky, quit Sawit with ⌘Q, relaunch it, ⌘V on the canvas: nothing is pasted, no toast, and no sawit-canvas:N text layer (Decision 15) — why: R-LIVE sends steps that need real hands to the hand checks; no socket op reaches BoardView::run_clipboard (run_op doesn't carry out Ui outcomes, report Decision 11, the cost P2-C7 accepted, and the command op's edit.copy stays tab-local), and an unattended run that wrote the pasteboard would also replace the user's own clipboard; the four GPUI tests (mod.rs:1964-2095) reach the glue only through the test platform's clipboard, not through NSPasteboard with another app's types, a screenshot or a relaunch — costs if wrong: if P2.T7 leaves them out, a defect only the real pasteboard shows (an image type GPUI doesn't hand over, text that arrives without a plain-text form, the marker after a relaunch) stays unseen until the user meets it.
