For whichever of P2.T5 and P2.T6 integrates second: git merge-tree lane-5 lane-1 (tree 7409e28) also stops in mod.rs's module doc (keep both sentences) and its appended tests (keep both blocks; no helper name repeats), besides the same policy.rs conflict, and both lanes add the identical BoardView::editing_text, which git merges into one — costs if wrong: an integration that stops on a conflict and goes to the Opus integrator, or a gate failure the integrator fixes; nothing reaches main unchecked.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T6) PLAN-CONFLICT clipboard.rs:145 (|| is_marker(text), is_marker at :71-74): paste_source's rule 2 takes any sawit-canvas:<digits>, not only text equal to this run's marker, and while text is edited a marker types nothing (mod.rs:421-429, Decision 3) where P2-C8 says a paste inserts the system text → dismiss: ratify Decisions 15 and 3 as built: rule 2 reads "text that is a canvas marker (sawit-canvas: then digits only), this run's or another's → Canvas"; rules 1 and 3-5 and their order stay; the brief's five tests stay unchanged; while text is edited ⌘V and ⇧⌘V type the system text, SVG source included, except a canvas marker, which types nothing; a_marker_from_another_run_is_no_text and the app step at mod.rs:2086-2089 stay — why: a marker stands for copied layers and is never text anyone meant to paste; under the literal rule every relaunch leaves the last run's marker on the system clipboard with marker() None, so ⌘V on the canvas makes a text layer reading sawit-canvas:5, a junk paste where Review Focus 5 and C16 want a quiet nothing, and the widened rule gives exactly that quiet nothing; equality can't tell runs apart anyway, since every run numbers its copies from 1 (Decision 8); FigCraft's own paste does the same (ui-egui menus/clipboard.rs:70-84, is_clipboard_marker takes any figcraft://clipboard/ marker and falls through to edit.paste); P2-C8's stated costs (no text pasted into stickies, ⌘V adding layers while typing) don't arise: other text still types and no layer is added — costs if wrong: the literal text sawit-canvas:<digits> copied elsewhere can't be pasted as text, and with two Sawit processes (an installed app beside a debug build) the other's marker pastes this one's own newest copy, which may be older layers (report Concern 4), undone with ⌘Z; going back to the brief's rule is dropping || is_marker(text), the test a_marker_from_another_run_is_no_text and that app step.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T5) ⚠️ Real IME behaviour (R-LIVE): Pinyin composition, the candidate window at the caret, an Escape cancel, the accents menu, ⌥E then E and Space in a sticky can only be checked by hand, and the report's Concern 1 is their only record → dismiss: no P2.T5 gap, nothing enters the fix loop. CARRY to P2.T7: write these into the Plan 2 file under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)", beside Step 4's "type with a Chinese IME" (plan :426), and P2.T7's review confirms they are there. Set-up: add Pinyin – Simplified (System Settings › Keyboard › Text Input › Edit › +); Q and a click place a sticky, typing in it. (1) With Pinyin, type ni → ni shows underlined in the sticky and the candidate window opens just below it, from its left end, not at a corner of the window or the screen; again after a pinch zoom-in; Space (or 1) → the first candidate (你) replaces the underlined ni and no space is typed; type ni, then Return → Pinyin commits it (the letters ni) and no new line starts. (2) Type ni, then Escape → the letters vanish and nothing goes in; the caret stays and typing composes again; a second Escape ends the edit. (3) Click-away (next ruling): type ni, click empty canvas → the edit ends with no underline, caret or candidate window left; write down what the sticky then reads (ni, 你, or nothing added) — each passes Review Focus 3 — and, for comparison, what TextEdit keeps on the same click; then double-click the sticky and type a → a composes alone, never nia. (4) An edit that ends under the input method (report Concern 3): in another app's terminal (not Sawit's own), with the launch recipe's ctl and SOCK, run sleep 5; ctl '{"cmd":"canvas","op":{"op":"key","key":"Escape"}}', switch back with ⌘Tab (a click would end the edit) and type ni in the sticky within the 5 s → the edit ends and the underline goes; double-click the sticky, type a, and write down whether a composes alone or nia shows. (5) With ABC or U.S. and press-and-hold on (the macOS default): type caf, then hold e → the accents menu opens by the e; choose é → the sticky reads café. (6) ⌥E → ´ shows underlined at the caret; then E → é, no ´ left. (7) Space: the P2.T2/P2.T4 carry's "while typing in a sticky, Space types a space" stands; with Pinyin on and nothing composed, Space types a space too. (8) The caret holds steady where FigCraft blinks it (report Decision 6, review Minor 3): say whether that is fine. (9) While ni is composing, pan with two fingers → the candidate window stays where it was until the next key (review Minor 2, deferred): say whether it bothers — why: R-LIVE sends real IME composition to the hand checks, and synthetic input can't reach the window (memory visual-verify-sawit); T5's brief has no live step, and a plan file is edited only by its plan's live check (dag.md :129); T5's tests reach the input handler by calling it or through GPUI's test platform (lane-5 mod.rs:2156-2412, text_input.rs:401-487), never through NSTextInputContext, a candidate window, press-and-hold or a dead key; the expectations follow gpui-pre-macos 0.3.10: while a composition is marked a key goes to the input method first and comes back only through doCommandBySelector (window.rs:2673-2737, :3520), and insertText, setMarkedText and unmarkText call the handler straight (:3440-3486) — costs if wrong: if P2.T7 leaves them out, a candidate window at the screen's corner, a lost or doubled commit, a new line from Return, an accent or dead key that doesn't replace its letter, or a composition carried into the next edit stays unseen until the user meets it; the steps cost the user about ten minutes.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T5) ⚠️ Click-away while composing: gpui-pre-macos 0.3.10 gives a mouse-down to NSTextInputContext before the canvas sees it (window.rs:2879-2885, the press reaches GPUI at :2939), so an input method that commits on a click commits into the sticky before the press ends the edit, and Pinyin likely commits the letters ni, where P2-C9's app test (mod.rs:2246) models only the other order → dismiss: a committed ni meets Review Focus 3; nothing enters the fix loop. RF3 reads: no composition text reaches the document unless the input method commits it. A click-away the input method answers with a commit keeps what it commits (ni, 你, …), the Mac convention (a click in TextEdit keeps what was composed); one it ignores leaves nothing. P2.T7 records what the sticky reads (the previous ruling's step 3); the stale composition of the report's Concern 3 stays a hand check (step 4), as the P2.T6 ruling on paste left it (progress.md:151). Checked that RF3 holds in every order: the commit arrives as insertText, which calls replace_text_in_range straight (window.rs:3440-3459), so it is committed text, shown plain; an input method that ignores the click leaves a preedit that the press's textedit::end drops with the TextEdit (engine textedit.rs:110-112; a press away ends the edit, engine tools drag.rs:21, click.rs:152); an unmark after that commits nothing (owner rule, text_input.rs:282-295, pinned by mod.rs:2246); a commit or mark with no edit open types nothing (replace and compose return without a text edit, text_input.rs:211-213, :238-240, and gpui-pre drops the handler from the next frame, gpui-pre window.rs:3466-3508); saves read board.doc(), and the composition's document goes only to the render job and the overlay (mod.rs:519, :539-541, :730); the commit-then-press order is the plain commit path (the_input_method_commits_its_composition, mod.rs:2295: the commit replaces the composition and a later unmark adds nothing) followed by an ordinary press — why: the spec says only that text input goes through EntityInputHandler, ElementInputHandler and window.handle_input and is silent on what a click does to a composition, so RF3's "cancelled … by clicking away" is the plan's reading of FigCraft, whose egui UI rejects the IME events of an interrupted composition (ui-egui shortcuts.rs:685-709); through gpui on macOS the input method sees the click first, gpui-pre 0.3.10 has no call to discard the platform's marked text (only Window::invalidate_character_coordinates), and dropping a commit the input method made would throw away text the user saw, unlike Mac text views — costs if wrong: if the user wants FigCraft's cancel-on-click, the sticky keeps what the input method commits on a click-away (deleted by hand, as in TextEdit) until a post-run change in text_input.rs drops a commit that arrives with a press away from the text, which needs a hook gpui-pre lacks (AppKit's current event through objc2-app-kit, already an app dependency at crates/app/Cargo.toml:38, or deferring the commit past the press); a stale composition that step 4 shows going on into the next edit is fixed there too, with NSTextInputContext discardMarkedText when an edit ends while ime_owner is set.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T5) ⚠️ Integration with P2.T6: the report's merge-tree finds 3 keep-both hunks in crates/app/src/board/mod.rs (module doc, test helpers, tests), which only the integrator's rebase and gates can verify → dismiss: no P2.T5 gap, nothing enters the fix loop; the integrator rebases lane-5 onto main under R-LANES, resolves the three hunks keep-both, then runs the gates (fmt, clippy -D warnings, cargo test -p sawit-board and -p sawit). Checked at the current heads: P2.T6 is on main (a2208cf; the commit before it, 1cd4cc4, touches only Cargo.lock and crates/mcp), and git merge-tree --write-tree main lane-5 (tree e707771) stops only in crates/app/src/board/mod.rs, in the report's three hunks: (1) the module doc's last sentence: keep main's clipboard sentence, then T5's text-input sentence; (2) and (3) the test helpers and tests both sides append: keep main's block, then T5's, each whole, so main's reads and T5's sticky_reads each end with the 5 lines the conflict shares (let text = doc.node(text)…; text.characters.clone(); }); }; a blank line), and main's last test (ending no_toast(&view, &cx);) and T5's (ending assert_eq!(caret(&mut cx), 1..1);) each get their own closing brace before the module's. Resolved that way in a scratch copy, the file has no conflict marker, rustfmt --check passes on it and on the board modules it declares, it holds 47 tests (main's 40 + T5's 7), and no name is defined twice (the only repeats, open, run_op and drop, are main's own method and test-helper pairs); editing_text, which both lanes add identically, merges into one; T5's rename of the paint closure's handle (drags → painted) leaves no drags reference; the test module's imports merge without a repeat; and every BoardView item text_input.rs calls (handle_ui_requests, show_toast, board, board_mut, focus, editing_text) is on main with T5's signatures — why: this is an integration step under R-LANES, foreseen by the P2.T6 ruling on its own rebase (progress.md:153), not T5 code; the gates after the rebase are what verify it — costs if wrong: a rebase that stops for the Opus integrator, or a gate failure it fixes; nothing reaches main unchecked.
- Ruling: (delegate, P2.T5) ⚠️ The report's clippy gate prints cargo's "block v0.1.6" future-incompat note, which no ruling records as known → dismiss: no P2.T5 finding, nothing enters the fix loop; from now on the note is KNOWN in Plan 2's tasks, as R-EHFRAME's linker line is: no task's finding, and no task bumps, patches or drops a dependency or changes lint levels for it (any other cargo or clippy message is still a finding); the controller may record it once in the program ledger beside R-EHFRAME so other plans' reviews stop raising it — why: it predates the program: Plan 1's baseline clippy run, before any canvas commit, prints the same note (2026-10-29-native-canvas-1-foundation/baseline-clippy.log:1155); block 0.1.6 (Cargo.lock:730 at a474bcc) comes in through cocoa, cocoa-foundation, metal, objc-foundation and screencapturekit-sys, the macOS platform crates; T5 changes no manifest or lockfile (git diff a474bcc lane-5 touches four .rs files only); it is cargo's notice about a dependency, not a rustc or clippy warning, so clippy -D warnings exits 0; the P2.T1, P2.T3 and P2.T6 reviews already called it no finding (task-1-review.md:33, task-3-review.md:80, task-6-review.md:91) — costs if wrong: none during this run, since rust-toolchain.toml pins 1.99.1; the rejection bites only at a deliberate toolchain bump, which then has to update the crates that pull block 0.1.6 in, or patch it.

z22-big.png and z24l-big.png after); D4 fixes overlay colours to the canvas colours, which rules out FigCraft's theme-coloured caret (ui-egui overlays.rs:231), and no spec line, brief, plan line (:206 Caret {top, bottom, color}; :328 'a Caret from layout.caret') or ruling sets a width; the fix is one pure GPUI-free function, one constant and one paint arm, leaves Shape::Caret's data, caret_at and the IME's bounds_for_range unchanged, has RED/GREEN tests, and the workspace passes at 17a6e48 (1097 passed, 0 failed); landing it from this lane is safe: no lane-2..5 commit beyond main touches either file, no unfinished dag task lists them (only P2.T3 and P2.T5, both on main), and git merge-tree --write-tree main lane-1 at main 504abec merges with no conflict; - Ruling: (delegate, P2.T7) ⚠️ bold doesn't show in stickies on a Mac without Inter (plan :527-540, report Concern 1): ⌘B, ⌘I and weight changes change the document but not the canvas, and all Inter text renders in the fallback face → dismiss: no P2.T7 gap, nothing enters the fix loop (the live check found, diagnosed and escalated it, which is its job), but a real program gap that no task carries, so CARRY: (1) P8.T1 embeds Inter: InterVariable.ttf, InterVariable-Italic.ttf and their OFL-1.1 text go to crates/board/fonts/ (the text as OFL-Inter.txt); local copies with the licence are in ~/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/cosmic-text-0.19.0/fonts/ (Inter-LICENSE), else take rsms/inter's release; a new crates/board/src/fonts.rs (pub mod fonts; in lib.rs, R2) has register(), run once (OnceLock), which include_bytes! both and adds them as figcraft-text adds its own embedded fonts (fontdb.rs:551-557): Face::from_bytes(FontBytes::Static(bytes), 0, Some("Inter"), Some("Regular" or "Italic")), db().write().add(face), then figcraft_text::clear_layout_cache(), which bumps layout_generation so boards re-render (D13); the family override is required, because Inter 4's variable files name their family 'Inter Variable' (name ID 1), so load_font_bytes would register that name and documents asking for Inter would still miss it; board::init (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:62) calls register() before the system-font scan, and sawit-mcp's headless path (crates/mcp/src/app.rs:502) calls it before it runs an op (crates/app/src/main.rs:107 starts sawit-mcp without board::init); sawit-board tests: after register(), family_styles("Inter") lists Regular, Bold and Italic, and the same text set in Inter Bold lays out wider than in Inter Regular. (2) P8.T3: NOTICE credits Inter after FigCraft's text (its copyright line verbatim from the licence file, the SIL Open Font License 1.1, and the full text at crates/board/fonts/OFL-Inter.txt), and README's Credits names it; shipping NOTICE and the OFL text inside Sawit.app stays P8-C11's follow-up, which now covers both and should land before the first release that embeds Inter (FigCraft's packaging ships both: packaging/macos/package.sh:118-123). (3) P8.T4 says how much of the release size change is the fonts (about 1.7 MiB) and adds a hand check: with no Inter installed, ⌘B in a sticky shows bold, and the Design panel lists Inter's weights; this replaces Plan 2 hand check 4's Helvetica Neue workaround. (4) Until P8.T1 lands, Inter text rendering in Helvetica Neue Regular whatever its style is KNOWN in every task, the live checks P3.T4, P4.T3, P5.T6, P6.T4 and P7.T7 included: no task's finding, no task embeds fonts or changes a family for it, and a check that must show a style sets its text in a system family, as Plan 2 hand check 4 does. (5) P7.T8: sawit-mcp's headless path has no font at all today (it never scans the system's fonts, and Sawit embeds none), and figcraft-text skips a run that has no face (text layout.rs:383), so text that its headless export_svg and export_png lay out would come out with no glyphs: headless() first calls figcraft_text::scan_system_fonts_on_demand() (through sawit-board, since sawit-mcp has no figcraft-text dependency), as FigCraft's CLI does (apps/figcraft-cli/src/main.rs:117), with a test that a headless export of a text layer has glyphs — why: Inter is the family every new FigCraft text, sticky and label starts in (figcraft-doc text.rs:275-277), and FigCraft's official releases embed it (CRAFT_FONTS_DIR, packaging/env.sh:93, and its NOTICE); a git dependency never reads FigCraft's .cargo/config.toml, so CRAFT_FONTS is empty in Sawit, and figcraft-text lays a missing family out in default_face(), Helvetica Neue Regular, whatever the style (layout.rs:356, fontdb.rs:740); this Mac answers text.fonts {family: Inter} with []; the spec presumes the styles show (the conflict table gives ⌘B to text.bold while text is edited, and the Design panel's Typography section edits weight and style), while D13 scans only system fonts and the missing-fonts download dialog is a non-goal, so nothing but an embed can supply Inter; FIGCRAFT_DEFAULT_FONT would only rename new text and leave FigCraft files and examples (D14; P5.T6's ridgeline-app.figcraft) in the fallback; nothing ships before cutover (D6) and the hand checks run after the run (R-LIVE), so Plan 8 is early enough, keeps Plans 3-7 as planned, puts the font beside its licence notice (D16), and leaves the controller time to strike it before any font lands in git — costs if wrong: about 1.7 MiB in the app and in git history, about 60 lines and two tests in P8.T1, and a NOTICE paragraph; to accept the gap instead, strike (1)-(3) and keep (4) for good (Inter text then never shows bold, italic or weights on a Mac without Inter, and the Design panel names a family the canvas doesn't draw); to land it sooner, move (1) to a task that hasn't started (P5.T5, the typography views), at the cost of that task's scope; (5) is one call, harmless if headless text already finds a font.
- Final review: Ruling: (delegate) Important 1 of Plan 2's final review, still open after its fix wave (re-review: NOT ADDRESSED): a double-click on a frame's or section's name renames nothing, because BoardView::handle_ui_requests sends canvas.renameLabel {id} to its catch-all arm, which logs and drops it (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:442-457, the arm at :452, at main a77fa36) — load-bearing, no Plan 2 code: CARRY to P4.T2, which hasn't started (Plan 4's progress.md has no Task 2 line) and builds the rename Input, the blank-keeps-the-old-name rule and the panels' arms of that match: P4.T2 adds a "canvas.renameLabel" arm that reads data.id (figcraft_engine::params::id_of, as FigCraft does) and starts a rename of that one layer, either as P4-C11 handles layers.rename for ids [id] (show_layers = true, reveal, that row's rename Input, scrolled into view: the smaller change) or as an inline Input over the name's label box, which overlay_paint::layout measures and tool.labels keeps as a page quad (FigCraft's and Excalidraw's way, and a board's panels stay hidden, D11); either closes the finding; the rename commits object.rename {ids: [id], name} through BoardView::choose, and a blank or unchanged name runs no command (Plan 4 Review Focus 2; the P4.T1 delegate ruling on rename); an inline Input in the Board key context relies on the on_key focus guard, on main since a77fa36 (mod.rs:489-491); GPUI test: with the Move tool, a double-click on a frame's name starts its rename, and committing a blank name leaves the name and the undo depth unchanged; why: on a Move or Scale double-click on a name the engine selects that layer and queues the request (engine canvas/tools/click.rs:31-45), Plan 2 made the path live (names are hit-tested through tool.labels, and pointer_op_leaves_the_views_requests_for_it, crates/board/src/ops.rs:2759-2790, pins the request), no plan's list of arms holds it (Plan 2 Task 4: panel.layers, panel.design, palette.actions, panel.shortcuts; Plans 4 to 6: layers.rename, layers.collapseAll, panel.leftSidebar, contextMenu), FigCraft renames in place (ui-egui app/commands.rs:239-241), and the re-review checked the fix report's draft of this carry (final-fix-report.md:42-59) against the engine; this line names P4.T2, so sync_rulings.sh copies it into Plan 4's rulings.md, which closes the item — costs if wrong: if P4.T2 runs without it, after cutover a section's name can't be changed where it shows, which the Excalidraw canvas does today, and nothing says why, since boards open with both panels hidden (D11); the arm is then a follow-up of a few lines and one GPUI test in the next task that edits handle_ui_requests; carried, P4.T2 grows by one match arm and one test, more if it builds the inline field.

## Plan 3 (toolbar)

- Ruling: P3-C1 — Stickies get [Colors] only; ink [Colors]; shapes [Colors, Shape, FontSize]; connectors [Colors, Routing, StartCap, EndCap]. Rename the test to sticky_selection_shows_colours, expecting [Colors] with current == Some("yellow"). — why: The spec calls the bar a port of board_bar.rs, whose has() gives stickies colour only, and the pinned engine cannot size a sticky's note; the plan already reads the spec's 'shape' as shapes-only, and Review Focus 2 forbids a control that errors. — costs if wrong: Stickies lack S/M/L/XL buttons that would need an engine change to work.
- Ruling: P3-C2 — controls_run selects a sticky, a shape and a connector in turn and applies, per control, the first option that differs from current, asserting Ok; P3.T3 treats picking the current shape as a no-op (no command, no toast). — why: board.setStyle refuses an unchanged shape at the pinned rev; Review Focus 2. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C3 — ContextBar.anchor is where the bar's bottom-centre goes (selection top-centre - 12 px); clamp_anchor takes and returns the bar's top-centre exactly as its test pins; P3.T3 calls clamp_anchor((ax, ay - bar_h), (bar_w, bar_h), canvas) and puts the bar's top-left at (x - bar_w/2, y). — why: Keeps both stated facts true and keeps the bar off the selection, as board_bar.rs place() does. — costs if wrong: One subtraction to move.
- Ruling: P3-C4 — clamp_anchor never panics: compute with max/min so the lower bound (the 8 px margin) wins when the canvas is smaller than the bar + 16 px; add one assert to anchor_is_clamped_inside_canvas for such a canvas. — why: D15 never-panic; zero and narrow canvases are already a Plan 1 review focus. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C5 — current = the BOARD_COLORS name matching a sticky's fill, a shape's outline child's fill, or a connector's or ink's stroke compared with each board colour's 0.55 line shade (a local copy of FigCraft's private line_shade, commented); None when nothing matches or the items differ. — why: Those are exactly the paints board.setColor writes at the pinned rev. — costs if wrong: A missing check mark on connector or ink swatches if a later rev changes the shade factor.
- Ruling: P3-C6 — Only stickies, shapes, connectors and ink take part; other selected layers (stamps, labels, tables, sections, non-board layers) are left out of the intersection, as board_bar.rs has() leaves out layers that take nothing; context_bar is None when none of the four is selected or the shared set is empty. — why: board.setColor and board.setStyle skip layers that don't take the value and succeed when any changes, so every shown control still works (Review Focus 2), and select-all over a template keeps a colour bar. — costs if wrong: With a section and a sticky selected, the bar shows colours that change only the sticky.
- Ruling: P3-C7 — P3.T2 appends `pub mod context_bar;` to crates/board/src/lib.rs and lists it under Files, as its git add line already does (Plan 1 ruling R2). — why: A module must be declared to compile. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C8 — place_template sends {"name": id} with no x/y (the engine centres it on view.cx/cy); place_table sends {"rows": r, "columns": c}. — why: Parameter names at the pinned rev (board_templates.rs template_cmd; board_table.rs spec, which refuses `cols`). — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C9 — P3.T3 adds every Lucide icon that tool_icon and the chrome use and that is not already embedded to ExtraIcons in crates/app/src/assets.rs, lists that file and git-adds it. — why: Otherwise the toolbar renders blank buttons; the Global Constraints require Sawit's Lucide icons. — costs if wrong: About a dozen small SVGs in the binary.
- Ruling: P3-C10 — While the toolbar shows, P3.T3 lifts the canvas toast to sit 8 px above the toolbar; with the UI hidden the toast keeps Plan 1's bottom position. — why: Spec D11 (toolbar at the bottom centre) and D15 (toast at the bottom) both hold. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C11 — P3.T3 builds the name, zoom menu, Hide UI and Maximize in top_bar.rs; P6.T2 inserts the main-menu button first; P4.T2 and P5.T4 add their panel toggles in crates/app/src/board/top_bar.rs (add it to their Files) and depend on P3.T3, like P6.T2. — why: The spec's top bar holds all of these, and a file must exist before it is extended. — costs if wrong: None; adds scheduling edges P4.T2, P5.T4 and P6.T2 -> P3.T3.
- Ruling: P3-C12 — Keep the button as "Hide UI" with the tooltip "Hide UI Cmd+\" (shown with Mac glyphs); Cmd+\ (and Plan 6's empty-canvas context menu) shows the UI again. — why: D11 hides all chrome with Cmd+\; a button that can only hide should say so. — costs if wrong: Label wording.
- Ruling: P3-C13 — BoardView::choose (P3.T3) is the single execute -> toast on error -> notify path; P5.T4's apply_edit calls choose(edit.command, edit.params, cx) and P6.T2's run_menu calls it for non-UI ids. — why: Avoids a duplicated logic block that a review would flag. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C14 — P3.T3 calls toolbar::templates() when the Templates & table popover opens and keeps the list on BoardView until it closes; P3.T1's signature stays. — why: Every execute bumps ds.revision and grows session.journal; an idle canvas must stay at about 0 % CPU. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C15 — Step 1 launches as Plan 1 Task 7 Step 1 without the cp line (empty project, no canvas.figcraft), as Plan 2 Task 7 does, so the canvas is a new board at 100 %. — why: Matches Step 1's expectation and Step 3's later copy of ridgeline. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C16 — After the pointer up, send {"op":"key","key":"Escape"} to end text editing, then select.all, then screenshot. — why: The bar is hidden while editing (P3.T3) and select.all does not end editing; Plan 2 Task 7 uses the same Escape. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P3-C17 — Stop only this instance (/bin/kill on the pid from pgrep -f "$S/home"), copy ridgeline-app.figcraft over $S/proj/.nyawit/canvas.figcraft, relaunch with the same $S, then open the canvas page. — why: The open tab would not reload and its autosave could overwrite the copy; Plan 4 Task 3 reuses this recipe. — costs if wrong: One extra relaunch. — Amended by the controller: stop the instance with `/bin/kill $PID` saved at launch (Plan 1 C7, program rule R-LAUNCH); `pgrep -f "$S/home"` matches nothing because SAWIT_HOME is in the environment, not in argv.
- Ruling: P3-C18 — After Step 4, commit the recorded answers alone: git add docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-native-canvas-3-toolbar.md, subject 'docs: record plan 3 live checks' (Plan 1 ruling R5 pattern). — why: Lanes integrate commits only. — costs if wrong: One doc commit.
- Ruling: P3-C19 — Read it as the name label and the zoom button side by side ('Canvas', '100%'); P3.T3 needs no separator. — why: P3.T3's interface is the binding description of the top bar. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T2) ⚠️ consumer contract spanning P3.T3 (clamp recipe P3-C3; picking the current shape is a no-op P3-C2; bar hidden while session.tool.busy() or text is edited) → dismiss: no P3.T2 gap; these are P3.T3's obligations and already bind it — P3-C2 and P3-C3 name P3.T3 in rulings.md, which the T3 implementer must apply ('every ruling that names this task'; note P3.T3's dag.json notes list C3 but not C2) and the T3 reviewer judges the brief as amended by; the hide rule is in the T3 brief itself, on real engine state (Tool::busy, engine canvas/tools.rs:578; DocState::text_edit, doc_state.rs:72); the model documents all three (context_bar.rs:49-51, :79-82, :258-259, :302-305) and pins the clamp recipe in a test (:925-936); the engine refuses only an unchanged shape (boards/board/edit.rs:123-128; setColor, fontSize, routing and caps all count as changed on a re-pick), so P3-C2's Shape-only no-op is complete — costs if wrong: until P3.T3's review or FINAL.P3 catches it, re-picking the current shape shows a 'nothing selected takes these' toast, or the bar covers the selection's top by its own height.
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T2) ⚠️ the option→command mapping exists only in the test-local options() (context_bar.rs:399-446), so the view's own copy can drift untested → fix: P3.T2 makes it public model API — pub fn options(control: &Control) -> Vec<ControlOption>, ControlOption with P3.T1 FlyoutItem's fields (command, params, label, swatch, selected) and exactly today's commands (fontSize as the TEXT_SIZES number); its tests pick through it; the docs tell the view to render options() and run the pick through BoardView::choose (a selected Shape option runs nothing, P3-C2); the brief's interface stays as is; P3.T3 renders options() instead of writing its own mapping — spec D2 puts panel and menu models in sawit-board under headless TDD and keeps views thin, as P3.T1's flyout() already does for the toolbar; as planned, the shipped mapping would live untested in the GPUI view (no P3.T3 test, P3.T4 live step or hand check picks a context-bar option), and the plan's own comment 'board.setStyle {fontSize}; names from board::TEXT_SIZES' invites {fontSize: 'M'}, which the engine parses as no size and refuses (engine automation/params.rs:14-21, boards/board/edit.rs:197-199), so Review Focus 2 ('never a control that errors') would rest on a test copy; a dismissal's 'T3 reviewer compares' would reach P3.T3 only if copied into rulings.md (its implementer and reviewer prompts bind rulings.md, not this ledger) — costs if wrong: about 40 lines of public API P3.T3 may not use, and one fix round on P3.T2 before P3.T3 (not started) begins.
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T1) ⚠️ consumer halves of Review Focus 3, 4 and 5 live in P3.T3 (each button's .selected from toolbar::active(&session) on every render; open_flyout reset when kind() changes and no design flyout; templates() on popover open, a flyout item runs item.command with a clone of item.params through BoardView::choose) → dismiss: no P3.T1 gap, nothing enters the fix loop — the model halves are proved on lane-2 at 4133077 (active_tool_follows_session toolbar.rs:567 for q, b, x, v; groups_differ_by_kind :406; groups_follow_the_specs_lists :419 pins flyout None on all five design groups, :450-474; every_flyout_item_runs :610; template_is_one_undo_step :639) and the model docs state each consumer duty (:1-5 a button or flyout item runs its command with its params through BoardView::choose; :216-218 the view marks the active button on every frame; :297-302 templates() is asked when the picker opens and kept until it closes); the consumer halves already bind P3.T3 through what its implementer and reviewer prompts carry, not only this ledger: task-3-brief.md :28 (active tool .selected), :32 and test flyout_closes_on_kind_change :41 (reset open_flyout), Review Focus 3 and 4 at :81-82 (shown active on the next frame; no stale flyout), rulings.md P3-C13 (choose is the one execute path) and P3-C14 (templates() on popover open; dag.json P3.T3 notes list C9-C14); a design group cannot open a flyout because its group.flyout is None and the brief adds a chevron only to flyout groups (engine boards/board.rs:205-222 refuses tool.sticky/shape/connector/stamp off a board); a GPUI-free model cannot stop a view from caching, and P3.T3 does not exist yet (main has no BoardView), so FINAL.P3, which checks every delegate ruling against main HEAD, confirms the three in crates/app/src/board/toolbar.rs — costs if wrong: after Q, B or X the toolbar highlights the old tool until a click, or a stale board flyout over a design file toasts on every item (tool.sticky even rewrites the remembered sticky colour before require refuses, board.rs:160-165), or board.templates runs every frame and the canvas never idles (P8.T4 idle-CPU budget); each is a few lines in crates/app/src/board/toolbar.rs.
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T3) ⚠️ how the chrome looks can't be checked from the diff: the context bar's menu widths (context_bar.rs:44-48), flyouts and the picker opening above the toolbar (Anchor::BottomCenter, toolbar.rs:238, :320), the .selected look of ghost tool buttons (toolbar.rs:206) → fix: one change, and the rest stands with no extra P3.T4 screenshot: widen the text-size menu (context_bar.rs:45, Control::FontSize) from 52 to 76 px and name "Mixed" in its width comment — why: I measured the labels with CoreText in the system font the app draws with, at the app's real rem of 13 px (zed-one-dark.json font.size 13, which gpui-component root.rs:714 makes the rem; theme.rs applies it at startup). At that rem a .small() menu button has 11.375 px text, 6.5 px padding on each side, a 3.25 px gap and an 11.375 px caret. Every option label fits: shape 160 px, "Rounded rectangle" 102.1 px in 132.4 px of room; caps 156 px, "Reversed triangle" 94.1 in 128.4; routing 92 px, "Straight" 42.9 in 64.4; size 52 px, "XL" 14.3 in 24.4. They still fit at a 16 px rem, the 14 px text the review says the widths were sized for (122.3/126, 112.5/122, 51.2/58, 17.2/18). The one label that doesn't fit is the view's own fallback "Mixed" (context_bar.rs:171-173) in the size menu: 32.1 px against 24.4 px. So selecting shapes whose text sizes differ, or aren't one of S/M/L/XL (crates/board/src/context_bar.rs:242-251), shows "Mi…". No later task touches this file (only Plan 3 Task 3 lists it), so the bug would ship. 76 px fits "Mixed" by the same rule (38.5 + 34 ≤ 76). The tests pin the controls to the bar's own edges (mod.rs:3061-3062, :3116-3117), so none change. The direction holds, and a live check couldn't test it anyway, because synthetic clicks can't open a popover (R-LIVE). Anchor names the popover's own corner (gpui-component popover.rs:157-161). The vendored base Popup puts that corner, the bottom centre, at the trigger's top centre less the 0.25 rem gap (vendor/gpui-base/src/popup.rs:109). Positioner::corner clamps it 8 px inside the window and never flips it (positioner.rs:94-100, :218-223; test corner_positioning_clamps_but_does_not_flip :577), and the gpui-component test pins BottomCenter above its trigger (popover.rs:613). Only a picker taller than the room above it, in a short window, gets pinned 8 px from the window top and covers the toolbar instead of opening below. The selected look holds: a selected ghost Button paints tokens.secondary_active (button.rs:1325) as its selected state (:815-826) and shows no hover or press colours while selected (:730, :755). That is #454a56 on the toolbar's popover colour #2f343e, distinct from the ghost hover (accent #363c46 at 0.5). Sawit already uses this mark for the active sidebar panel tab on the same #2f343e (workspace.rs:1106-1111) and for the ERD toggles (db/diagram.rs:936-940). These are Sawit's theme colours, as D12 requires. P3.T4's Step 1 screenshot shows the mark on the active tool, and its Step 4 hand check of tools and flyouts covers how it feels — costs if wrong: one fix round on P3.T3 (a constant and a comment, then the gates and a re-review), which delays its integration and its dependents P3.T4, P4.T2, P5.T4 and P6.T2 by that round, and makes a shape's context bar 24 px wider. Left unfixed, selections of shapes with mixed text sizes read "Mi…" until someone widens the menu.
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T4) ⚠️ run 1's board changed with no check op behind it (plan :338-343: a Flow chart board at 34 %, emptied again by 18:18:21, held in canvas.figcraft.bak), and the note's premise "only the Templates & table picker places a template" is false (board.template from any control-socket client places one) → fix (docs only): the fixer restates that note on the evidence below and names the likely cause, a person at the machine, as not yet confirmed; controller: ask the user at the run's next report whether they used the debug Sawit window around 18:17-18:18 (a delegate can't ask them); CARRY to the live checks P4.T3, P5.T6, P6.T4, P7.T7 and P8.T4: the machine is attended (R-ROOT 17:55, and this run), so each step starts from a get (and the zoom label), a change that no op of the check made is recorded with its time and evidence and undone with ops before going on, never failing the check, and {"cmd":"focus"} is sent only right before a screenshot batch, since each one raises Sawit over the user's work — why: a person fits every trace and no other socket client could have reached this one: the instance listened on the per-SAWIT_HOME fallback /tmp/sawit-$USER-945615192beed5f.sock (live-P3.T4/tools/env.sh), which sawit-mcp and the sawit CLI never use without that SAWIT_HOME (AppPaths::default_macos and cli_socket, crates/core/src/instances.rs:20-28, :62-72; app_control.rs:303-309), no file in the scratchpad but this check's own names it, the run's other live checks (live-P1.T7, live-P2.T7) ended at 06:12 and 17:25 on their own sockets, and the agents at work then (P5.T4's implementer, the FINAL.P2 review's test runs) use their own temp roots; 34 % is no step of the zoom menu or keys (they halve, double or jump to 50, 100 or 200 %, engine view_behaviour_tests.rs:96-118) and no fit of the 1220 × 360 flow chart on that 1040 pt wide canvas (about 75 % with the 64 pt margin, engine view_state.rs:91-103), but it is what a pinch or a ⌘-scroll leaves; the window is key in s1 (18:17:20) and no longer in s2a (18:18:34), so another app was brought forward in between; the check's verdicts don't rest on that board (Step 2 reset the view and placed its own sticky), so only the record's reasoning needs the fix — costs if wrong: if nobody touched the window, an unknown client drove a debug instance's socket; the note then still says unconfirmed, and the controller's question to the user brings it out; the doc fix is one paragraph of the plan file that the narrow-canvas fix below edits anyway.
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T4) ⚠️ a view.set sent right after a fresh open_page is lost to the first-frame fit (c01 and c02 at 73 %; crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:769-772, fitted) → dismiss: no P3.T4 gap, and no code change carried — confirmed by reading: zoom_to_fit has one call site, the BoardView's first prepaint with a size (mod.rs:769-772), as Plan 1 specifies ("The first frame calls zoom_to_fit", Plan 1 :336), so a view op that runs between the canvas opening (control.rs:320-330, Command::Canvas opens it and runs the op at once) and its first painted frame is replaced by the fit; ops in that gap run against the engine's default 1200 × 800 viewport (ViewState::default, engine view_state.rs:43-50), not the canvas's, so a viewport-dependent result (view.zoomFit, view.zoomToSelection) would be wrong for the real canvas anyway, and the fit at the real size is the better view; what is lost is an absolute view.set sent in the same instant, while the document is untouched and no spec line promises that a view op outlives the open (agent wire contract: command answers {ok, result}); keeping such ops needs the canvas's size before layout, or ops held until the first frame (Answer::Later, which stalls while a covered window doesn't paint), a change to Plan 1's foundation out of proportion to a lost zoom; the notes' "same view" wording is already Task 4's deferred minor (progress.md:116); CARRY to the live checks P4.T3, P5.T6, P6.T4, P7.T7 and P8.T4: after open_page on a fresh launch, let one frame paint (a 0.5 s wait or a screenshot) before any view op, and read the zoom label before relying on it — costs if wrong: an agent's zoom or pan sent before the canvas first paints (Sawit covered, or the canvas just opened by that same op) shows the fit-all view instead, though its reply said ok; the remedy is local to BoardView: keep the view.* commands run before the first frame and run them again after the fit in prepaint (about 15 lines and one GPUI test).
- Ruling: (delegate, P3.T4) ⚠️ narrow canvas, the controller's decision before P4.T2 and P5.T4 land (report Concern 1; plan :356-365, hand check 6 :413; Task 3 deferred minor, progress.md:106): the toolbar pill (508 pt on a board, 476 pt on a design file) is flex_shrink_0 with no wrap or scroll, so a narrower canvas cuts both ends → fix: the pill keeps its full width while the canvas has room for it and 8 pt on each side, and is never wider than the canvas less 16 pt; when its contents don't fit, they keep their size and scroll sideways inside it (overflow_x_scroll, which GPUI also drives with a vertical wheel on an x-only scroller, gpui-pre 0.3.10 elements/div.rs:3413-3421); one GPUI test; the Task 4 notes and hand check 6 describe it — why: it is FigCraft's own rule for the toolbar this spec ports for FigJam/Figma familiarity (D11): ui-egui toolbar.rs pill_rect :474-478 caps its pill at the canvas less 16 px and pill_scroll :187-199 scrolls the buttons inside it, pinned by pill_never_outgrows_a_phone_canvas (:640-652); the spec keeps the other floating bar inside the canvas too ("The bar is clamped inside the canvas"); D11's own layout makes the canvas narrow: 480 pt at the 720 pt minimum window with Sawit's sidebar (14 pt cut at each end today), and with P4.T2's and P5.T4's 240 + 264 pt panels beside a design file, which D11 opens with both, 256 pt in a 1000 pt window with the sidebar, where about 110 pt goes at each end (Move, Scale and Hand; Pen, Pencil and Text), and 216 pt at the minimum window without it; only P3.T3 lists crates/app/src/board/toolbar.rs (dag.json: P4.T2, P5.T4 and P6.T2 change top_bar.rs and mod.rs), so nothing later would fix it before cutover, and a pill that follows the canvas's width needs nothing from P4.T2 or P5.T4 (P5.T4's canvas is already flex_1 with min_w_0, lane 5 843b726); wrapping would make the pill two rows tall, against the 60 pt bottom band the context bar and the toast are clamped above (context_bar.rs:94-97, mod.rs:984), and smaller buttons would shrink the hit areas — costs if wrong: one fix round on P3.T4 (about 15 lines in toolbar.rs, one GPUI test, two doc paragraphs, the gates and a re-review), which delays FINAL.P3 by that round (P4.T3 and P5.T6 also wait on P4.T2 and P5.T5, which haven't started); if hand check 6 shows the user wants wrap or shrink, it is a local change to the same lines. Left as it is, the board toolbar loses 14 pt at each end at the minimum window, and once Plans 4 and 5 land a design file in a window under about 1000 pt loses six tools to the pointer (their keys still work) until the user hides a panel.

- Final review: parked — The context bar during a version preview: FigCraft hides its bar on a read-only file, but version history is a spec non-goal and its commands are hidden (declined to judge) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, with one correction: FigCraft hides its bar only while active_read_only() holds (ui-egui canvas/board_bar.rs:168), a live session's view-only access or publishing (engine sharing/live/events.rs:397-410), which Sawit never enters (live collaboration is a spec non-goal; collab.join and collab.publish are hidden, crates/board/src/policy.rs:27-28); over a version preview FigCraft shows its bar too; in Sawit a preview is reached only by an agent's command op running version.preview (no menu item or key runs it, and file.versionHistory is hidden), it starts with nothing selected (engine version_preview_tests.rs:38), every edit made during it, a pick from the bar included, is refused as Disabled with FigCraft's reason (you're viewing an earlier version...) and rolled back (engine session/exec.rs:148-156), which choose shows as a toast (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:380), and the shown version is never saved (a_version_shown_in_place_of_the_document_is_not_saved, mod.rs:1675) — costs if wrong: a user who selects an item while an agent shows an earlier version sees the bar, and a pick shows that toast and changes nothing; hiding the bar while ds.preview is set is one more early return in render_context_bar (crates/app/src/board/context_bar.rs:96-104).
- Final review: parked — Inter drawn in the fallback face, and the R-EHFRAME linker warning: both known, carried to Plan 8's Task 1 and Task 4 (declined to judge) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: neither is Plan 3 code, and both are carried where they land: the Inter embed is the Plan 2 Task 7 delegate ruling's carry, in Plan 8's rulings.md (the embed in Plan 8 Task 1, NOTICE in Task 3, a size note and a hand check in Task 4), with Inter text in Helvetica Neue Regular KNOWN until it lands; the debug-link __eh_frame line is known in every task from Plan 1 Task 6 on under the amended R-EHFRAME (program ledger), and Plan 8 Task 4 records the debug app's __eh_frame size for the user's choice — costs if wrong: if Plan 8 drops the Inter carry, board text set in Inter keeps showing in the fallback face, without bold or italic, on a Mac without Inter; a dropped R-EHFRAME record leaves one known warning line in debug links, which slows only exception unwinding in debug builds.
- Final review: parked — P5.T4's own code in Plan 3's files (the Design toggle, the row layout, the on_key focus guard), judged only where it meets Plan 3's chrome; the rest is FINAL.P5's (declined to judge) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: that code had its own task review (spec compliant, quality Approved, 6 minors and three warnings ruled, complete at a77fa36), and Plan 5's final review judges it whole once Plan 5's last tasks land; the seams Plan 3 owns were judged and hold at main f91467b: the canvas column holds the toolbar, the context bar and the toast, so both bars measure the canvas, not the window; the Design toggle sits after the zoom menu (P3-C11) and reaches the panel.design arm through choose's drain; the guard's effect on the chrome is the review's Minors 1 and 2, and Minor 1 is fixed in hand check 1 (plan :425-429, f91467b); one note for FINAL.P5: the toggle's icon, Lucide PanelRight (crates/app/src/board/top_bar.rs:42), is in gpui-kit's default set (gpui-kit-assets 0.8.1 default-icons.txt:76), so it is drawn, but every_icon_of_the_chrome_is_embedded (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:4029) doesn't list top_bar::DESIGN_ICON; this line names P5.T4, so it reaches Plan 5's rulings.md — costs if wrong: a defect inside the Design panel's own code is Plan 5's finding, for its final review; without the icon line, a later swap to an icon outside the default set would draw a blank toggle with no test failing (one line in that test).
- Final review: parked — Re-running the gates: the reviewer didn't (a read-only review, main checked out nowhere it may build under R-ROOT, the disk tight) and relied on the recorded results: P3.T4's cargo test --workspace, 1136 passed, 0 failed, 9 ignored at lane commit b978572, its fix round's board:: 91 passed, and the integrators' re-runs before each fast-forward (declined to judge) — Ruling: (delegate) superseded since the review: main is now f91467b, the very commit the fix wave gated in lane 4 (the same SHA, fast-forwarded with no rebase), where cargo fmt --all -- --check passed, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings checked sawit-board, sawit-mcp and sawit with no warning, cargo test -p sawit-board passed 597 of 597 and cargo test -p sawit 177 of 177 (scratchpad/final-p3-fix: test-board2.log, test-app2.log, clippy2.log; the new test red against main's view, then green); the diff since the reviewed a77fa36 is crates/app/src/board/context_bar.rs, tests in crates/app/src/board/mod.rs, one const made pub in crates/board/src/context_bar.rs, and the plan — costs if wrong: sawit-core's and sawit-mcp's tests weren't re-run at f91467b, though the diff changes nothing they call; the next integration onto main runs the gates again (R-LANES).

## Plan 4 (layers)

- Ruling: P4-C1 — PageRow.divider = doc.is_divider(id) (an empty page whose name starts with '-'). Keep pages_list_and_dividers as written ("-" is empty); optionally assert that a non-empty '-x' page is not a divider. — why: The spec doesn't define dividers. The engine decides what can be opened (page.select refuses only is_divider pages), and FigCraft's own panel uses the same rule. — costs if wrong: A page whose name starts with '-' but has layers can't be opened from the panel, hiding its content.
- Ruling: P4-C2 — Pass the session's ds.page as `current` and expect `current` on "Two" (3 rows, one divider). — why: Matches engine behaviour (page.add opens a non-divider page) and still checks both the current flag and dividers. It also stops an implementer from bending pages() to mark the first page. — costs if wrong: A failing test or a wrong current marker in the pages list, and a wasted review cycle.
- Ruling: P4-C3 — Allow Into when the target is_container(), is not an Instance, is not a board atom (node.board.is_some_and(BoardItem::is_atom)) and is not inside an instance (figcraft_engine::components::structure_locked). For Into, Above and Below, return None when a dragged Section would land in a parent where !doc.takes_sections(..). Keep every listed test, and add drop_into_sticky_is_refused and section_into_frame_is_refused. — why: The panel then never offers a drop that the engine refuses or that hides the layer. It mirrors Document::reparent, the engine's instance checks and FigCraft's own panel. The spec only requires own-subtree drops to be refused, which still holds. — costs if wrong: A layer dropped onto a sticky vanishes from the panel; drops onto instances and misplaced sections toast engine errors; layers can't be dragged into sections.
- Ruling: P4-C4 — Polygon and Star map to Vector, BooleanOp to Group, ComponentSet to Component, a Rectangle with an image fill to Image, Line (arrow cap or not) to Line, Slice to Other. BoardItem::Label maps to Connector, with its children hidden like other board items. — why: Keeps the plan's enum unchanged, follows FigCraft's node_icon, and removes guesswork for the implementer and reviewer. — costs if wrong: Cosmetic: unexpected icons; low.
- Ruling: P4-C5 — Rewrite it as a row comparison: with G collapsed, rows(filter "c") lists G expanded, and rows(filter "") afterwards with the same set equals the pre-filter rows (G collapsed, C absent). In P4.T2, compute filter expansion per render and never write it into self.expanded; assert self.expanded is unchanged after setting and clearing layer_filter. — why: It tests Review Focus 3 ('clearing the filter restores the expand state') at the one place it can actually break. — costs if wrong: A reviewer flags a no-op test, or clearing the filter leaves every matched branch expanded.
- Ruling: P4-C6 — Keep the 20 ms budget, but build the 200-group fixture outside the timed region, time only rows() as the best of 3 runs, and also assert 201 rows with depths 0..=200 in order. Don't copy FigCraft's depth-64 recursion cap. Read Review Focus 5 as 201 rows (200 groups plus the leaf). — why: Keeps the plan's performance guard while removing scheduler noise and the 200-vs-201 inconsistency. — costs if wrong: An intermittent failure under parallel load blocks an integration, or a copied depth cap silently truncates deep trees.
- Ruling: P4-C7 — In P4.T2, make on_key (P2.T4) return early unless the canvas's own focus handle is_focused(window) (not contains_focused), so keys typed in the panel's Inputs never reach route_key. Add a GPUI test: with the filter input focused, typing "v" leaves the tool unchanged and the filter reads "v". Plan 5's NumberField inputs rely on the same guard. — why: One guard at the only routing site; P2.T4's bindings and its binding-resolution tests stay as they are. — costs if wrong: Rename and filter are unusable (shortcut letters switch tools and vanish), and the same bug reappears in every Design panel field.
- Ruling: P4-C8 — Render the layers panel only when show_layers && !ds.view.ui_hidden, leaving show_layers unchanged so the panel comes back with the UI; assert this in a P4.T2 test. — why: Spec D11 binds (⌘\ hides all the chrome); smallest change. — costs if wrong: ⌘\ leaves a 240 px panel on screen.
- Ruling: P4-C9 — P4.T2 handles layers.collapseAll as FigCraft does: clear `expanded`, then re-add reveal(selection). — why: It is a layers-panel request that P6 shows as an enabled Object menu item and the keymap binds to ⌥L. — costs if wrong: A visible menu item and shortcut that silently do nothing.
- Ruling: P4-C10 — P4.T2 treats panel.leftSidebar like panel.layers (toggle show_layers). — why: FigCraft's left sidebar is the pages-and-layers panel; this is one extra match arm. — costs if wrong: ⇧⌘\ does nothing on the canvas.
- Ruling: P4-C11 — On layers.rename: set show_layers = true, expand reveal(data.ids), start renaming the topmost listed id of data.ids (selecting it alone), and scroll it into view. With several ids, only that one is renamed. — why: Matches FigCraft's start_rename without the out-of-scope batch-rename dialog. — costs if wrong: Renaming from the keyboard or the menu looks broken on boards.
- Ruling: P4-C12 — Set ds.hover = None and notify when the pointer leaves the rows without entering another row. — why: The spec says hovering a row highlights the layer; leaving the row should end the highlight. — costs if wrong: A stale highlight stays on the canvas (cosmetic).
- Ruling: P4-C13 — Add crates/app/src/board/top_bar.rs to P4.T2's files (its `git add crates/app/src/board` already covers it). Place the toggle in the spec's top-bar order: after the zoom menu, before Maximize. — why: Lets the scheduler see the overlap with P5.T4 and P6.T2, which also edit top_bar.rs. — costs if wrong: Two lanes edit top_bar.rs at once and the rebase conflicts.
- Ruling: P4-C14 — P4.T2 lays out [layers panel | canvas container]. The canvas container is `relative` and holds the canvas element, the toolbar and the context bar. Canvas mouse, scroll and pinch handlers stay on the canvas element. P4.T3 screenshots a selection to confirm the context bar sits above it. — why: Keeps the canvas-local coordinates of P2 and P3 valid without changing their models. — costs if wrong: The context bar floats 240 px off its selection, the toolbar is off-centre, and scrolling the layers list pans the canvas.
- Ruling: P4-C15 — In P4.T3 Step 2, follow select.all with {"op":"command","command":"select.children"} and expect the 4 frames expanded with their child rows highlighted. Step 3 then hides those children. — why: Makes reveal observable with the same fixture and no new tooling. — costs if wrong: The live check passes without ever exercising reveal.
- Ruling: P4-C16 — After ops::run in run_op, drain session.ui_requests through the same view dispatcher that keys and pointer events use. P4.T2 adds this if no earlier task has, with a test that a view.layers command op toggles show_layers. — why: Gives every input source one dispatch path. The spec drives live checks through the control socket, and P6.T4 expects {command: app.actions} to open the palette. — costs if wrong: Socket-driven panel toggles don't work, and stale requests fire on the next keystroke.
- Ruling: (delegate, P4.T1) ⚠️ P4.T2 filter mode: rows() reports expanded/has_children for the filtered list (layers.rs:152-157) and the restoring half of filter_restores_expansion (:692-694) holds by construction → dismiss: no P4.T1 gap; P4.T2 review confirms (a) the P4-C5 assertion that self.expanded is unchanged after setting and clearing layer_filter, filter expansion being computed per render and never stored; (b) while layer_filter.trim() is non-empty (the same test rows() applies, :130) an expand-arrow click leaves self.expanded alone (arrows inert or not drawn); (c) an empty rows() under a non-blank filter renders an empty-state line (FigCraft: No matching layers), never the previous tree — rows() takes &HashSet and decides filter expansion from the found set alone (:120-161); the test has the shape P4-C5 mandated and its first half asserts behaviour (G open while the set holds only F, :689-691); filter_without_matches_is_empty asserts the empty answer after a positive control (:670-676); the arrow and the empty state are view code, and FigCraft sidesteps the arrow by listing matches flat with has_kids false (ui-egui panels/layers.rs:542-550; empty hint :1075) — costs if wrong: an arrow click under a filter flips self.expanded unseen, so clearing the filter does not restore the tree (Review Focus 3), or a no-match filter shows a blank or stale list; both surface only in P4.T2 review
- Ruling: (delegate, P4.T1) ⚠️ P4.T2 rename: LayerRow.name is the kind label for a blank-named layer (layers.rs:308-314), not the stored name → dismiss: no P4.T1 gap; P4.T2 starts the rename Input from doc.node(id).name (empty for a nameless layer), and rename_layer runs no command when the name is blank or equals the stored name; P4.T2 review checks blank_rename_keeps_name plus an untouched commit on a nameless layer (name stays empty, undo depth unchanged) — the field doc (:63) says the row shows the name or the kind, as FigCraft paints rows (panels/layers.rs:1144); FigCraft seeds its rename field from the stored n.name (:1136) and commits only text that differs from it and is not blank (widgets.rs:1183); the engine object.rename writes any name through doc_mut, so even an unchanged name records an undo step (engine cmd/edit.rs:254-265) — costs if wrong: an untouched Enter renames a nameless layer to its kind label (Frame) and adds an undo step; caught in P4.T2 review
- Ruling: (delegate, P4.T1) ⚠️ P4.T2 engine refusals: a dragged layer inside an instance and a drop that changes nothing are left to the engine (layers.rs:221-223; Minor 1) → fix: the no-op drop is handled in P4.T1 — drop_target returns None when every dragged layer would stay where it is (fix-loop finding; the existing Some checks at :1153 and :1224 are such drops and move to Below); a dragged layer inside an instance stays the engine’s to refuse, and P4.T2 review checks that drop_rows runs node.reparent through the one execute→toast path (BoardView::choose, Plan 3 C13), with a test: a layer dragged from inside an instance onto a free frame sets the toast and leaves children, selection and undo depth unchanged — the engine refuses that move on the old parent (engine guard.rs:67-82) and rolls document, selection and page back (session/exec.rs:84, :111-121), and FigCraft’s own panel leaves it to the engine, whose message says why (a deliberate exception to P4-C3’s aim of never offering a refused drop: a missing drop line would explain nothing); but nothing guards a no-op reparent: node.reparent calls doc_mut (cmd/edit/props.rs:249), so after_command records an undo step (exec.rs:225; implementer probe: depth 0 to 1), and Document::reparent re-derives each moved layer’s transform as parent_world.inverse() * world (doc lib.rs:646), which rounding can change; the order arithmetic already lives in layers.rs and is tested against the real engine, so the check belongs there rather than copied into GPUI code, the view then draws a drop line only where a drop moves something, and P4.T2 starts around slot 24 (dag.md), so the fix loop costs no critical path — costs if wrong: no drop line in the gaps beside a dragged row (cosmetic); left unfixed, a sloppy drag adds an invisible undo step that the next ⌘Z spends
- Ruling: (delegate, P4.T2) ⚠️ look and feel (drop marks and the drag ghost, the rename field in a 28 px row, the page context menu, divider lines, the canvas hover outline) belongs to P4.T3 (R-LIVE) → dismiss: no P4.T2 gap; the code draws each piece: the row is a fixed 28 px (layers_panel.rs:60, :1001) and the rename field an xsmall Input, h_5 = 20 px at a 16 px rem (gpui-component 0.8.1 sizing.rs input_h; :1185); a drop mark (a 2 px line, or a theme.drag_border frame for Into) only where drop_target lands (:816-853, :891), the ghost DragLabel (:204), the page menu (:1137, :1190-1212), a divider as a 1 px line (:1112), a row hover setting ds.hover (:1014); CARRY to P4.T3, two socket screenshots and worded hand checks: (a) after Step 3 send {"op":"command","command":"select.set","params":{"ids":[<one frame id from get>]}} then {"op":"command","command":"object.rename","params":{}} (a nameless rename is the UI request layers.rename, engine cmd/edit.rs:254-259, which run_op hands to start_rename, mod.rs:502, layers_panel.rs:497): the screenshot shows that row holding a text field with the name selected, inside its 28 px row, the rows around it unmoved; then {"op":"command","command":"view.layers"} twice: the field is gone and get shows the name unchanged (its blur keeps an untouched name and runs no command); (b) {"op":"command","command":"page.add","params":{"name":"-"}} (an empty page named "-" is a divider and the canvas stays on its page, engine panels_behaviour_tests.rs:346-351): the screenshot shows a 1 px line under App in the pages list; then page.delete with the id it answered; (c) under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)": drag a row: a label with its name follows the pointer, a 2 px line marks the gap above or below the row under it, a row that takes layers is framed while the pointer is in its middle half, no mark shows over the dragged row itself or its own children and a release there changes nothing; right-click a page: Rename page, Duplicate page, Delete page, and Delete page on the only page shows a toast and keeps the page; hover as the P2.T3 carry in Plan 4 rulings.md words it (Move tool, the row of an unselected layer hovered: a 1.5 px blue outline on the canvas, none extra on a selected layer) — why: pixels and pointer feel are not in a diff; R-LIVE gives P4.T3 screenshots of what socket ops can produce, so (a) and (b) need no hands and the user is needed only for drag, the context menu and hover — costs if wrong: a misdrawn mark, ghost or menu shows only at the user's hand check after the run, then a small view fix; (a) and (b) add six socket ops and two screenshots to P4.T3
- Ruling: (delegate, P4.T2) ⚠️ P4-C14's other half: with the layers panel shown, the context bar sits over its selection (a P4.T3 screenshot) → dismiss: no P4.T2 gap; the panel moves the whole canvas container and the bar and toolbar are placed inside it: the bar's anchor is canvas-local (sawit_board::context_bar anchor() through ds.view.page_to_screen, crates/board/src/context_bar.rs:280-293), clamped to (ds.view.vw, vh), which prepaint sets from the canvas element's own bounds (mod.rs:851), and drawn absolute at that place (app context_bar.rs:108-123, :158-160) as a child of the relative #board-canvas (mod.rs:1067-1068, :1101), whose origin is x = 240 with the panel shown (asserted by the_layers_panel_goes_with_the_ui_and_comes_back); the toolbar is absolute left_0/right_0 + justify_center in the same container (toolbar.rs:214-219), and scroll and pinch listen on #board-canvas only (mod.rs:1074-1075); CARRY to P4.T3: the bar is drawn only on boards (context_bar() returns None unless is_board, crates/board/src/context_bar.rs:302-306), so it never shows on ridgeline-app.figcraft and the C14 screenshot is taken on a board: before Step 1 copies ridgeline-app over .nyawit/canvas.figcraft (or in a launch with a fresh $S/proj), open_page canvas, place and select a sticky as Plan 3 Task 4 Step 2 did (tool.sticky, pointer down and up, key Escape, select.all; the ops are in its notes), send {"op":"command","command":"view.layers"}, wait 0.5 s, then screenshot: the 240 pt panel at the left, the bar's middle over the sticky's middle with its bottom 12 pt above the sticky's top, and the toolbar centred on the canvas (240 + canvas width / 2), not on the window; hand check: with the frames opened in Step 2 so the list scrolls, a two-finger scroll over the layers list scrolls the list and never pans the canvas — why: P4-C14 gives this half to P4.T3 and the code holds it by construction; without the carry P4.T3 would look for a bar on a design file, where the model never makes one — costs if wrong: on a board with the panel shown the bar floats 240 px off its sticky; that screenshot finds it and P4.T3 fixes it in render_canvas
- Ruling: (delegate, P4.T2) ⚠️ integration: main moved past the base cdc216e (89b6196 P5.T5, b42ec77): the assets.rs icon list, the design_panel/mod.rs tests, mod.rs and events.rs, and P5.T5's tests, which never ran beside the layers panel (a 396 px canvas in their 900 px window) → dismiss: nothing for the fix loop;
