- Final review: parked — Whether the gates pass at main HEAD (declined to judge: not re-run; the review is read-only, R-ROOT keeps it out of the main checkout, the lanes are other agents', 11 GB free; it relied on P4.T3's workspace run at ef8c209, 1245 passed, 0 failed, and the integrators' gate runs) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: P4.T3's full cargo test --workspace at ef8c209 ran with all of Plan 4 in it (5b03e4e, c16fee2 and 90d0a5f are ancestors of ef8c209), which is the spec's end-of-plan gate (Testing: each plan ends with cargo test --workspace); since then layers.rs, layers_panel.rs and top_bar.rs are unchanged on main (git log ef8c209..a11fcd8 on them is empty), main HEAD a11fcd8 differs from df633b9 only in README.md, NOTICE and .claude/commands/consolidate.md, and the one code commit after ef8c209 (8e63845, the canvas menus' import, save as, export and copy as, which also edits board/mod.rs) passed its integrator's fmt, clippy -D warnings and the sawit and sawit-board tests, the two crates that hold every Plan 4 test; a whole-workspace run on the merged main is step 3 of the program ledger's FINISH STEPS anyway — costs if wrong: a test that 8e63845 broke in a crate its integration did not re-test (sawit-mcp, sawit-core) shows only at that FINISH STEPS gate run, before anything merges; nothing is pushed in between.
- Final review: parked — Pixels and pointer feel: the drop marks, the drag label, the hover tint, the list's scroll feel, a two-finger scroll over the list versus a canvas pan (declined to judge: R-LIVE hand checks 1-3, 6 and 10) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly under R-LIVE: each is on main under Hand checks (pending — needs the user) in docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-native-canvas-4-layers.md (:343-451) with exact steps and what to look for, and R-LIVE has such checks wait for the user without failing the task; what the socket could reach passed in P4.T3 (the rename field inside its row, the divider line, the context bar and toolbar centred on the canvas beside the panel, the scroll to a revealed row, the 205-row chain), and the scroll split holds by construction (P4-C14: scroll and pinch listen on the canvas element only) — costs if wrong: a misdrawn mark or a list scroll that pans the canvas shows only at the user's hand check after the run, then a small view fix in layers_panel.rs.
- Final review: parked — A drop Below an expanded container: the mark shows right under the container's row, but the layers land below its whole subtree, as its sibling (declined to judge: FigCraft's drop_zone and drop_layers do exactly this; the spec is silent) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: Sawit's drop_pos and drop_target (layers_panel.rs:251-259; layers.rs:255-286) are FigCraft's (ui-egui panels/layers.rs:765-795 draws the insertion line at the row's bottom edge from the row's own indent, :875-893 reparents into the target's parent at the gap below it), the model P4-C3 follows; the line starts at the container's own indent, so it marks the container's level, not its children's; the spec asks only that drags reorder and reparent through node.reparent; the top of an open container stays one drop away (Into its row puts the layers on top, layers.rs:276), a drop that lands elsewhere is one ⌘Z, and no later plan builds on where a drop lands — costs if wrong: a user aiming at the gap under an open frame's row gets the layer after the frame's subtree instead of first inside it; a follow-up maps Below on an open container with children to Into (a few lines in drop_target and one test).
- Final review: parked — FigCraft extras that neither the plan nor the spec lists: holding over a collapsed container during a drag to open it (hold_opens, 0.6 s), a drop zone below the last row, a context menu on layer rows, arrow-key row navigation, the eye and padlock sweep (declined to judge) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the spec's Layers panel section and Plan 4's goal list what was built and none of these, and what they would add stays reachable: a drop Into a collapsed container (it lands on top) or opening it first stands in for hold-to-open, Below on the last top-level row reaches the bottom of the page, and the canvas context menu (spec, Context menu) with each row's eye, padlock and double-click rename covers the row actions; Plans 5 to 8 on main build on none of them — costs if wrong: a FigCraft habit (hold to open, sweep the eyes, arrow through rows, right-click a row) does nothing in Sawit until a follow-up adds it, each a separate change in layers_panel.rs.
- Final review: parked — An agent's nameless object.rename (or canvas.renameLabel) shows the panel and moves the keyboard into the rename field even while the user types elsewhere in Sawit (declined to judge: P4-C16 gives agent ops the UI-request path on purpose; whether agents should trigger UI requests at all is the agent contract's call, Plan 7) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: it is the command's documented contract, not a slip: the engine's params doc for object.rename reads {ids?, name?} (without a name: rename the layer inline in the layers panel) (engine cmd/edit.rs:254-258), which the commands op hands agents as each command's params (crates/board/src/ops.rs:555-562), and a misspelled name can't fall into it, since execute refuses unknown parameters first (engine cmd.rs:152-162); P4-C16 and the spec's Testing section (the pointer, key and type ops run the same code path as GPUI's events) make an op act as input, and the live checks rely on it (P4.T3 opened the field and toggled the panel this way); nothing is lost: Escape drops the field, a blank keeps the name, a typed name is one ⌘Z; whether an agent op may open UI that takes the keyboard at all (this field, the Actions palette, the context menu) is one policy question for the controller at Plan 7's final review, not a Plan 4 gap — costs if wrong: after the cutover, an agent's nameless rename, or a pointer-op double-click on a frame's name, while the user types in a terminal pane of the same window takes their next keys into the field, and Enter or a click elsewhere names the layer with them (one ⌘Z; the terminal misses those keys); a follow-up lets run_op start the field without the keyboard unless the canvas had it (a flag through start_rename, a few lines and one test).
- Final review: parked — Deep rows run off the 240 pt panel from about depth 11 (declined to judge: P4.T3 recorded it under Seen, not defects and asked the user; FigCraft has no indent cap either) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: Plan 4 Task 2 fixes the indent at depth × 12 px in a 240 px panel, and the spec asks only for a virtualized list, which holds (P4.T3 built a 200-deep chain, scrolled to its leaf and selected it); a deep row stays clickable and selectable, and FigCraft indents 4 + 16 px a level with no cap; the question to the user sits in the plan notes (Seen, not defects: say if you want it), not in the hand-check list the controller collects, so the controller should put it to the user with the hand checks — costs if wrong: from about depth 11 names are cut and from about depth 19 rows are blank until a follow-up caps the indent or adds a horizontal scroll (a few lines in the row render).
- Final review: Ruling: (delegate) The eye, padlock, drop and page actions run while a canvas text edit is open without ending it; only selecting a row ends it (declined to judge: FigCraft's panel does the same, and the engine's execute handles commands mid-edit) — load-bearing for the page actions, which can silently lose typed text, so the premise fails there: a canvas text edit is an engine interaction (textedit::begin opens Edit text, engine canvas/textedit.rs:94), and page.select, and page.delete of the open page through settle, clear ds.text_edit without ending that interaction (engine cmd/page.rs:79, :21); it stays open holding the document from before the typing, the next gesture's begin_interaction reuses it (session/undo.rs:58), and a press on a layer with the Move tool cancels it, putting that document back, when the press doesn't drag (canvas/tools/draw.rs:159; session/undo.rs:104-112), or previews from it when it does (undo.rs:65-88), while move_down ends a text edit only while ds.text_edit is set (canvas/tools/drag.rs:16-22): type in a text, click another page's row (click_page, layers_panel.rs:776-790, then page_action :367-391, which runs choose and no text.edit.end), click a layer there, and the typed text is gone, the autosave writes that, and no undo step holds it; FigCraft has the same gap (its pages list runs page.select the same way, ui-egui panels/layers.rs:365), so following it is no defence, and the spec's never-lose-data intent binds (D5, as the controller's P7-X3 reads it); the eye, padlock and drop parts only fold into the text's one undo step (no command records a step while an interaction is open, session/exec.rs:225; none at all when the edit was on a new text left empty, textedit.rs:136-149) — smallest change: page_action ends an open text edit before its command, in one run closure, as select_layers does (layers_panel.rs:451-461: if ds.text_edit is set, execute text.edit.end, then the command), best as one helper that toggle_visible, toggle_locked and drop_rows call too, so each panel action is its own undo step; GPUI test: two pages, a text on the first being edited with a character typed, page_action Select of the second leaves ds.interaction None and the character in the text, and a later Move-tool click on a layer of the second page keeps it; the command op reaches the same engine gap (an agent's page.select, or page.delete of the open page, during the user's text edit), for the controller to route to Plan 7's final review: run_op ending an open text edit before just those command ids, as it ends a colour scrub (mod.rs:414-416) — costs if wrong: a few lines and one test in layers_panel.rs, and picking a page ends the text edit as picking a row already does; left as is, after the cutover the panel can drop a user's typed text without a trace.
- Final review: parked — show_layers is neither kept across a reopen nor worked out again when a document changes kind, board to design (declined to judge: D11 covers only how a file opens) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: D11 says how a file opens (boards with both panels hidden, design files with both shown), which BoardView::new does from the file's kind (mod.rs:329-367 sets show_layers and show_design from design_file), and keeping the flag across a reopen would break that rule at the next open; the Design panel's show_design behaves the same, so the two panels stay consistent; a kind change inside an open tab (a replace that loads the other kind) is not an open, settle_chrome already closes what belonged to the old kind (mod.rs:463-479), and ⌥1, ⌥8 and the top bar's toggles show either panel — costs if wrong: after such a replace both panels keep the old kind's state until toggled; a follow-up re-derives both flags in settle_chrome when shown_kind changes (a few lines for both panels at once).
- Ruling: (controller) FINAL.P4's load-bearing residual (page actions lose an open text edit) → routed to FINAL.P7's fix wave; see Plan 7 ledger.

## Plan 5 (design-panel)

- Ruling: P5-C1 — P5.T1's DesignSelection::read builds each node's map from node_json, then flattens it. layout becomes layout (the mode string), gap, crossGap, padding plus paddingTop/Right/Bottom/Left, mainAlign, crossAlign and wrap. sizing becomes sizingH and sizingV. cornerRadius (a number or an array) becomes topLeftRadius, topRightRadius, bottomRightRadius and bottomLeftRadius. read also adds constrainProportions (Node.constrain_proportions) and textAlignVertical (TextData.align_v) from the Document. T1 adds one test that sets these keys with node.set and reads them back. — why: The plan promises one key space for reads and writes. That holds only with this flattening, and T3's add_layout_then_gap plus T4/T5's layout, sizing, constrain and vertical-align fields depend on it. T1 owns read(). — costs if wrong: If T3 does the flattening instead, it costs one more edit of design/mod.rs in T3. No API changes.
- Ruling: P5-C2 — DesignSelection::set sends a text key (characters, fontFamily, fontStyle, fontSize, lineHeight, letterSpacing, textAlign, textAlignVertical, textAutoResize) only to the selected TEXT layers. get compares only the layers whose map holds the key, and returns Absent when none do. T3 adds a test: with a text and a rectangle selected, set("fontSize", 18.0) runs Ok and the rectangle is unchanged. — why: The spec says an edit applies to every selected layer. T1 shows Typography when any layer is text, and node.set skips only auto layout and path keys (edit/props.rs:136). — costs if wrong: Typography edits on mixed selections show an error toast and change nothing.
- Ruling: P5-C3 — Same ruling as Plan 4 C7: BoardView::on_key (P2.T4) returns early unless the canvas's own focus handle is_focused(window). Whichever of P4.T2 and P5.T4 lands first adds it. P5.T4 adds a GPUI test: with a NumberField input focused, typing "r" leaves the tool unchanged and the field reads "r". Escape during a label scrub calls scrub_end(false) before any routing. — why: One guard at the only routing site leaves P2.T4's bindings and tests as they are, and the spec's Design panel needs typed input (W, hex, font search). — costs if wrong: Every Design panel field is unusable: shortcut letters switch tools and the text never reaches the input.
- Ruling: P5-C4 — P5.T2 adds EffectKind::Other(String), holding the effect's type tag. effects() returns every effect in order. P5.T5 shows Other rows read-only, with eye and remove only. set_effect and remove_effect re-send every other row's JSON unchanged. T2 adds a test: a layer with a glass (or noise) effect beside a drop shadow; editing the shadow leaves the glass identical. — why: FigCraft has 7 effect kinds and the pinned examples use glass. The plan's own rule is that rows not being edited are re-sent unchanged, the same principle as Review Focus 4. — costs if wrong: Editing any effect on such a layer deletes or corrupts its glass, noise or texture effect, or the section errors.
- Ruling: P5-C5 — DEFAULTS adds fills, strokes, effects and exports as []. blend is read from Node.blend for every node instead of defaulting to "normal". paints(), effects() and export_rows() read an empty list as an empty list, not as mixed. — why: node_json leaves out empty fills, strokes, effects and exports, and it leaves out both normal and passThrough blends, while containers default to passThrough (node.rs:675). — costs if wrong: Empty Stroke and Effects sections show "Mixed", and picking Normal on a frame silently changes how it composites.
- Ruling: P5-C6 — read() stores every JSON number as f64, DEFAULTS included. Mixed derives Debug and PartialEq. Tests compare against f64 literals: json!(100.0), json!(1.0), json!(12.0) and json!(18.0). — why: node_json emits f64 values, and serde_json's Number equality is strict about the variant, so json!(100) != json!(100.0). — costs if wrong: The T1 and T3 tests fail as written, and an integer default reads as Mixed against an equal float.
- Ruling: P5-C7 — Keep T1's parse_number. P5.T4's opacity field shows NN% and appends '%' to input that lacks one before calling parse_number, so "50" becomes 0.5. Its label scrub moves 1% per px. — why: T1's test pins ("opacity","3") -> 1.0, and T4's Appearance row shows opacity in %. — costs if wrong: Typing 50 into the % field sets 100%.
- Ruling: P5-C8 — P5.T5's line height field accepts auto, a finite number or a finite N%. Letter spacing accepts a finite number or N%. The value goes through set() as a JSON number or string. Anything else is rejected and the field shows the current value again. Font size stays a NumberField. — why: node_json reports LineHeight::label() strings, node.set takes auto|px|"150%" and px|"2%", and parse_number returns f64 and takes % only for opacity. — costs if wrong: Line height can't be set to Auto or a percentage, and the field rejects valid input.
- Ruling: P5-C9 — The test calls figcraft_text::scan_system_fonts() first and asserts !families().is_empty(). Then it checks case-insensitive order (the order family_names uses) and no duplicates. — why: CRAFT_FONTS is empty unless FigCraft is built with CRAFT_FONTS_DIR, so family_names() is [] headless and the test asserts nothing. — costs if wrong: A review flags a vacuous test.
- Ruling: P5-C10 — Same line as Plan 2 C12: P5.T3 adds base64 = "0.22" to crates/board/Cargo.toml unless P2.T6 already has, and stages crates/board/Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock. — why: file.export without a path returns base64 data (cmd/file/export.rs), and base64 0.22.1 is already in Cargo.lock. — costs if wrong: T3 doesn't compile, or two base64 versions end up in sawit-board.
- Ruling: P5-C11 — P5.T4 and P5.T5 add to ExtraIcons in crates/app/src/assets.rs every Lucide icon they use that isn't in default-icons.txt or ExtraIcons already: align and distribute, link/unlink, blend, text-align, arrow-up-to-line/arrow-down-to-line and so on. Each lists the file and stages it with git add. — why: IconName names every bundled Lucide icon, but Sawit serves only the default set plus ExtraIcons, and any other icon renders blank. Same ruling as Plan 3 C9. — costs if wrong: Blank buttons in the Position, Auto layout and Typography rows.
- Ruling: P5-C12 — P5.T5 adds BoardView::export_selection(&mut self, cx) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, BoardError>. For each selected layer and export row it calls export_bytes, then nyawit::ensure plus nyawit::unique(exports_dir(project), "<layer name>[@Nx].<ext>"), and writes the file. The project is the parent of the .nyawit folder that holds the board file; otherwise exports go to <AppPaths root>/exports. It returns the paths, and only the Export button's handler runs open -R on the first one. The test checks the returned .png path. — why: T5's Produces omits the method, BoardView knows only its file path (P1.T5), and a unit test must not launch Finder. — costs if wrong: cargo test opens Finder windows, and exports from file tabs land in an unexpected folder.
- Ruling: P5-C13 — P6.T3's file.export calls P5.T5's export_selection, which falls back to the page's top-level layers when nothing is selected, instead of re-implementing the export folder, naming and reveal. — why: Both write the selection's exports to .nyawit/exports and reveal the first file, and a review flags the duplicated block. — costs if wrong: Two export paths drift apart in naming, folder and reveal.
- Ruling: P5-C14 — Keep the plan: begin_interaction, then execute node.set on each drag step, then commit_interaction or cancel_interaction, as ui-egui's field_run does. Run the three Session calls through figcraft_engine::guard::run (D15). End a scrub on a window-level mouse-up, using P2.T2's drag pattern. — why: Session::preview takes a closure over a Document, not a command, and execute records no undo step while an interaction is open (exec.rs:225), so the scrub stays one undo step as the spec intends. — costs if wrong: If a scrub is never closed, every later command joins it and its undo step is lost.
- Ruling: P5-C15 — P5.T5 scrubs a colour with scrub_begin("fills", "strokes" or "effects"), then apply_edit(set_solid(..) or set_effect(..)) for each change, then scrub_end(true) when the picker closes or scrub_end(false) on Escape. No new API. — why: apply_edit inside an open interaction adds no undo step of its own (exec.rs:225), and scrub_to only builds node.set {key: f64}. — costs if wrong: Each colour change becomes its own undo step, and color_pick_is_one_undo_step fails.
- Ruling: P5-C16 — Keep header() as the kind or the count. P5.T4's header row shows header() and, when one layer is selected, its name from get("name"). — why: The spec's Design panel section 1 is the kind and name, with a count when several layers are selected. — costs if wrong: The header shows only "Rectangle".
- Ruling: P5-C17 — P5.T4's Appearance row adds an eye toggle that sets visible on the selection. A mixed value shows the mixed state. — why: The spec's Design panel section 4 lists visibility. — costs if wrong: A spec item is missing, and the final review flags it.
- Ruling: P5-C18 — Render the Design panel only when show_design && !ds.view.ui_hidden, and leave show_design unchanged so the panel comes back with the UI (as Plan 4 C8). Assert this in a T4 test. — why: Spec D11: ⌘\ hides all the chrome. P3.T3: while ui_hidden is true, only the canvas renders. — costs if wrong: ⌘\ leaves the Design panel on screen.
- Ruling: P5-C19 — Same ruling as Plan 3 C11: P5.T4 adds its toggle in crates/app/src/board/top_bar.rs, lists and stages the file, and depends on P3.T3. — why: The top bar is P3.T3's file. — costs if wrong: None beyond a scheduling edge and the Files list.
- Ruling: P5-C20 — Same ruling as Plan 3 C13: apply_edit calls choose(edit.command, edit.params, cx). — why: The app keeps one execute, toast-on-error, notify path. — costs if wrong: A review flags the duplicated block.
- Ruling: P5-C21 — P1.T5 names the field toast: Option<..> and makes it readable from sibling board modules (pub(super) or pub(crate)). P5.T4's apply_edit_error_sets_toast reads it. — why: P1.T5 only says "save-error toast", and tests in P2, P4, P5 and P6 all read toast. — costs if wrong: A rename across those tests.
- Ruling: P5-C22 — parse_number rejects negative values for width, height, fontSize, gap, crossGap, strokeWeight and cornerRadius, and for every key that starts with padding or ends with Radius. Add ("paddingTop","-5") -> None to parse_rejects_bad_numbers. — why: T4's per-side padding and per-corner radius fields use paddingTop… and topLeftRadius…. The engine clamps negatives to 0 instead of refusing them, so the spec's "invalid input is rejected" doesn't hold there. — costs if wrong: -5 typed into one padding side silently becomes 0.
- Ruling: P5-C23 — Add crates/board/src/design/mod.rs to T2's and T3's Files (the `pub mod` lines, and T3's run fold in read), and crates/app/src/board/design_panel/mod.rs to T5's (its mod lines and sections). Their directory-level git add lines already cover these files. — why: A new module needs its mod line, and the scheduler's file-overlap check needs the real file lists. — costs if wrong: The scheduler misses the overlap: T2 and T3 conflict on design/mod.rs in parallel lanes, though the merge is trivial.
- Ruling: P5-C24 — Read the plan's "key list is cmd::props::KEYS" as documentation. No code iterates it, and tests use literal keys. — why: At rev 0971d09, props.rs:19 declares pub const KEYS: &str. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P5-C25 — P5.T6 selects the card with {"op":"command","command":"select.byName","params":{"query":"Trail card"}} (or gets its id from node.find {name}, then select.set). — why: get lists only the page's top-level layers. In ridgeline-app.figcraft those are 4 frames, and the only card is "Trail card". — costs if wrong: The check selects a screen frame instead of a card. The same sections still show.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T1) ⚠️ empty selection: sections() is [] and header() is "" with nothing selected, though the brief says Position, Appearance, Effects and Export are "always shown" (design/mod.rs:144-170; pinned by nothing_selected_shows_nothing) → dismiss: the code stays; "always shown" means for any selection, whatever the layer kinds, and the no-selection state is P5.T4's to render (a short empty-state line, no fields) — the spec describes the Design panel only for selected layers (section 1 "kind and name, with a count when several layers are selected"; section 9 "the layer's export settings"), and with nothing selected a field could only send node.set {ids: []}, which the engine refuses (node.set → req_targets, cmd/edit/props.rs:96 → Disabled "nothing is selected", automation/params.rs:160), so those four sections would offer edits that can only toast; T4 has not started and takes sections() as a list, so an empty list renders no section rather than breaking anything — costs if wrong: a design file opens with the Design panel shown (spec D11) and usually nothing selected, so it shows a blank panel until T4 adds an empty-state line (a few lines in design_panel/mod.rs). This ledger line does not reach T4 (its prompt reads rulings.md), so the controller can add it there or leave it to the Plan 5 final review.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T1) ⚠️ auto layout frame + plain frame selected: get counts only the frames that already have auto layout (design/mod.rs:126-136), so layout, gap, padding, mainAlign, crossAlign and wrap read Same, while node.set writes those keys to every frame-like layer and creates an auto layout where there is none (FigCraft cmd/props.rs:316 ensure_layout, 361 layout_mut, 640-669 apply_layout; cmd/edit/props.rs:132-141 skips only non-frame-like layers) → fix: for the keys fields::flatten_layout lifts (layout, gap, crossGap, padding, paddingTop/Right/Bottom/Left, mainAlign, crossAlign, wrap), get counts every frame-like layer and a frame without auto layout differs from one with it (Mixed); Absent when no frame has one; every other key keeps the P5-C2 rule; plus a doc line on get and set and one test — why: the spec says mixed values show as "Mixed". A throwaway probe on the real engine at 4fc6202 (git archive in the scratchpad, nothing committed) showed the gap. With a vertical auto layout frame (gap 12) and a plain frame, get gives Same("vertical") and Same(12.0). Re-committing the shown 12 gave the plain frame a horizontal auto layout and moved its children from (20,20)/(120,20) to (0,0)/(42,0). Re-sending the direction shown as active did the same with a vertical layout. P5-C2 was written for text keys, where set narrows its ids to the layers get counts; for layout keys set does not narrow, so its literal rule breaks the spec at this edge. T4 lives in crates/app and cannot see per-layer state. A dismissal here would reach neither T3 nor T4 (their prompts read rulings.md, not this ledger). Single-frame reads are unchanged (probe: [F1], [F2], [F1,R], [F2,R] read as before), so the T1 and T3 tests as planned still hold. CARRY to P5.T3 (controller: add to rulings.md): layout.add on ids that include a frame with auto layout wraps them all in a new frame (structure.rs:611-624; probe: layout.add [F1,F2] reparented both into a new frame), so add_layout must name only the frames without one; align_cell must handle a Mixed layout direction. sizingH/sizingV stay as they are: a wrong pick there is refused with a toast ("Hug needs auto layout or text"), not applied silently — costs if wrong: one more fix round on T1 (about 20 lines and a test) before T2 and T3 can start. If the controller would rather route layout keys only to the frames that have auto layout (the text-key pattern), get and set change together, and the probe cases are the test.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T2) ⚠️ T5 must check shadow input before set_effect: set_effect returns an Edit, and a row with a bad hex or a non-finite number comes back as lists::refused() (lists.rs:72, effects.rs:204-239), which P5-C20's apply_edit would show as a toast reading "nothing is selected (pass ids)" (engine automation/params.rs:160) instead of showing the current value again → dismiss: T2 stays as written; the requirement is P5.T5's, and P5.T5's review checks it. CARRY to P5.T5 (controller: add to rulings.md; T5's prompt reads rulings.md, not this ledger): every typed effect field is checked before an EffectRow is built, and on a failed check nothing is sent and the field shows the current value again. Hex: design::paints::parse_hex (paints.rs:137). X, y and spread: parse_number (finite; negatives are valid). Blur (a shadow's or a blur's radius): parse_number, and refused when negative, because node.set's effects arm is plain serde (engine cmd/props.rs:72, 612) and stores and reads back a negative radius as typed. Opacity: a % field as in P5-C7 (parse_number("opacity", ..) keeps it in 0..1). A None from set_solid (bad hex, non-finite opacity, no such row, mixed list, non-solid row; paints.rs:67-86) shows the paint row's hex and opacity again. Colours from the picker and values from label scrubs are valid by construction. — why: the plan gives set_effect -> Edit (plan line 139) beside set_solid -> Option<Edit> (line 130), so it puts the typed-input check in the view; T2 conforms, makes parse_hex public for this check, and documents the rule (effects.rs:145); a refused edit changes nothing (effects.rs:773 checks revision, document and undo depth), so a miss in T5 costs a misleading toast and a stale field, never the document; no design_panel code exists on main or on any lane yet, so this is a prompt line plus a review check, not a change to T2 — costs if wrong: if T5 sends unchecked rows, a bad shadow hex shows "nothing is selected (pass ids)" and keeps the typed text, and a negative blur is stored and shown as typed; the remedy is a few lines in T5's effects view, or set_effect returning Option<Edit> (effects.rs and its tests only).
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T2) ⚠️ T4/T5 must write fills, strokes and effects only through design::paints and design::effects (report Concern 1: sel.set("fills", sel.get("fills")) is refused for any image or pattern row) → dismiss: no change to T2, whose functions are the safe write path. CARRY to P5.T5, and to P5.T4 for completeness (controller: one line in rulings.md): fills, strokes and effects change only through paints::{add_paint, set_solid, set_visible, remove_paint, replace_with_solid} and effects::{add_effect, set_effect, remove_effect}, never by handing get() of a list key back to set(); strokeWeight, strokeAlign and dashes go through get and set as planned. — why: read() holds every number as f64 (P5-C6), and the engine refuses floats in ImagePaint.rotation (i32) and PatternPaint.source (u64) (figcraft-color lib.rs:465, 556; engine cmd/props.rs:532); lists::rows makes whole numbers integers before any re-send (lists.rs:20-26, 84-102), pinned against the engine by paints.rs:457; git grep over main and lanes 1-5 finds no write of a list key outside design/paints.rs, effects.rs and lists.rs (T1's tests only read fills); T4's rows (Position, Appearance with P5-C17's eye, Auto layout) write no list key, T5's brief consumes design::{paints, effects}, and dashes is Vec<f64> (figcraft-doc props.rs:84), so floats are fine there; T1's false module-doc clause (design/mod.rs:5-8) is the Task 2 deferred minor above — costs if wrong: a T5 path that copies a list through get and set fails with an engine toast on layers that hold an image or pattern paint, and changes nothing; the fix is to call the model function instead.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T2) ⚠️ variable bindings under a whole-list node.set (report Concern 2): untouched solid rows go back as node_json's 8-bit hex, so a bound colour off the 8-bit grid lets go when a neighbour row is edited; removing paint row i lets go of the bindings on the rows after it; the effects arm has no rebind, so a bound effects/k/radius stays on index k when an earlier effect is removed (engine cmd/props.rs:378-383, 612; cmd/lists.rs:161-176) → dismiss: accept whole-list node.set for every paint and effect edit, as built. — why: the spec says writes go through node.set {ids, props} (spec line 204) and lists variables and modes as a non-goal (line 16), and the brief has untouched rows go back as read; per-row commands (fill/stroke.remove, fill.set {index}, effect.set/remove, which FigCraft's own panel uses: ui-egui panels/right/paints.rs:559, effects.rs:62) would depart from both for a feature the spec leaves out. Exposure checked (zips read-only in the scratchpad): of the five FigCraft example files only ridgeline-app has bindings, 167 on 158 nodes, all fill or stroke colours on single-paint lists, alpha 1, on the 8-bit grid, none on effects; there every T2 edit keeps or drops bindings as the per-row commands would (adding re-sends row 0 exactly, per the implementer's identity probe on all five files; removing the only paint drops its binding either way). Undo restores a document snapshot (engine doc_state.rs:6-11), so it brings bindings back with the list. The Task 2 deferred minor on the paints.rs and effects.rs module docs records the limit for T5 and T6 — costs if wrong: on a file with a bound paint above another paint in the same list, or a bound effect above another effect, removing the lower row from the Design panel silently drops the paint binding, or leaves the effect binding on the wrong or a missing index, and autosave writes that in place (D14); a bound translucent or off-grid colour lets go when a neighbour row is edited. The remedy is local to paints.rs and effects.rs, with the same tests: remove_paint and remove_effect become fill.remove, stroke.remove or effect.remove {ids, index}, whose bindings follow their rows (cmd/lists.rs:274-294); set_solid becomes fill.set {ids, index, color, opacity} if colours matter too.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T3) ⚠️ align_cell over frames that run both ways (layout.rs:88-99) and set_export_rows for rows that carry options (export.rs:136-139) return a command.batch, not a plain node.set, and reach the engine only through P5.T4's apply_edit and P3.T3's BoardView::choose, which exist on no branch yet → dismiss: both edits stay as built; there is no P5.T3 gap. CARRY to P3.T3: choose hands the command id and params it is given to BoardDoc::execute unchanged and keeps no allow-list of ids (command.batch, layout.add, layout.remove, node.set and export.update must all run); Plan 6's run_menu keeps the is_ui split before it calls choose. CARRY to P5.T4: one #[gpui_kit::test] selects a horizontal and a vertical auto layout frame, runs apply_edit(align_cell(&sel, 0, 2)), and asserts that toast stays None, that each frame reads the cell's mainAlign and crossAlign for its own direction (as layout.rs:410 does), and that one edit.undo restores both. — why: a batch is an ordinary engine command, and T3 already runs both batches the way choose will. command.batch is a registered command (FigCraft cmd/edit.rs:463-486) that runs its steps as one grouped undo step and restores the group-start state when a step fails (session/undo.rs:193-201, grouped at :254); refuse_on_board blocks only proto.*, dev.* and the prototype and Dev Mode views (boards/board.rs:210-217); Sawit's policy neither hides nor UI-routes any of these ids (main crates/board/src/policy.rs:8-80, is_hidden :101, is_ui :106); BoardDoc::execute is a plain Session::execute (main crates/board/src/doc.rs:135-137); T3's tests apply every edit with board.execute(edit.command, &edit.params) (design/testing.rs:30-32) and assert one undo step for both batches (layout.rs:410-437, export.rs:516-556). choose is specified as the single execute, toast-on-error, notify path (P3-C13, P5-C20); git grep finds no choose or apply_edit on main or on lanes 1-5, and P5.T4 runs after P3.T3 (P5-C19), so the P5.T4 test is the check on the real choose. — costs if wrong: if choose filters ids, a click on the alignment grid over frames that run both ways, or an edit of export rows that carry options, shows an error toast and changes nothing until choose drops the filter (a few lines in crates/app/src/board); the P5.T4 test catches it when P5.T4 lands.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T3) ⚠️ export_bytes runs file.export through Session::execute, which raises DocState::revision without replacing the document (session/exec.rs:219-227), so whether an export alone schedules an autosave that rewrites a third-party .figcraft in place (D14) depends on the app's change detection → dismiss: export_bytes stays as built, and nothing is left to check at integration: an export replaces no document, and the autosave saves only a replaced one. — why: file.export only reads. It takes s.doc(), a &Document (FigCraft cmd/file/export.rs:87, session/access.rs:12-14), encodes the layers and, without a path, returns base64 and writes nothing (:103-109); it is not in ENDS_PREVIEW (doc_state.rs:130-131); branches::sync and textpreview::end do nothing without branch tabs or a text preview (sharing/branches.rs:144-147, canvas/textpreview.rs:74-80); after_command bumps revision after every command but records an edit only when ds.doc is another Arc (exec.rs:217-231). T3's exporting_changes_nothing_in_the_file (export.rs:306) shows no undo step and the layer unchanged. On the app side, the Plan 1 Task 1 hand-off (Plan 1 rulings.md:12) keys autosave on the document Arc, never on revision(), and Plan 1 Task 5's BoardView does exactly that: flush, queue_save and autosave all compare with same(), which is Arc::ptr_eq (lane-1 ab94fca crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:182-221, :495-497), and its review names this a strength. Plan 7's .figcraft file tabs open through the same BoardView, and FigCraft's own unsaved marker is the same identity test (DocState::is_dirty, doc_state.rs:251-253), so upstream needs an export to leave the document alone as well. — costs if wrong: every Export click would rewrite the file it came from with equal content after the 300 ms debounce: a new mtime and possibly different zip bytes, but no lost data, because P7-C1's in-place save keeps history and branches and refuses while images are left in the file, and R9's .bak stays. Only a FigCraft bump that made file.export write the document could cause it; one assert of Arc::ptr_eq on board.doc() before and after export_bytes in export.rs:306 would catch that (the test's undo count can't, since file.export has history false, cmd/file.rs:118-123).
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T4) ⚠️ the look can't be judged from the diff (report Concern 1): the 264 px column and its scrollbar, the eight xsmall align and distribute buttons in one justify_between row (position.rs:99-114), the 28 px fields with 22 px labels (number_field.rs:34, :82-141), the 64 px grid beside the gap/padding column (layout.rs:246-294), the Mixed labels on the blend and sizing menus → dismiss: no P5.T4 change, nothing enters the fix loop; CARRY to P5.T6: Step 1 screenshots one frame with auto layout (the Trail card of P5-C25 if it has one, else another frame that has) with the header and the whole Position, Auto layout and Appearance sections in view; Step 2 adds a two-layer mixed selection made over the socket (node.set another blend, and another sizingH where the engine takes one, on one of two frames, then select.set both), whose screenshot shows Mixed on the blend menu and on the W sizing menu, the eight align and distribute buttons in one row, every field with its label, and the grid beside gap/padding, nothing clipped; a section below the window's fold, which no socket op scrolls to, goes on the hand-check list (R-LIVE) — why: by measure every T4 row fits its column at the app's 13 px rem (P3.T3 delegate ruling), with label widths taken in the system font through AppKit as P3.T3 took them: a section's content is 264 - 1 - 19.5 = 243.5 px; the eight xsmall icon buttons (size_5, gpui-component button.rs:688) take 8 x 16.25 = 130 px; an X/Y field is 118.5 px, so its input keeps about 94 px for Mixed (32.1 px), and W/H beside the lock about 83 px; the two-letter corner labels (12.0-13.0 px at text_xs plus 9.75 px of padding) make their handle about 22.75 px, min_w 22 being a floor; a sizing button gets about 101 px against the 38.5 px a .small() menu button adds to Mixed (32.1 px); the full-width blend button holds its icon and its longest label, Pass through (70.7 px); the 64 px grid stands beside gap + padding (28 + 6.5 + 28 = 62.5 px), which keep 173 px of width; the panel scrolls with a bare overflow_y_scroll that draws no scrollbar, as Sawit's other side panels do (ssh/panel.rs:471, run/panel.rs:1162, servers/list.rs:117, git/thread.rs:595), and the spec asks for none (D11, Design panel); the plan's Task 6 already screenshots a card and a select.all, but select.all over ridgeline's four top-level frames may leave blend and sizing Same, so Step 2 makes the mixed state on purpose — costs if wrong: a row that looks cramped, or clips at a UI font size other than 13 px, shows only in P5.T6's screenshots or under the user's hands, and is then a few lines in design_panel.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T4) ⚠️ real input needs the user's hands (R-LIVE): how a label scrub feels and ⇧×10, the left-right cursor on labels, ⌥8 through on_key → route_key → view.design → panel.design (the tests reach the panel.design arm only through the command op and the top-bar toggle, design_panel/mod.rs:793-811), and canvas shortcuts coming back once the canvas is clicked after a field edit → dismiss: no P5.T4 change, nothing enters the fix loop; CARRY to P5.T6: (1) the live check sends {"op":"key","key":"8","alt":true} twice over the socket, with a screenshot after each (the panel toggled each time); (2) Plan 5's "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)" gain, with exact steps: drag the X label sideways — the pointer is a left-right arrow over the label, X moves 1 per px and 10 per px with ⇧ held, the release ends the drag wherever it is, one ⌘Z undoes the whole drag, and Escape during a drag puts X back; click the canvas and press ⌥8 twice — the panel hides and comes back, and the top bar's Design toggle follows it; type 40 in W, press Enter, click the canvas, press R — the rectangle tool comes on — why: R-LIVE puts pointer feel, cursor shapes and the real keyboard on the hand-check list, which the live check writes; every link of ⌥8's path is already pinned without hands: no Sawit binding takes alt-8 in the Board context (board_keys_reach_the_canvas, board/mod.rs:2699); Alt+8 reads back as key 8 with alt (every_mac_shortcut_round_trips_through_gpui, crates/board/src/input.rs:660), which is how GPUI decodes ⌥8 on a Mac (key 8, alt, key_char •; gpui-pre-macos 0.3.10 events.rs parse_keystroke); route_key runs a canvas shortcut's engine command (input.rs:191-199, :244-256), FigCraft's view.design (Alt+8, engine keymap.rs:218) pushes panel.design (cmd/view.rs:313), and on_key drains right after route_key (board/mod.rs:503-504) through the arm the command op and the toggle reach; the guard is pinned both ways (typing_in_a_field_leaves_the_canvas_keys_alone, design_panel/mod.rs:610-641), and a press on the canvas gives it the keyboard first (events.rs:46); the key op runs route_key and leaves panel.design for run_op's drain (crates/board/src/ops.rs:777-798, board/mod.rs:363-372), so P5.T6 checks the route unattended — costs if wrong: a scrub that feels off, or a ⌥8 that a real keyboard doesn't deliver, is found when the user runs the hand checks after the run, and is a small fix then.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T4) ⚠️ the P5-C3 focus guard (board/mod.rs:489-491) is shared with P4.T2 and P6.T2 under the cross-plan ruling (whichever runs first): their lanes may add the same guard, the integrator must keep exactly one, and their key tests must give the canvas the keyboard first, as this task did in toggle_ui_hides_chrome (board/mod.rs:3422) → dismiss: no P5.T4 change, nothing enters the fix loop; CARRY to P4.T2 and P6.T2 (and their integrators): reuse the guard P5.T4 lands in on_key (return unless self.focus.is_focused(window), right after the label-scrub Escape branch, board/mod.rs:482-491) and add no second one; a GPUI test of theirs that expects the canvas to act on a key gives it the keyboard first (window.focus on the view's focus handle, as focus_canvas does), and a test that expects a key to stay in their own input (P4-C7's filter and v, P6-C3's palette and u p) ends by giving the canvas the keyboard and seeing the same key act, the negative control of typing_in_a_field_leaves_the_canvas_keys_alone (design_panel/mod.rs:637-640); should a lane hold a copy of its own anyway (only if P5.T4 failed and theirs landed first), its rebase keeps exactly one guard, with the label-scrub branch before it — why: P5.T4 is the first of the three by design (native-canvas-analysis/dag.md: P5.T4 adds the on_key focus guard and its GPUI test, P6.T2 and P4.T2 reuse it; their shared resources keep them from racing), and the scheduler can't start P4.T2 or P6.T2 before P5.T4 is on main: all three hold board/mod.rs#render-layout, board/mod.rs#ui-requests and top_bar.rs (scratchpad lane-args-run2.json), and a task keeps its resources until runTask returns after its integration (native-canvas-lanes script lines 547, 564, 572 and 457-459); no lane branch holds P4.T2 or P6.T2 commits now (git log main..lane-1 to lane-5), and the Plan 4 and Plan 6 ledgers show only their Task 1 done; P5.T4 did its own share: every key test in board/mod.rs gives the canvas the keyboard first (per the review, :2799 to :3422), and its own tests carry the negative control — costs if wrong: a P4.T2 or P6.T2 test that passes vacuously behind the guard, or a second guard left after a rebase; each is a one-line fix at their review or integration.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T4) PLAN-CONFLICT crates/app/src/board/design_panel/appearance.rs:130-165: the brief says blend mode "is a Select", but the code uses a dropdown-menu button with checked PopupMenu items, as do the W/H sizing menus (layout.rs:310-372) → dismiss: the code stays and nothing enters the fix loop; the brief's Select is read as a select control, which the checked dropdown menu is (the button shows the layers' mode or Mixed, the items are every FigCraft mode in FigCraft's groups with theirs checked, and a pick is one node.set to every selected layer, pinned by the_appearance_section_edits_the_layers, design_panel/mod.rs:913-944); the sizing menus depart from no text (the brief names no widget for them); CARRY to P5.T5: its brief's other Selects (stroke align, effect kind, style, export format and scale) may be the same checked menus; a control that keeps the keyboard after a pick, as gpui-component's Select does (the searchable family list may need one), gives the keyboard back to where it was, the canvas, after a pick, a cancel or Escape, and one GPUI test pins that for a Design panel menu (the canvas given the keyboard, a pick, then r with no click between selects the rectangle tool); CARRY to P5.T6: the hand checks gain "pick a blend mode from its menu, then press R without clicking the canvas — the rectangle tool comes on" — why: the spec names no widget (Design panel, section 4: blend mode) and asks only that differing values show Mixed and that an edit reach every selected layer, which both hold; gpui-component 0.8.1's Select focuses itself on confirm, on cancel and on Escape (select.rs:209, :267, :443), so under P5-C3's literal guard (board/mod.rs:489-491) every pick would leave the canvas shortcuts dead until the canvas is clicked, while the dropdown's popover records the focused handle when it opens and focuses it again when it closes (vendor/gpui-base/src/popover.rs:107, :130-134, the PopoverState that gpui-component's Popover uses, popover.rs:17-18) and its trigger button never takes the keyboard (button.rs:864-865); it is also the pattern of the chrome beside it, P3.T3's context bar (context_bar.rs:189-199, PopupMenus by Plan 3's own text), and FigCraft's own panel builds blend as a grouped menu with the same five group ends (ui-egui panels/right/menu.rs:19-30) — costs if wrong: if the controller wants gpui-component's Select after all, the two kinds of menu become Selects with a keyboard hand-back on confirm and cancel, about 40 lines and a test, before or with P5.T5.

click an effect's gear — x, y, blur, spread and the colour row show under it; open the font family list — a search field over a list at most 320 px tall, long names cut with an ellipsis — why: by measure every new row fits a section's 243.5 px at the app's 13 px rem (the P5.T4 delegate ruling's figures): the fixed parts are 16.25 px xsmall icon buttons, the 16.25 px picker swatch (ColorPicker small is size_5, gpui-component sizing.rs:312-318), the 20 px swatch frame, the 56 px opacity field and 6.5 px gaps, and every other part is flex_1 with min_w_0, with button labels that ellipsize (gpui-component button.rs:773-800);
