- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T5) ⚠️ real input needs the user's hands (R-LIVE): drag a picker's sliders, close it, and one ⌘Z undoes the whole pick; Escape in the picker puts the colour back; pick a font, then R without a click turns the rectangle tool on; and the reviewer's: with a fill's picker open, press-drag the selected layer on the canvas, which must move with no toast (fails until Important 1 is fixed) → dismiss: no P5.T5 change from this item (Important 1 is in the fix loop on its own); CARRY to P5.T6: Plan 5's 'Hand checks (pending — needs the user)' gain, with exact steps: (a) click a rectangle with a solid fill on the canvas, click its fill swatch in the Design panel, drag the picker's sliders (the canvas follows), close the picker by clicking the swatch again, press ⌘Z once — the fill has its colour from before the pick, and a second ⌘Z undoes the edit before the pick; (b) open the swatch again, drag a slider, press Escape — the picker closes, the colour goes back, and ⌘Z then undoes no colour; (c) open the font family list, type part of a family's name, pick it with Enter or a click, press R without clicking the canvas — the rectangle tool comes on; open the list again, press Escape, press R — the same; (d) after Important 1's fix: open a fill's picker, pick a colour, then press on the selected layer on the canvas and drag it — the picker closes, the layer moves, no toast shows, and ⌘Z twice undoes the move, then the colour; a click on empty canvas while a picker is open closes it, with no toast — why: R-LIVE puts slider feel, a real Escape and a real list pick on the hand-check list, since synthetic input can't reach the window, and what each check exercises is already pinned on the real engine: color_pick_is_one_undo_step and escape_puts_a_colour_pick_back (paints.rs:592, :624; the second sends an escape keystroke through the popover's focus to the panel's capture-phase Cancel, mod.rs:300), the_stroke_section_edits_the_strokes and a_family_picked_from_the_list_gives_the_keyboard_back (paints.rs:740, typography.rs:431), and Important 1's fix brings the review's two GPUI tests (a drag after a pick; an op, then Escape) — costs if wrong: a defect only real input shows is found by the user after the run, and costs a fix then.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T5) ⚠️ cross-plan, for the controller and P6.T3: with nothing selected, P5-C13 (implemented: each top-level layer of the page with its own rows, export.rs:293-336) and P6-C8 (one export_page(board, fmt, 1.0) file) disagree, and P6-C8's shared write-and-reveal helper has to come out of export_selection's write loop (export.rs:249-261) and export_and_reveal (:266) → dismiss: no P5.T5 change; settled for P6.T3, P6-C8 wins: with nothing selected, file.export writes one PNG and file.exportSvg one SVG of the whole page (ops::export_page(board, fmt, 1.0), the page's top-level layers together), named '<page name>.<ext>' by file_stem's rules ('Page' when they leave nothing), through the one helper P6.T3 pulls out of export_selection (exports folder, nyawit::ensure, nyawit::unique, write) and export_and_reveal (reveal of the first file); P6.T3 also turns export_selection's own nothing-selected branch (export.rs:297-299) into that page file, so one fallback remains and P5-C13's 'falls back to the page's top-level layers' reads as those layers together in one file, and rewrites the nothing-selected half of exports_follow_the_settings_and_fall_back_to_the_page (export.rs:455-466) to expect one '<page>.png' — why: the spec makes file.export UI-handled only to write into .nyawit/exports/ and reveal (spec :113-114), and FigCraft's file.export with nothing selected exports the page's top-level layers as one file (engine cmd/file/export.rs:88-91), as FigCraft's own Design panel offers 'Export page as PNG', one '<page name>.png', while nothing is selected (ui-egui panels/export_panel.rs:425-433); Plan 6's Review Focus 3 ('export the whole page') and P6-C13's test (one SVG whose width spans both shapes) describe one file; boards hold many top-level items (stickies, shapes, connectors), so a per-layer fallback would drop a file per item into .nyawit/exports/ and reveal only the first; and T5's per-layer branch is unreachable from T5's own UI, since the Design panel draws no section, so no Export button, while nothing is selected (design_panel/mod.rs:302-309) — costs if wrong: if the user wants a file per layer with nothing selected (FigCraft keeps that for file.exportLayers, the layers that have export settings), P6.T3's branch is a few lines to switch back, and meanwhile selecting the layers exports each one.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T5) PLAN-CONFLICT crates/app/src/board/design_panel/typography.rs:320: parse_line_height refuses negative pixels and percentages, while P5-C8 says the line height field accepts 'a finite number or a finite N%' → dismiss: the code stays and nothing enters the fix loop; P5-C8's 'a finite number or a finite N%' names the forms the field reads (auto, pixels, a percentage; the ruling exists because parse_number reads neither auto nor %), not a promise to keep a negative, and line height joins P5-C22's refuse-rather-than-clamp fields; letter spacing keeps its negatives, as in FigCraft — why: the spec rejects invalid input and shows the current value again (spec :206); FigCraft's own panel never sends a negative line height, it clamps every form to 0 (ui-egui panels/typography/spacing.rs:161-169), while the engine stores one unchecked (engine cmd/props.rs:148-156), as it stores a negative blur (P5.T2 delegate ruling), so the panel is the only place to refuse it; pinned by a_line_height_is_auto_a_number_or_a_percentage (typography.rs:456) — costs if wrong: a negative line height, which FigCraft's panel would make 0, can't be typed; allowing it is one filter at typography.rs:320.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T5) PLAN-CONFLICT crates/app/src/board/design_panel/export.rs:270: the Export button reveals the first file with cx.reveal_path instead of running open -R as the brief, P5-C12 and P6-C8 say → dismiss: the code stays and nothing enters the fix loop; 'runs open -R' reads as the spec's 'reveal in Finder' (spec :113-114), which cx.reveal_path does; CARRY to P6.T3: the shared write-and-reveal helper (the cross-plan ruling on P5.T5 above) reveals the first file with cx.reveal_path, as export_and_reveal does, and P6.T3's tests call the writer, never the reveal — why: GPUI's macOS reveal_path shows the file selected in a Finder window, as open -R does, through NSWorkspace selectFile:inFileViewerRootedAtPath: on a background thread with no process to spawn (gpui-pre-macos platform.rs:1003-1015), the way Sawit's file tree and shell config already reveal (files/mod.rs:489, shell_config.rs:462); P5-C12's purpose holds: export_selection never reveals, only the button's handler does (export.rs:266-276), and no test clicks the button (export.rs:497 only finds it drawn), where GPUI's test platform would panic on reveal_path (gpui-pre test/platform.rs:683-685) rather than open Finder — costs if wrong: nothing in behaviour; open -R instead is one line.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T6) ⚠️ the check set its instance's window frame through the macOS Accessibility API (plan :362-366, report Decision 2), outside R-LIVE's "only over the control socket", and the notes' "no mouse, key or accessibility action reached the app" reads loosely → dismiss: no P5.T6 gap, nothing enters the fix loop; the use is accepted as done and its verdicts stand, as no precedent; CARRY to the live checks P4.T3, P6.T4, P7.T7 and P8.T4: drive the app only over the control socket, as R-LIVE says: no Accessibility API call (AXUIElement…) on any process, the check's own instance included, and no osascript or posted events; the window keeps the size Sawit opens it at; a look that needs another window size (a section below the fold, the 720 × 480 minimum) goes on the plan's hand-check list with exact steps, or is computed from the code's widths, as Plan 3's live check did ("The window can't be resized over the socket", its hand check 6); listing windows (CGWindowListCopyWindowInfo) and screencapture -x -o -l stay, as the memory recipe has them — why: I read both attempts' transcripts and tools: every axwin call passed the PID of the check's own launch (93439 in run 3, 33125 in run 2's attempt), never the other Sawit alive then (PID 50568, from the sawit.dev-keymap worktree); axwin.swift writes only kAXPositionAttribute and kAXSizeAttribute of that PID's largest window; no command or tool of either attempt uses AXUIElementPerformAction, CGEvent, cliclick or osascript; so no input reached any app and no document changed; the notes disclose it, and "no accessibility action" is exact in AX's own terms (an action is AXUIElementPerformAction, never called; position and size are attribute writes), with the frame write named in the same sentence, so the record stands; the 720 × 480 frame (plan :457-459) was set the same way, and hand checks 10 and 13 repeat the fold and the minimum window by hand; the rule stays for later checks because the spec's live-check method is the socket plus screencapture (spec Testing, Live checks), the machine is attended, the AX API reaches every app's windows (a wrong PID moves the user's own), and no later check's steps need another window size (P8-C13 hides the sidebar and terminal with layout and records the canvas size that gives) — costs if wrong: if the AX use should not have been accepted, Plan 5's 856 pt and 720 × 480 verdicts (plan :362-366, :457-459) rest on it, and hand checks 10 and 13 repeat both; if AX window sizing should be allowed, later checks queue a few window-size looks as hand checks instead of screenshotting them.
- Ruling: (delegate, P5.T6) ⚠️ Step 3's "record their answers" can't happen in this run (R-LIVE): 13 hand checks (plan :475-609), among them the brief's six (scrub X, type W, colour picker, drop shadow, font change, Export 2× PNG), wait for the user, and until then Review Focus 1-3 rest on the Task 1 and Task 4 unit tests → dismiss: not a gap, nothing enters the fix loop; Step 3 is done as R-LIVE has it (queued with exact steps under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)", plan :475, which doesn't fail the task); the controller collects it at the end with rg -n 'Hand checks' docs/superpowers/plans (program ledger, Execution run), relays the list as it stands on main after FINAL.P5 (which holds Task 6's deferred minors on the checks' wording: start states, and bullets for Review Focus 4 and 5), and writes the user's answers under plan :609 as a docs: commit (R5); FINAL.P5 counts the checks as pending, not failed — why: R-LIVE overrides the plan's mid-run ask (steps that need real hands go to that heading and don't fail the task, since the user isn't at the keyboard during the run), as the Plan 1 live check's delegate ruling held for its hand checks; the six named checks are hand checks 1, 2, 5, 6, 7 and 8, whose facts the reviewer checked against the code; Review Focus 1-3 are pinned on main by editing_mixed_x_moves_all_in_one_step and parse_rejects_bad_numbers (crates/board/src/design/mod.rs:464, :498) and scrub_is_one_undo_step and cancelled_scrub_restores (crates/app/src/board/design_panel/mod.rs:776, :798), all four passing in this check's gate run at b42ec77 (1222 passed, 0 failed), so the hands add what tests can't reach (pointer feel, ⇧×10, the cursor, a real Escape mid-drag), not the undo and parse rules; lane-5's one docs commit touches only the plan file, which main's newer 90d0a5f doesn't, so the list reaches main with this task's integration — costs if wrong: R-LIVE's own cost: pointer feel, real key routing in the panel, the picker and the font list stay unverified until the user runs them, and a defect found then is a fix after the run.
- Final review: parked — How the panel looks: spacing, clipping, focus rings, and what is below the fold (declined to judge: cannot be judged from code; T6 screenshots and hand checks 10 and 13, R-LIVE) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly under R-LIVE: the look is judged by eye, and T6 checked it over the socket at b42ec77 (the 264 pt column and theme colours, every kind of layer, a mixed and a 109-layer selection, nothing clipped or overlapped at 856 pt, the 720 pt minimum window); hand checks 10 (the fold and the whole Export button) and 13 (hover, focus rings, anything hard to hit, cramped or cut, the 720 pt minimum) are on main in the plan under Hand checks (pending — needs the user); the spec fixes only the width (D11) and the theme (D12), both measured. For the controller when relaying the checks: the final review m3 carry (the canvas at the minimum window with both panels) never reached Plan 4 (its plan has no such check), and hand check 13 predates the layers panel, so on ridgeline-app it now shows a canvas of about 55 pt between the 240 pt and 264 pt panels; ask whether a panel should give way — costs if wrong: a look defect waits for the hand checks and is fixed after the run; in a tab narrower than about 504 pt the two flex_shrink_0 panels push the Design panel past the tab edge until one is hidden (final review m3), with no data at risk.
- Final review: parked — How scrubbing feels with real input: ⇧×10, the cursor shape, and a real ⌥8 (declined to judge: needs real hands, hand checks 1 and 9) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly under R-LIVE: synthetic input cannot reach the window, and each path exists on main: the label sets CursorStyle::ResizeLeftRight (design_panel/number_field.rs:258), a drag moves 10 per px with shift held (:547), and ⌥8 runs view.design to the panel.design arm (board/mod.rs:512), which T6 drove twice over the socket (alt-8 key op handled, the panel and the top-bar toggle followed); hand checks 1 (drag feel, shift, the arrow cursor, a release outside the window, Escape, one undo step) and 9 (⌥8 and the toggle) are on main with exact steps; what the spec asks of a scrub, one undo step, is pinned by scrub_is_one_undo_step and cancelled_scrub_restores — costs if wrong: a feel defect waits for the user and is fixed after the run.
- Final review: parked — Whether a drag over Mixed values should be relative, whether Enter should give the keyboard back, and whether the gradient swatch is enough (declined to judge: product choices queued to the user, hand checks 1, 2 and 12; they stay deferred minors) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: each meets the spec as written and no later plan builds on it: an edit of a Mixed field applies to every selected layer (spec Design panel, Review Focus 1), which an absolute scrub does as one undo step that Escape cancels; the spec says nothing of focus after Enter; and gradient and image rows are shown as swatches that can be removed or replaced by solid (design_panel/paints.rs:173, gradient_swatch :210, a theme ramp beside the row name, e.g. Linear gradient); hand checks 1, 2 and 12 put the three questions to the user with the final review recommendation (the FigCraft panel moves each layer from its own value; Enter gives the keyboard back, or at least selects the text). For the controller when relaying hand check 2: while a field keeps the keyboard after Enter, a canvas shortcut typed next (a digit for opacity, Backspace) edits the field, and the next press on the canvas applies it (the I1 commit), e.g. W 40 becomes 405 — costs if wrong: undoable surprises until the user answers; each change is small: a relative scrub is each layer start plus the delta in one command.batch, Enter giving the keyboard back is one focus call in the field Enter handler, and a true swatch needs the stops in PaintKind::Gradient (sawit-board) for the two-stop linear_gradient the swatch already draws.
- Final review: parked — Whether set_solid 8-bit re-send of untouched rows breaks bindings (declined to judge: variables and modes are a spec non-goal; the reviewer accepts that half of the P5.T2 delegate ruling) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, on a wider exposure than the review names: at 0971d09 node_json writes a solid row as to_css_hex (engine automation/inspect.rs:317-333), node.set keeps a paint binding only when the colour comes back bit-identical (cmd/props.rs:378-383, cmd/lists.rs:161-190), and colorAlpha is not in node_json, so it comes back 1 (figcraft-color lib.rs:636-640); every whole-list paint edit left on main (set_solid, set_visible, add_paint, replace_with_solid; design/paints.rs:74-131) therefore lets an untouched solid row bound to a colour off the 8-bit grid go of its variable, and a bound translucent one loses its colorAlpha split, the part a variable replaces, as design/paints.rs:11-14 documents; but variables and modes are a spec non-goal (spec :16), no later plan builds on these edits, nothing in Sawit shows or re-applies a binding, and the one example with bindings (ridgeline-app, 167, all opaque, on the grid, on single-paint lists; P5.T2 delegate scan) loses none — costs if wrong: a third-party .figcraft autosaved in place (D14) silently detaches colour tokens on rows the user did not edit, seen only in FigCraft; the fix is the m1 pattern: set_solid, set_visible and replace_with_solid through the engine per-row fill.set and stroke.set {ids, index, color, opacity, visible} (cmd/edit.rs:336-337), about 30 lines in design/paints.rs plus one binding test like removing_a_paint_keeps_the_bindings_of_the_rows_after_it.
- Final review: parked — Tab and ⇧Tab between Design panel fields (declined to judge: depends on how the Board and Input key contexts resolve, not run; the spec is silent) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: read statically on main, Tab in a field does nothing and harms nothing: the Input context binds tab and shift-tab to IndentInline and OutdentInline (vendor/gpui-base/src/input/base/state.rs:196-197), whose handlers a single-line input does not register (state.rs:5031, multi-line only); Board binds tab and shift-tab to NoAction (board/mod.rs:120-122) as the spec keyboard policy requires, so the root focus traversal never runs inside the canvas view; and the key falls to on_key, whose focus guard returns while a field has the keyboard (board/mod.rs:565); the spec gives Tab to the canvas (FigCraft select.next) and names no field order, the plan asks for none, and no later plan builds on one; the controller may add the question to hand check 2 when relaying it — costs if wrong: a Figma user who tabs between fields finds the key inert, nothing more; a field order is a follow-up: a tab binding in a field-level key context that moves focus through the panel in section order, with one GPUI test.
- Final review: parked — P6.T3 menu commands, Copy as, and the whole-page export at 8e63845 (declined to judge: Plan 6 code; judged only where it calls the Plan 5 export writer) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: they are Plan 6 Task 3 (ef8c209..8e63845 on main, Plan 6 progress.md), and Plan 6 has no final review yet, so FINAL.P6 reviews them whole in its own ranges; the seam this review owns was judged and holds: the menus write through the one export writer (cross-plan ruling: P5.T5 writer, reused by P6.T3), and with nothing selected one page file goes out (P5.T5 delegate ruling, P6-C8) — costs if wrong: nothing for Plan 5; a defect in that code is FINAL.P6 to find.
- Final review: parked — Panel performance beyond the live check 109-layer selection (declined to judge: the spec sets no panel budget; Plan 8 measures the canvas) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the spec budget (a 500-node board: pan at display rate, re-render p95 ≤ 50 ms, idle CPU about 0 %) is the canvas, which P8.T4 measures on a board, where the panel starts hidden (D11); the panel adds no idle or pan cost: it reads the selection again only when the document Arc or the selection changes (design_selection, design_panel/mod.rs:224) and has no timer, spawned task or animation frame; T6 drew a 109-layer selection within a second in a debug build under build load — costs if wrong: on a design file with the panel shown, a drag of hundreds of selected layers re-reads every one (node_json) on each preview frame and may stutter; a cap or a lazy read for large selections would be the follow-up.
- Final review: parked — The gates (declined to judge: read-only, not built or run; relied on T6 at b42ec77 and the integrators at 90d0a5f and 8e63845) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, and covered since: the fix wave ran fmt, clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings, sawit-board 610 and sawit 263 passed, 0 failed, at 7580cf0 (final-fix-report.md), and the FINAL.P5 integrator rebased it onto 588ceac with no conflict, re-ran fmt, clippy and the touched crates (sawit-board 620, sawit 263 passed) and fast-forwarded main to edc8cd2 (run 4 journal, agent a884edf78d727eeef: MERGED); the wave touched only crates/board, crates/app and the plan, and workspace clippy compiles every crate test target; P8.T4 Step 6 and the program FINISH STEPS run cargo test --workspace on the merged main — costs if wrong: a sawit-core or sawit-mcp test broken by Plan 5 would surface only then; unlikely, since neither crate changed.
- Final review: parked — FigCraft own behaviour at 0971d09: node.set dropping export options whose names have two words, and no undo step inside an interaction (declined to judge: upstream; judged only how Plan 5 uses it) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: D1 pins the rev and makes any bump its own task with the full suite; Plan 5 works around the first (set_export_rows restores the dropped options with export.update inside one command.batch, one undo step, with round-trip tests) and builds on the second by design (P5-C14, P5-C15: a scrub is node.set inside one interaction), pinned by scrub_is_one_undo_step and cancelled_scrub_restores; reporting the node.set bug upstream is optional and the user call — costs if wrong: a rev bump that changes either fails those tests loudly; one that fixes node.set only makes the batch redundant.
- Final review: parked — How to handle an open pen path in the C1 fix: end it, or refuse the scrub (declined to judge: left to the fixer) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, and the fixer choice holds: scrub_begin opens no scrub while any canvas gesture is open (design_panel/mod.rs:131-170, open_gesture :216), so a label drag is refused and a picker colour runs as a plain edit that joins the pen gesture; nothing is lost that way: the engine pen gesture only ever commits (pen_finish on Enter, double-click, Delete and a tool switch, and Escape reaches pen_end before any cancel; engine canvas/tools/pen.rs:217-238, tools/keys.rs:101-107), pen_update rebuilds the path in place and keeps its paints (pen.rs:121-203), an undo with a gesture open keeps the current state for redo (session/undo.rs:114-135), and no Sawit code outside the panel calls cancel_interaction or drop_gesture; pinned by a_colour_pick_leaves_an_open_pen_path_alone and a_label_drag_leaves_an_open_pen_path_alone (re-review: ADDRESSED) and queued as the pen bullet of hand check 5; the spec asks only that a scrub be one undo step — costs if wrong: during a pen path Escape in the picker keeps the colour (one ⌘Z after Enter takes path and colour together) and a label drag is inert; the re-review rare minor stays: an agent op that ends the path while the picker is open leaves it open with no scrub, so each later colour is its own undo step and it can show open over another layer swatch; ending the path first (pen_finish before the scrub, as the text edit ends) is a few lines in scrub_begin if the user prefers it.

## Plan 6 (menus)

- Ruling: P6-C1 — In P6.T2, run_menu's is_ui branch shows the same 'Coming soon' toast as P2.T4's on_key. P6.T3 creates commands.rs and switches that branch to commands::run_ui. Add crates/app/src/board/menus.rs and mod.rs to P6.T3's files; its `git add crates/app/src/board` already covers them. — why: Keeps the plan's task order and T3's 'Create commands.rs'. T2 then compiles and passes its four tests on its own (none of them uses a UI id). — costs if wrong: P6.T2 doesn't compile, or its lane writes a stub commands.rs that collides with P6.T3's create.
- Ruling: P6-C2 — P6.T3 also routes KeyOutcome::Ui(id) in on_key (P2.T4) to commands::run_ui, for every UI id except the clipboard ids P2.T6 handles (Plan 2 C7). Add an app test: on_key with ⌘S gives the toast 'Saved'. — why: P2.T4 defers these to Plan 6, and nothing in Plan 6 picks them up. The spec makes FigCraft's keymap the one shortcut source (D7). — costs if wrong: ⌘S, ⇧⌘S, ⇧⌘E, ⇧⌘I, ⇧⌘K and ⇧⌘C still toast 'Coming soon' after Plan 6; only the menus work.
- Ruling: P6-C3 — Rely on Plan 4's C7 guard: on_key returns early unless the canvas's own focus handle is_focused(window). P6.T2 adds the guard if P4.T2 hasn't landed. The palette, the sheet and the popup menus take focus when they open, handle Up, Down, Enter and Escape themselves (gpui-kit's component::command::Command already does), and give focus back to the canvas when they close. Add the test typing_in_palette_keeps_tool: with the palette open, simulate_keystrokes 'u p' leaves the tool unchanged and the query reads 'up'. — why: gpui-pre dispatches key-down along the whole focus path before macOS inserts text. So the Board root's on_key_down (route_key, then stop_propagation) eats bound letters and the arrow keys typed into a child input or menu. — costs if wrong: The palette can't be typed in, Escape deselects instead of closing the sheet, and the arrow keys nudge layers while a menu is open.
- Ruling: P6-C4 — run_menu sends Plan 2's policy CLIPBOARD ids (edit.copy, edit.cut, edit.paste, edit.pasteOver, edit.pasteReplace) to the same BoardView handler that on_key uses for them (P2.T6 under Plan 2 C7 and C16). A context-menu edit.paste passes the menu's page point as `at`, so clipboard::paste sends edit.paste {x, y}. Drop clipboard::after_copy from P6.T3's Consumes. Add a P6.T2 test: copy a sticky, right-click empty canvas, choose Paste here, and the copy's top-left is at the click point. — why: The spec names the item 'Paste here'. Keyboard and menu copy and paste must agree, and the marker, the system text and PNG, and the cross-tab slot all live in P2.T6's handler. A plain execute skips them, and on an empty clipboard it would toast where ⌘V stays quiet. — costs if wrong: Copy from a menu doesn't reach other apps or tabs, Paste here pastes at the copy's old position, and an empty-clipboard Paste toasts 'the clipboard is empty'.
- Ruling: P6-C5 — ShortcutGroup rows become Vec<ShortcutRow { id: &'static str, label: String, keys: String }> (or a 3-tuple with the id first). A row whose keys are all dropped is dropped too. no_hidden_ids_anywhere checks the row ids. The sheet assertions add 'no ⌘J row' (vector.join) and 'no ⌃⌘M row' (object.mask): neither is hidden, so only the SAWIT_WINS filter removes them. — why: As written, the test has no ids to read from (label, display) rows. The ⌘W check passes through the hidden filter alone, so the key filter could be missing and every test would still pass. — costs if wrong: Review Focus 1 goes untested for the sheet, and ⌘J and ⌃⌘M rows can ship. Task 4 Step 2 would catch ⌘J only by eye.
- Ruling: P6-C6 — The context menu, palette, sheet and toast render whatever ds.view.ui_hidden says. Only the top bar (with the main-menu button), the toolbar, the context bar and the panels hide, as P3.T3 rules. Add a P6.T2 test: with ui_hidden set, a right-click on empty canvas opens the context menu, and it holds view.toggleUi. — why: Plan 3's C12 ruling makes the empty-canvas menu's Show/Hide UI item the on-screen way back. P3.T3's 'only the canvas renders' would hide it, and ⌘K would open an invisible palette. — costs if wrong: With the UI hidden, only its keyboard shortcut brings it back, and ⌘K and ⌃⇧? appear to do nothing.
- Ruling: P6-C7 — Take Plan 2 C10 / Plan 4 C16 as given: run_op drains ui_requests through the view's one dispatcher, and P6.T2 adds this if neither has landed. In run_op, a `key` op goes to the view first: while the palette, sheet or context menu is open, Escape closes it and answers {ok: true, handled: true}. Draining only sets palette_open and sheet_open; their input states are built lazily in render, because run_op has no Window. — why: Task 4 opens the palette with {command: app.actions} and closes it with {key: Escape}, and the spec drives live checks through these ops. The key op otherwise runs select.none in sawit-board. — costs if wrong: Task 4 Step 2's screenshot shows the palette over the sheet, or the palette never opens from the socket.
- Ruling: P6-C8 — Use one export path. For a non-empty selection, P6.T3 calls BoardView::export_selection (P5.T5), with its write-to-exports-and-reveal step pulled out into one shared helper. With nothing selected, it writes export_page(board, fmt, 1.0) through the same helper; file.exportFramesPdf uses that helper too. Without a project, exports go to <app root>/exports/, as in P5.T5. P6.T3 depends on P5.T5. — why: Both write export bytes into nyawit::unique(nyawit::exports_dir(project), ..) and run open -R; written twice, review flags the duplicated block. The spec's UI-ids text names only .nyawit/exports/, which a project-less canvas doesn't have. — costs if wrong: Two export writers drift apart (names, folders, reveal), or exports fail on the project-less canvas.
- Ruling: P6-C9 — Keep base64 out of crates/app. Copy as SVG and PNG use export_bytes (P5.T3) or export_page. Add ops::frames_pdf(board) -> Result<Vec<u8>, BoardError> beside export_page; it executes file.exportFramesPdf and decodes with the base64 dependency that Plan 2 C12 adds to sawit-board. Import and Place image encode with sawit_core::browser::base64_encode, or pass {path}. P6.T3 makes no Cargo change. — why: The app has no decoder, and the plan's files and git add line include no Cargo.toml or Cargo.lock. — costs if wrong: A lane adds a crate to crates/app and to Cargo.lock without the scheduler knowing, which brings Cargo.lock conflicts, or hand-writes a third base64 decoder.
- Ruling: P6-C10 — In events.rs, a right-click that hits nothing (no label, no hit) clears the selection with select.none before the menu opens, so context_menu shows the four empty-canvas items. A hit keeps ui-egui's rule: an unselected target becomes the selection, and a selected one keeps the whole selection. Extend right_click_selects_target: right-clicking empty space with a sticky selected leaves the selection empty and shows 4 items. — why: The spec splits the context menu into 'Over a selection' and 'On empty canvas'. ui-egui keeps the selection on an empty hit, so with the plan's selection-based context_menu the empty-canvas menu never appears while anything is selected. — costs if wrong: While anything is selected, a right-click can't reach Paste here, Zoom to fit or Show/Hide UI. If the user prefers ui-egui's behaviour, revert to keeping the selection.
- Ruling: P6-C11 — Run `cargo test -p sawit-board -- menu:: actions:: shortcuts::`, with the filters after --. — why: cargo test takes a single TESTNAME; libtest takes several filters after --. — costs if wrong: Low: the failing run before the code is written is never really seen.
- Ruling: P6-C12 — disabled_command_toasts_reason runs a non-UI id that the engine refuses: on a fresh board, run_menu('edit.pasteProperties') leaves a toast containing 'no copied properties' (EngineError::Disabled, cmd/edit/clipboard.rs:332). frames_pdf_without_frames_explains stays as written: run_ui('file.exportFramesPdf') gives 'no visible frames'. — why: run_menu forwards UI ids to run_ui, so both tests run the same code and leave Review Focus 2's execute path uncovered. Review flags the duplicate. — costs if wrong: A refused menu command could fail silently with no test failing.
- Ruling: P6-C13 — export_without_selection_exports_page places two shapes far apart (x 0 and x 1000), selects one, and asserts that the bytes are SVG that starts with '<svg' and that its width attribute spans both shapes. FigCraft writes '<svg width=.. height=.. viewBox=..>'. cancelled_dialog_is_a_no_op also runs run_ui('file.import') and answers with simulate_path_prompt_response(|_| None): the page is unchanged and toast is None. — why: The test as written can't fail for a wrong ids rule. gpui-pre 0.3.10's test platform can answer both kinds of prompt. — costs if wrong: A broken whole-page export, or an Import cancel that toasts, ships unnoticed.
- Ruling: P6-C14 — When copy_png_scale returns less than 0.01, run_ui toasts 'The selection is too large to copy as PNG' and copies nothing. copy_png_is_capped compares floats with a 1e-12 tolerance. — why: The engine raises any scale below 0.01, which breaks the 4096 px cap of Review Focus 5 for very long selections. — costs if wrong: Low: rare oversized clipboard images.
- Ruling: P6-C15 — Menu items that have a shortcut use PopupMenuItem::element, with the label on the left and the shortcut on the right; checked, disabled and on_click all work on ElementItem. The palette shows ActionItem.shortcut as muted text, or builds a Keystroke for Kbd from gpui_keystroke(keymap::primary(id, Mac)). No interface changes. — why: These are the gpui-kit 0.8.1 APIs as they are. The plan's wording leads to a dead end. — costs if wrong: Cosmetic: an implementer stalls, or drops the shortcuts.
- Ruling: P6-C16 — T1 consumes input::gpui_keystroke (P2.T1) to compare keymap specs with SAWIT_WINS, and doesn't import SAWIT_WINS_UNLESS_EDITING. ⌘B, ⌥⌘T and ⇧⌘↩ stay in the menus and the sheet, because they work while editing text. Whichever of P3.T1 (the toolbar shortcut) and P6.T1 (shortcut_label) lands second calls the other's helper; otherwise accept the one-expression overlap. — why: SAWIT_WINS is in GPUI notation and keymap specs aren't; gpui_keystroke is the only converter. An unused import fails clippy -D warnings. — costs if wrong: Nothing beyond a review comment.
- Ruling: P6-C17 — sheet() also skips the Prototype group, view.devMode, view.designPrototype, collab.cursorChat, view.nextRegion and view.previousRegion (the spec's Non-goals). They stay unhidden for the keyboard, per the spec's list. P6.T2 handles the contextMenu request (view.contextMenu, ⇧F10) by opening the context menu at the selection box. — why: The spec's hidden list leaves these keymap rows in, so the sheet would advertise Present, Preview, Dev Mode and cursor chat. The contextMenu request belongs to Plan 6's context menu. — costs if wrong: Misleading rows in the sheet, and a listed ⇧F10 shortcut that does nothing.
- Ruling: P6-C18 — Accept: the spec runs everything else through execute, and errors already toast. Record text.link, vector.offset, object.moveToPage, object.batchRename and file.colorProfile in the Plan 6 notes as a follow-up for input dialogs. P6.T4's hand-check doesn't count them as failures. — why: The spec's hidden list is explicit and binding, so hiding or disabling these items would need a spec change. — costs if wrong: A few visible items always error or do nothing until a follow-up adds dialogs for them.
- Ruling: P6-C19 — As recorded ruling R5 and Plan 2 C19 do: P6.T4 writes the answers into the Plan 6 file and commits them as a docs: commit. — why: Lane worktrees are rebased and fast-forwarded, so uncommitted edits are lost. — costs if wrong: The hand-check answers are lost.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T1) ⚠️ the sheet lists view.leftSidebar (⇧⌘\) and select.area (⌥Space or F8), and the review found no handler for their UI requests → fix: skip only select.area: add it to NOT_OFFERED (crates/board/src/shortcuts.rs:29-35, with a doc line naming the FigCraft keyboard box selection, a box the canvas never draws) and to the id list of sheet_skips_what_sawit_does_not_have (:211-219); keep view.leftSidebar on the sheet and pin its keys "⇧⌘\" beside the view.contextMenu check (:226-230) — the review search missed Plan 4 ruling P4-C10 (Plan 4 rulings.md:14: P4.T2 treats panel.leftSidebar like panel.layers and toggles show_layers), so ⇧⌘\ will work and belongs on the sheet; no plan adds a selectArea arm to the one UI-request dispatcher (ui-egui answers it with a modal key box, ui-egui shortcuts.rs:537-596 and app/commands.rs:228, which no plan ports), so its row promises a box that never appears, the misleading row C17 exists to prevent; the keyboard still reaches select.area, as it reaches the C17 ids — costs if wrong: if P4.T2 ships without the P4-C10 arm, the sheet shows a dead ⇧⌘\ (the P4.T2 review checks C10); a later task that ports the key box takes select.area back out of NOT_OFFERED.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T1) ⚠️ visible items that cannot finish beyond C18: file.revert (refused: the file was never saved) and pref.nudge (run with {} it only reads the values) → dismiss: no P6.T1 change; the C18 follow-up note that P6.T4 writes into the Plan 6 notes also lists pref.nudge (Nudge amount…, a dialog item) and file.revert (always refused on a canvas: the store owns the file and autosaves it), and the P6.T4 hand-check counts neither as a failure; the engine session keeps DocState.path None: P6.T3 writes Save As with to_bytes as the plan says, never through the engine file.saveAs or file.save {path}, which set DocState.path (engine cmd/file/save.rs:188), and P7.T6 binds tabs through store::open, never DocState::new(doc, Some(path)); a task that does give the session a path also makes run_menu and the command op refuse file.revert — the Menu policy hidden list names neither id and Enabled means an id the engine knows, so T1 must show both, enabled (menu.rs:173); BoardDoc builds its session without a path (crates/board/src/doc.rs:53-56 and :103-105; engine session/new.rs:73-76) and the P7-C1 with_images_left_in_file call sets none (engine doc_state.rs:179-182), so Revert only toasts its reason, as Review Focus 2 wants; it reloads from disk behind the store (engine cmd/file/open.rs:113-131) only once a path exists — costs if wrong: if a task sets the path anyway, Revert to saved swaps in the file from disk under the store, drops the undo history and any newer edits (after an engine Save As, everything since that copy), and the autosave writes the result over the canvas file.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T1) ⚠️ ActionItem.path ends with the item label (Object › Group selection) and ShortcutRow.keys joins alternatives with " or " → dismiss: T1 keeps both shapes; P6.T2 prints each palette row label once, either the path as a breadcrumb whose last segment is the label, or the label beside the menu part of the path (path.rsplit_once(" › ")), and a sheet that draws keycaps splits keys on " or "; the P6.T2 review checks that no palette row prints its label twice — the brief comment path "Object › Group" names the item itself (the catalog has no Object/Group submenu: object.group is "Group selection" directly under Object, engine catalog.rs:133), so a path through the item is the brief reading, documented at actions.rs:21-22 and pinned at :196-206; ruling C5 allows keys as one String, and its join is documented at shortcuts.rs:43-45; the FigCraft palette shows the label under its top menu as a section heading (ui-egui menus/palette.rs:262-304), never a path that repeats it — costs if wrong: cosmetic: a doubled label in palette rows until the P6.T2 review or the P6.T4 screenshot catches it.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T2) ⚠️ only a screen or real hands can check the ≡ menu's key column (menus.rs:653-665, w_full + justify_between inside gpui-kit's item row), the palette keycaps, the sheet's wrapped 248 px columns in both themes, and whether a real secondary click (two-finger tap or ⌃-click) reaches on_mouse_down(MouseButton::Right) (mod.rs:1028) → dismiss: no P6.T2 change, nothing enters the fix loop; CARRY to P6.T4: (1) Step 1 adds a screenshot that needs no hands: with one sticky selected, {"op":"command","command":"view.contextMenu"} opens the selection's context menu 8 px under it, the path that a_key_ops_escape_closes_the_open_menu already pins (menus.rs:1095-1124; engine cmd/view.rs:349-353 raises contextMenu, run_op drains it, mod.rs:381 and :469); expected: Cut ⌘X, Copy ⌘C, Duplicate ⌘D and Delete show their keys at the right edge of their rows, in one right-aligned column, with the labels on the left; the same menu_item builder (menus.rs:643-671) draws the ≡ menu and its submenus, so this shot stands for their key column; {"op":"key","key":"Escape"} then closes it (C7); the Step 1 palette shot also expects each of its 12 rows that has keys to end in a keycap (an empty query lists the File and Edit items in menu order, actions.rs:46-47); (2) Step 3 asks the user, in these words or close to them: in the ≡ menu and each submenu, the keys sit flush right in one column and stay readable on the highlighted row; a two-finger tap (or a mouse's right button) and a ⌃-click each open the context menu at the pointer, on an unselected sticky (it becomes the selection) and on empty canvas with something selected (the selection clears and the four empty-canvas items show), and a ⌃-click does nothing else (no drag, marquee or tool stroke); the sheet's groups scroll with a two-finger swipe when they don't fit — why: I checked what the code can answer. Key column: gpui-component 0.8.1 puts an ElementItem's builder in an h_flex().flex_1() cell (popup_menu.rs:1296-1325) of a row that the items v_flex stretches to the menu's width (:1514-1522), and its own Item draws its keybinding with the same h_flex().w_full().justify_between() (:1352-1356), so the keys land on the right edge as in gpui-kit's own menus; keys that stay muted on a highlighted row are what gpui-kit's Kbd does too (kbd.rs:242-243). Secondary click: gpui-pre-macos 0.3.10 sends mouseDown: and rightMouseDown: through handle_view_event (window.rs:122-137), maps buttonNumber 1 to MouseButton::Right (events.rs:147-152), and turns a ⌃-left-down into a right-down with control cleared and its ⌃-left-up into a right-up (window.rs:2820-2840, :2857-2876); that is the MouseDownEvent the tests' simulate_mouse_down(.., Right, ..) gives the window (menus.rs:848-851), so only the trackpad's own secondary-click setting is left to hands. Sheet: it scrolls (max_h_full and an overflow_y_scroll body, menus.rs:466 and :500). Both themes: the app has one; main.rs:137 applies Zed One Dark over gpui-component's Light default (theme/mod.rs:40), nothing in Sawit or gpui-kit calls sync_system_appearance, and the spec hides view.themeLight, view.themeDark and view.themeSystem (Menu policy; D12 ties the chrome to the Sawit theme), so the Step 2 shot covers the only theme there is. What is left is how it looks and feels, which the spec's Testing section (no pixel tests; live checks screenshot the window) and R-LIVE give to P6.T4; synthetic clicks can't open the ≡ menu, but view.contextMenu reaches the same builder without one — costs if wrong: a key column off the right edge shows in that P6.T4 shot and is a few lines in menu_item; a secondary click that doesn't open the menu is found only at the user's hand check after the run, and its fix comes then; if Sawit gains a light theme, the sheet's black 0.45 dim (deferred Minor 3, menus.rs:458) is the first line to look at.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T3) ⚠️ Export frames to PDF on a board writes one PDF page for each sticky, shape and stamp, which FigCraft builds as frames (report Concern 1) → dismiss: no P6.T3 change, nothing enters the fix loop; FigCraft's rule stays: ops::frames_pdf runs the engine's file.exportFramesPdf unchanged (crates/board/src/ops.rs:926; export_frames_pdf writes it, crates/app/src/board/design_panel/export.rs:262, and commands.rs:63-66 reveals it), so on a board each visible top-level sticky, shape, stamp, connector label and table is a page, a sticky inside a section is one too, and connectors, ink, loose text and the sections themselves get no page; CARRY to P6.T4: (1) Step 1, over the socket, no hands: on its board of two stickies with nothing selected, {"op":"command","command":"file.exportFramesPdf"} answers ok with result.pages 2, and the notes record that a board's items are FigCraft frames, kept on purpose under this ruling; (2) a hand check (pending — needs the user): on a canvas with 3 stickies, one of them inside a section, and a connector between the other two, with nothing selected, ≡ ▸ File ▸ Export frames to PDF… shows a new <page name>.pdf selected in .nyawit/exports/ in a Finder window, and Preview shows 3 pages, one sticky each, ordered by top edge and then by left edge (frames_in's reading order), with no page for the section or the connector; then ask whether boards keep this rule or get their own (the whole board on one page, or a page per section), and record the answer: neither answer fails P6.T4, and a change becomes a follow-up after the run — why: the spec makes file.exportFramesPdf UI-handled only for where the file goes and the reveal ('write to .nyawit/exports/, then reveal in Finder', spec :113-114), runs everything else as the engine does (:117) and takes FigCraft's own data as the source of truth (D7, D8), and the plan says to execute the command unchanged (plan :184); FigCraft's rule is deliberate: a sticky, a shape and a stamp are NodeKind::Frame (engine boards/board/items.rs:74, :186, :249), and so are a connector's label (boards/board/edit.rs:35) and a table (boards/board_table.rs:102); frames_pdf takes every visible top-level frame-like node (cmd/file/export.rs:372-381; figcraft-doc lib.rs:966-990, node.rs:346-348); FigCraft's own app runs it on any file, boards included (ui-egui menus.rs:113, :228-244); and the engine's one refusal on boards covers only prototyping and Dev Mode (session/exec.rs:53; boards/board.rs:209-217); 'sections and real frames' isn't a filter of that list but a new export: frames_in walks into a section and never makes it a page, file.export puts layers that aren't frames on one PDF page (cmd/file.rs:382-387), and pdf_bytes is pub(super) (cmd/file/export.rs:367), so a page per section needs figcraft-pdf in sawit-board, a Cargo change that P6-C9 rules out for P6.T3; 'real frames only' would leave a typical board of stickies, shapes and connectors, which has none, with 'the page has no visible frames'; the command op runs the same engine command (HANDLED_BY_SAWIT, ops.rs:63-76, doesn't list it), so the menu and an agent get the same pages; and as P6-C18 ruled for visible items whose engine behaviour falls short, a doubt about what the engine does goes to the user as a recorded question, not into a product rule a delegate invents — costs if wrong: until a follow-up, Export frames to PDF on a board makes a page per board item, each time in a new file (nyawit::unique, crates/core/src/nyawit.rs:49-62, which never overwrites a file), in the flagged build (D6); meanwhile a section already exports whole as one PDF page through the Design panel's Export rows (PDF is one of its formats, design_panel/export.rs:39-43); the follow-up is a few lines in ops::frames_pdf and a test for the whole board on one page (ops::export_page(board, 'pdf', 1.0) when the file is a board), or a figcraft-pdf dependency and about 20 lines for a page per section.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T3) ⚠️ the real dialogs, the Finder reveal and the clipboard were only checked against GPUI's test platform, whose reveal_path is unimplemented, so no test reaches the reveal → dismiss: no P6.T3 change, nothing enters the fix loop; CARRY to P6.T4: Step 3's 'Import an image, Export, Copy as PNG pasted into Preview' become these hand checks (pending — needs the user), each run from the ≡ menu or its key: (a) Import from file… (⇧⌘I): the open panel's button reads Import, ⌘-click picks two images, and both come in at the middle of the view, selected; Place image… (⇧⌘K): its button reads Place; Cancel in either changes nothing and shows no toast; (b) Save as… (⇧⌘S) on a project's canvas: the save panel opens in the project folder with canvas.figcraft in its name field, and Save toasts 'Saved a copy as canvas.figcraft'; (c) Export… (⇧⌘E) with a sticky selected, then with nothing selected: each time a Finder window opens on .nyawit/exports/ with the new file selected; Export selection as SVG… and Export frames to PDF… do the same; (d) Copy as ▸ Copy as PNG (⇧⌘C) with two stickies selected, then Preview ▸ File ▸ New from Clipboard: one picture of both, in the canvas's colours; (e) Copy as text, CSS and SVG, each pasted into TextEdit in plain-text mode: the stickies' text one line each, the first sticky's CSS with its width and height, and an <svg …> document; (f) after each dialog closes, the canvas has the keyboard again (V and H switch tools) — why: the tests stop at the platform, and reading gpui-pre-macos 0.3.10 (the version in Sawit's Cargo.lock) shows each call reaches AppKit as these checks expect: the open panel takes files, multiple selection and the button title from PathPromptOptions (platform.rs:882-883, :909), which import passes as files, multiple, no directories, and 'Import' or 'Place' (crates/app/src/board/commands.rs:87-92); a cancel comes back as Ok(None) (platform.rs:896), which import drops (commands.rs:94); the save panel takes the folder and the name (platform.rs:940, :943), which save_as gives as the project folder for .nyawit/canvas.figcraft and the canvas file's own name (commands.rs:130-165); reveal_path selects the file in a Finder window through NSWorkspace selectFile:inFileViewerRootedAtPath: (platform.rs:1003-1015), on the first file written (design_panel/export.rs:311-324); the pasteboard writes a single string entry as NSPasteboardTypeString and a single PNG image as public.png (pasteboard.rs:160-170, :203-231, :256), and copy_as writes exactly one entry (commands.rs:235-255): a PNG tagged sRGB for an sRGB document, Sawit's default (figcraft-render lib.rs:430-436; figcraft-doc lib.rs:281), and tagged with ICC and cICP otherwise; none of it can run over the socket, so R-LIVE puts it on the hand-check list, as the P2.T6 delegate ruling did for the system clipboard: the command op runs the engine's own file.import, file.placeImage and file.export* (no dialog and no reveal; an export comes back as base64), refuses file.save and file.saveAs (crates/board/src/ops.rs:63-76), and doesn't know edit.copyAs* (UI-only in FigCraft too, ui-egui menus.rs:34-48); the key op answers handled:true for a UI id without running it (ops.rs:806; P2-C7); and the test platform's reveal_path panics (gpui-pre platform/test/platform.rs:683-685; the P5.T5 ruling); (f) covers the one thing a dialog's own window could break that no test can see: the canvas keeping the keyboard behind the P5-C3 focus guard — costs if wrong: a fault in a dialog, the reveal or the clipboard on the real platform shows up only at the user's hand check after the run, and its fix comes then, local to commands.rs and design_panel/export.rs; the six checks take the user a few minutes.
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T4) ⚠️ Step 2's 'without ⌘W/⌘J rows' is seen live only for the top of the sheet (s2-sheet.png ends at Zoom to fit, Strikethrough and Intersect; nothing over the socket scrolls it; sheet_drops_the_keys_sawit_keeps pins the data and HC 3 the drawing) → dismiss: no P6.T4 change, nothing enters the fix loop; Step 2 is met, with HC 3 as the user's look below the fold as the P5.T6 carry has it; two corrections for the record: the notes' 'the part below the fold (Object, Vector)' (plan:252-253) and this item name groups the sheet doesn't have (FigCraft keymap.rs:29-42 has no Object or Vector group; shortcuts.rs:139-154 pins the sheet's 11: Essential, Tools, View, Zoom, Text, Shape, Selection, Edit, Transform, Arrange, Components): what went unseen is Zoom, Text and Shape below Zoom to fit, Strikethrough and Intersect, and all of Selection, Edit, Transform, Arrange and Components, which is the wording for whoever takes deferred Minor 2; and at HC 3 the user will meet ⌥⌘J (Justify, text.alignJustify, keymap.rs:254, not a Sawit key) in Text, which is not a ⌘J row — why: ⌘W's one binding is file.close in Essential (keymap.rs:173; hidden too, policy.rs:20), and s2-sheet.png shows Essential whole, the five rows shortcuts.rs:158-168 pins (Actions…, Keyboard shortcuts, Show/Hide UI, Save, Save as…), so no ⌘W row was seen, not inferred; ⌘J's one binding is vector.join (keymap.rs:273), which no hidden rule removes, below Intersect in Shape; the view draws nothing but shortcuts::sheet(): render_sheet takes its groups once (menus.rs:438) and sheet_column draws each row's label and its keys split on ' or ' (menus.rs:721-750), so a ⌘J row can't be drawn unless sheet() returns one, and sheet_drops_the_keys_sawit_keeps asserts that no row of any group shows any of the 8 SAWIT_WINS keys, ⌘W and ⌘J among them (shortcuts.rs:183-197; policy.rs:110-119), and that vector.join has no row (:177-181); the shot shows that filter at work in the drawing too: Shape's Outline stroke (⌥⌘O, keymap.rs:265, not hidden, no other key) is missing between Flatten and Union; nothing over the socket shows more: the pointer op sends only down, move and up in page points to the engine (ops.rs:747-754), the key op takes only Escape while a menu is open (menus.rs:243-259), no op scrolls GPUI's board-sheet-groups (menus.rs:497-500), the terminal was already hidden (the implementer's layout terminal:false), and no width adds a column: four need 4 x 248 + 3 x 32 + 2 x 16 = 1120 pt (SHEET_COLUMN menus.rs:56, p_4 and gap_x_8 at :501-507), over the 1040 pt cap (:465), so hiding the sidebar shows nothing new, and the window keeps its size under the P5.T6 carry; menus.rs, shortcuts.rs, policy.rs and the keymap are unchanged on main (edc8cd2) — costs if wrong: a drawing fault below the fold (a cut label, a key past the edge) shows only at HC 3 and is a few lines in sheet_column; a ⌘J row can't show while sheet_drops_the_keys_sawit_keeps passes; until Minor 2 is taken the notes name two groups that don't exist, which misleads no step, since HC 3 says scroll to the end
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T4) ⚠️ everything HC 1-10 asks stays open until the user answers (R-LIVE): the ≡ menu's own key column and highlighted row, a real secondary click and ⌃-click reaching on_mouse_down(Right), the open and save panels, the Finder reveal, the clipboard, and the canvas keeping the keyboard after each dialog → dismiss: no P6.T4 change, nothing enters the fix loop; the ten checks stay pending in the Plan 6 file (plan:264-296) for the end-of-run collection (program ledger: rg -n 'Hand checks' docs/superpowers/plans), and under R-LIVE they don't fail the task — why: R-LIVE sends exactly these to the hand-check list (a real pointer, real dialogs, Finder, the pasteboard), and none can run over the socket: no engine command or UI request opens the ≡ menu (handle_ui_requests' arms, mod.rs:510-523, have none; the ≡ button's dropdown_menu opens on a click, top_bar.rs:154-161), so s1-ctx.png through view.contextMenu is the nearest look at the builder both menus share (add_items and menu_item, menus.rs:611-672; the ≡ menu at :593, the context menu at :534); the pointer op sends page points to the engine (ops.rs:747-754), never a MouseDownEvent, so a right button or a ⌃-click needs hands (the P6.T2 delegate ruling traced gpui-pre-macos's mapping of both to MouseButton::Right); run_ui's dialogs, reveal and clipboard (commands.rs:38-76) run only from on_key (mod.rs:587) and run_menu (menus.rs:154) and need a Window that run_op has not: run_op runs ops::run (mod.rs:410-425), whose command op runs the engine's own commands and whose key op answers handled for a UI id without running it (ops.rs:785-806); every carried item has its check: HC 1-3 = the P6.T2 carry (2), HC 4-6 and 8-10 = the P6.T3 carry (a)-(f), HC 7 = the P6.T3 carry (2); I read each expectation against the code and found none a correct build would fail: HC 2's four empty-canvas items are ON_EMPTY_CANVAS (crates/board/src/menu.rs:165-170), HC 4's Import and Place titles and the selection of what came in (commands.rs:86-125), HC 5's folder and toast (commands.rs:130-187), HC 6's reveal after each export (commands.rs:55-66); main since df633b9 (P7.T8, then the FINAL.P5 fix wave to edc8cd2) touches none of menus.rs, menu.rs, commands.rs or top_bar.rs, and in events.rs only on_left_down; for deferred Minor 1 (HC 10 asks only about dialogs): the code also gives the canvas the keyboard back after the ≡ menu, since fill_main_menu makes the canvas focus its action_context (menus.rs:577-593), gpui-component 0.8.1 sets previous_focus_handle only for its own context menus (context_menu.rs:483) and never focuses a dropdown's trigger (dropdown_menu.rs:178-179), and dismiss then focuses action_context (popup_menu.rs:1074-1091); tests pin the same for the palette, the sheet and the context menu (menus.rs:929, :967, :1058, :1119, :1147, :1200), so Minor 1's extra HC 10 line is a look for the final review's triage, not a gap — costs if wrong: a fault in the ≡ menu's look, the secondary click, a dialog, the reveal or the clipboard is found only at the user's hand check after the run, and its fix comes then, local to menus.rs, events.rs, commands.rs or design_panel/export.rs
- Ruling: (delegate, P6.T4) ⚠️ the green gate numbers (1268 passed, 9 ignored, 0 failed; fmt and clippy clean at 504dad6) are the implementer's, from scratchpad live-P6.T4/gates.log, and main has moved since df633b9 → dismiss: no gap and no change; the log opens with clippy's Finished line (so fmt passed, or clippy would not have run, and -D warnings would have ended in could not compile) and the known block v0.1.6 future-incompat note; its 13 test result lines all read ok and add up to 1268 passed (254, 608, 324, 3, 14, 1, 5, 49, 10 and four 0s), 9 ignored, 0 failed, with no FAILED line (its other failed hits are passing tests named a_failed_…); 504dad6 changes one file, the Plan 6 plan (+61 lines), which no build or test reads (the crates embed only CHANGELOG.md and a mermaid fixture, and name .superpowers only in the file tree's hidden list, crates/core/src/files.rs:16), so its gates are df633b9's;
