- Ruling: (controller) R-TEXTEDIT in the program ledger's Cross-plan rulings binds this plan's final fix wave: one BoardView helper ends an open canvas text edit before any non-text, non-clipboard command; FINAL.P6 creates it with its C1 fix, FINAL.P7 routes the layers panel and the command op through it.
- Final review: parked — The look and real input in hand checks 1-10: the ≡ menu's key column and highlighted row, a real secondary click and ⌃-click, the open and save panels, the Finder reveal, what reaches the pasteboard, and the keyboard after dialogs (declined to judge: R-LIVE gives these to the user) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly under R-LIVE: all ten are on main under 'Hand checks (pending — needs the user)' in docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-native-canvas-6-menus.md (:269-306) with exact steps and what to look for, and the FINAL.P6 fix wave (7823f0d) widened HC 2 (the selection's menu stays in the window and scrolls to Select all) and HC 10 (the keyboard back after the ≡ menu, the palette, the sheet and the context menu close); none of it can run over the socket (no op opens the ≡ menu or a real panel, synthetic clicks don't reach the window, the test platform has no reveal), the P6.T4 delegate ruling already kept them pending and the reviewer agreed, and what code can pin is pinned by GPUI tests on real dispatch (⌘K, ⌘/, ⌃⇧?, ⇧F10, the ≡ menu's keyboard navigation, drawn menu items, right-clicks through on_right_down) — costs if wrong: a misaligned key column, a ⌃-click that also starts a drag, a panel opening in the wrong folder or a missing pasteboard flavour shows only when the user runs the checks after the run, then a small fix in menus.rs, events.rs or commands.rs; the preamble's SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS=1 is a harmless no-op since P8.T1 (763326c) removed the flag.
- Final review: parked — Undoable commands run from the ≡ menu while typing that don't remove the edited text (Duplicate, Group) fold into the 'Edit text' undo step, as in FigCraft's own app (declined to judge: engine parity; only the data-loss case was raised, C1) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, and superseded for the menus since the review: R-TEXTEDIT's fix (1cb80c9) has run_menu end the edit before any id but text.* and copy and paste (crates/app/src/board/menus.rs:136-162; policy::keeps_text_edit, crates/board/src/policy.rs:145), so Duplicate or Group from the ≡ menu, the context menu or the palette now leaves the typing as its own undo step and the command as the next (menu_commands_end_the_typing_unless_they_act_on_it, menus.rs:1221); what still folds in is three chrome actions that call BoardView::run rather than choose: template and table placement (toolbar.rs:107, :113) and Add auto layout (design_panel/layout.rs:174); their commands only set the selection, which keeps ds.text_edit while its layer exists (engine doc_state.rs:330-334, per the re-review), so the edit stays open and later ends as one step with them, as FigCraft's own app does; nothing is lost, and the spec says nothing of undo steps while typing — costs if wrong: placing a template or table, or adding auto layout, while a sticky is typed in gives one undo step for the typing and that action, so one ⌘Z takes back both and ⇧⌘Z brings both back; a follow-up calls end_text_edit before those three (a few lines and one test).
- Final review: parked — Save As over a .figcraft file that is open in another tab: the user confirms the replacement, and that tab's disk-change guard (P7.T6) then refuses its saves (declined to judge: the guard is Plan 7's code) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: Save As writes another file only after the save panel's own replace confirmation (a name typed without the extension that lands on an existing file is refused instead, figcraft_path, crates/app/src/board/commands.rs:192-207), sends the canvas's own file to Save with its refusals (same_file by device and inode, :178-180, :211), and writes the copy through a writer of its own (write_copy, :221); the other tab then meets exactly the case P7.T6 built the guard for, any app or window writing its file: its next save is refused (Writer::guard and refuse_overwriting_changes, crates/board/src/store.rs:278, :388) with a toast that names the ways out, close and reopen to load the new file, or save under another name to keep its version (a_file_changed_on_disk_since_it_opened_is_not_saved_over, crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:1923), FigCraft's own rule (#1330); the spec asks only that Save As writes a .figcraft copy (Menu policy) and that file tabs autosave in place (D14), and the guard is its never-lose-data intent at work: neither tab silently overwrites what the other wrote — costs if wrong: a user who replaces a file another tab holds and keeps editing there sees the refusal at that tab's next autosave; edits made there after the replacement stay unsaved until they are saved under another name or the file is reopened, and closing that tab first drops them, as its toast warns; a follow-up could refuse a Save As target that another canvas tab is bound to, or have that tab reload (a few lines in save_copy).
- Final review: parked — A Save As copy leaves out images the loader left in the file (declined to judge: FigCraft parity and P7-C1's territory) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: those are the images past FigCraft's load budget, more than 20,000 images or 4 GiB of them in one file (figcraft crates/format/src/lib.rs:66, images_past_budget), which the loader leaves in the file unread; FigCraft's own file.saveAs writes such a copy and refuses only a save or Save As over the original (figcraft crates/engine/src/images_left_in_file_tests.rs:26-41), and Sawit matches it at HEAD: opening says the images stay in the file and that saving over it would drop them (crates/board/src/doc.rs:98-103), an in-place save is refused with 'Save it under another name; this file keeps them' (refuse_dropping_images, doc.rs:134; P7-C1; a_file_with_images_left_in_it_is_not_saved_over, crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:1872), and Save As never writes the original (same_file sends it to Save, commands.rs:178-180); the original keeps every image, and the copy is the one FigCraft's own refusal steers the user to — costs if wrong: a Save As copy of a .figcraft file past that budget lacks the unread images and its toast 'Saved a copy as …' doesn't say so, while the original still holds them; a follow-up adds the count to that toast (one line).
- Final review: parked — Whether an open palette, sheet or context menu survives a switch of canvas tabs and comes back without the keyboard (declined to judge: depends on GPUI focus behaviour the reviewer couldn't run; worth a hand-check line) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: neither the spec, Plan 6 nor P6-C3 speaks of a tab switch with an overlay open; P6-C3 asks that each overlay takes the keyboard when it opens and gives it back when it closes, which tests pin and HC 10 covers; from the code it can come back without the keyboard: Workspace::activate_tab moves no focus (crates/app/src/workspace.rs:655), and an overlay takes the keyboard only on the frame that builds it (crates/app/src/board/menus.rs:269-321, the palette's field at :314), so if focus went elsewhere while the tab was hidden, the overlay shows again while keys reach neither it nor the canvas (on_key's focus guard), and one click on it or outside it settles that; a tab that isn't drawn registers no key listeners and each tab's overlays run only that tab's view, so no command lands in the wrong tab and nothing is lost; no later plan builds on it — costs if wrong: after ⌃Tab or a tab click and back, an open palette, sheet or context menu may need one click before it takes keys; the controller can add one line to HC 10 when it collects the hand checks (open the palette, switch to another tab and back, type: say where the keys go), and a follow-up closes the overlays when the tab is hidden or refocuses the open one when it shows (a few lines in render_menus).
- Final review: parked — GPUI tests (cargo test -p sawit) and clippy not re-run at HEAD; the review relied on the integrator gates and P6.T4's 1268-test run, and re-ran sawit-board (620) and rustfmt --check on Plan 6's files (declined to judge: no disk for an app rebuild) — Ruling: (delegate) superseded since the review: the fix wave ran fmt, clippy -D warnings, sawit (267) and sawit-board (624) at b340f77 after touching its sources against the re-cloned target (scratchpad final-p6/gate-final.log), and the FINAL.P6 integrator then rebased lane-4 onto 885eb0c (P8.T1's cutover), ran cargo fmt --all -- --check, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings (clean), sawit-board (626 passed) and sawit (260 passed, the four new GPUI tests at menus.rs:1159-1250 among them), and fast-forwarded main to that same commit, 7823f0d, which is HEAD (run wf_6ecd7f0b-036, agent a859d44e29b9d6cf9); the counts reconcile with the test attributes in the tree (P8.T1 took 7 app tests with canvas.rs and added 2 board tests; the fix added 4 of each), and dedup.log shows no re-clone of lane-4 after 06:26, so the run used lane-4's own builds; the whole-workspace run on the merged main is FINISH STEPS 2-3 anyway — costs if wrong: a failure in a crate the integration didn't re-test (sawit-core, sawit-mcp; the fix touched neither) shows only at that FINISH STEPS gate run, before anything merges; nothing is pushed in between.
- Final review: parked — Plan 1's fix wave a7da64f hid six View toggles (declined to judge: Plan 1's decision; judged only its effect on Plan 6's menus and the spec drift it leaves, Minor N2) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the hide was Plan 1's final-review I1 fix, one of that review's own options, marked ADDRESSED by its re-review (Plan 1 final-rereview.md:3-21), and it follows the Menu policy's own rule (hidden: out of scope) with D4's overlay list, which draws no rulers, grids, slices or mask and frame outlines (crates/board/src/policy.rs:44-51 says why); for Plan 6 it changed only a test example (checked_follows_engine now uses view.outlines), and no_hidden_ids_anywhere holds with the six — costs if wrong: the spec's hidden list (spec:97-110) stays six ids short of policy::HIDDEN until the controller records them; Plan 1's re-review (:21) and this review's N2 both asked, and neither the spec nor any ledger has them yet, while P6-C18 treats that list as binding, so a later reader of the spec could un-hide six toggles that tick and draw nothing; one controller ruling line or one spec bullet closes it (View: view.pixelGrid, layoutGrids, rulers, slices, maskOutlines, frameOutlines; nothing draws them), and bringing one back means drawing it in overlay.rs first.
- Final review: parked — The dialog-less items that P6-C18 and the P6.T1 delegate ruling accepted as follow-ups: text.link, vector.offset, object.moveToPage, object.batchRename, file.colorProfile, pref.nudge, file.revert (declined to judge: ruled follow-ups) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the controller ruled the first five (P6-C18: the spec runs everything else through execute and its hidden list is binding; errors already toast) and the P6.T1 delegate added pref.nudge and file.revert; the follow-up list is on main in the plan's Task 4 notes (plan:265-267), and the hand checks don't count these as failures; file.revert's refusal still rests on a session without a path at HEAD, after P7.T6 and P8.T1: BoardDoc::load passes None (crates/board/src/doc.rs:105), pinned at crates/board/src/ops.rs:1503 — costs if wrong: until a follow-up adds input dialogs, these visible items toast a parameter error or do nothing (text.link also on ⌘K and ⇧⌘U while text is typed), and Revert to saved is always refused; Object ▸ Crop image (review N1) belongs on the same list and isn't on it yet.

## Plan 7 (agents)

- Ruling: P7-C1 — R-D14, in P7.T6, keeps what it can and refuses what it can't, as FigCraft's own file.save does. (1) from_bytes_with_issues puts loaded.versions and loaded.branches on the session's DocState, and to_bytes writes save_bytes_with_history(&doc, None, &ds.versions, &ds.branches), so history and branches survive an in-place save. Their two 'saving drops it' notes go, and doc.rs's two tests flip to 'a save keeps them'. (2) Images past the loader's budget can't be written back. open_with_issues records them with DocState::with_images_left_in_file(path, n), and BoardView skips in-place autosave while ds.images_lost_saving_to(path) is Some. It shows the engine's refuse_dropping_images sentence; Save As still works. (3) R9's .bak stays the backstop for what the loader repaired. P7.T6 adds crates/board/src/doc.rs and crates/board/src/store.rs to its files. — why: D14 promises in-place autosave, and the spec's never-lose-data intent (D5 backups) forbids silently stripping a file. The APIs exist at 0971d09, and this is exactly the engine's own save rule. — costs if wrong: About 20 lines in doc.rs, store.rs and BoardView, plus two rewritten tests. Sawit's files then carry a history the canvas never shows.
- Ruling: P7-C2 — Move the Excalidraw half of replace ({scene}, and {path} ending .excalidraw) into P7.T4, which adds crates/board/src/ops.rs to its files. T2 ships replace {path: .figcraft} only and answers {scene}/.excalidraw with {ok:false, error:'replace: ...'} until T4 lands. Order: T1 -> T2 -> T4. — why: The smallest move that breaks the cycle; T4 already owns the importer. — costs if wrong: T4 grows by one op arm and one test; nothing is lost.
- Ruling: P7-C3 — replace {path: .figcraft} runs in the current session as one grouped undo step. It runs file.import {path}, which appends the file's pages (a missing or bad file is an engine error, so the reply is ok:false), then page.delete on each of the canvas's old pages. There is no session swap: the canvas keeps its kind, autosave baseline and view, ⌘Z restores the old board, and nothing is written beside the source. — why: This matches today's undoable replace and the spec's backup intent. file.import of a .figcraft and page.delete both exist (engine cmd/file/open.rs:186-206, cmd/page.rs:90). — costs if wrong: About 10 more lines than a swap. A design file loaded into the project canvas stays a board (doc.kind), which suits D11's project canvas.
- Ruling: P7-C4 — summarize skips BoardItem::Label layers and reports a label's text as its connector's `text` (board::label_of + text_of). get_summarises_types_and_text adds a labelled connector and still expects exactly 3 elements. — why: The spec's Summary gives connectors text? and has no label type, and T7 expects 3 nodes + 2 connectors. — costs if wrong: A few lines. Agents edit a label through its connector, as update's text rule already does, not by the label's own id.
- Ruling: P7-C5 — Gate T5's native offline path. Sawit gets a native_canvas: bool, read once from SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS in Sawit::new; tests set it through a constructor or setter, never set_var. Off: today's offline behaviour (get reads canvas.excalidraw; other canvas tools need the app). On: the plan's path. Plan 8 Task 1 removes the gate and adds crates/mcp/src/{lib.rs,app.rs} to its files. — why: D6 binds (opt-in during build-out, main stays releasable), and the MCP process can't see the app's flag. — costs if wrong: One bool and a branch until Plan 8.
- Ruling: P7-C6 — Offline canvas_get is store::open (which migrates) + summarize; with neither file it returns {elements: []}. Today's canvas_file_summary stays only as the fallback when the import fails (an .excalidraw the importer rejects). That is the MCP fallback Plan 8 keeps legacy_canvas_file for. Under C5 it is also the flag-off path. — why: Keeps the test's two halves and gives Plan 8's 'MCP fallback' a meaning. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C7 — Migrate in open_with_issues's NotFound branch, only when the path's file name is nyawit::BOARD_FILE. Return the import notes as issues (shown as a toast per R9), write no .bak, and touch neither file. — why: R9 makes BoardView open with open_with_issues; open delegates to it, so both paths migrate. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C8 — BoardView starts with no saved baseline when its file doesn't exist yet and the loaded board has layers (a migration or an import), so the first autosave or flush writes the .figcraft. T6's test fixture has at least one element. — why: D5's 'imports it once', T4's 'the first autosave writes canvas.figcraft', and T6's flush test. — costs if wrong: One condition in BoardView::open/import.
- Ruling: P7-C9 — Key board file tabs by the bound .figcraft path (BoardView::path(), the sibling for an .excalidraw), so both clicks reach one tab. open_file routes a .figcraft equal to the project's board_file to open_canvas. — why: Each view autosaves its own document; newest-save-wins would silently drop one view's edits. — costs if wrong: A path comparison in open_file.
- Ruling: P7-C10 — Skeleton.points are relative to (x, y), as in Excalidraw and today's schema; End {x, y} are page coordinates. apply adds (x, y) + offset to points. An unbound arrow end without End x/y uses (x, y) for the start, and for the end the last point, defaulting to (x + width, y + height). T4 passes points through and fills End x/y only for unbound ends. — why: Today's MCP schema is Excalidraw's, so agents already send relative points. — costs if wrong: None if agreed before T1 lands.
- Ruling: P7-C11 — One predicate decides both ops: commands lists exactly the ids command runs, with the hidden ids and P1.T6's refusal list removed. commands_hide_hidden_ids also asserts that file.save is absent. — why: The list exists so agents can call what it shows; one predicate also avoids two diverging lists. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C12 — update and apply share one colour helper that implements the spec's rules (board.setColor for board items, fill for other nodes, transparent = no fill, never board.setColor of backgroundColor on connectors or ink). — why: Spec 'Skeleton' colour rules; avoids duplicated logic. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C13 — T1 and T3 list Cargo.lock and add it to their commits. The versions are already locked (serde 1.0.229, base64 0.22.1, roxmltree 0.21.2, kurbo 0.13.1). T3 may use figcraft_geom::kurbo instead of a direct kurbo dependency. — why: Adding a dependency to a workspace member rewrites its Cargo.lock entry. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C14 — T1 adds base64 = "0.22" (FigCraft's version, already locked) to crates/board/Cargo.toml, unless an earlier lane already did (P2.T6 and P5.T3 also need it). Later tasks reuse it. — why: file.import takes base64 data; export_png returns png_base64. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C15 — Add crates/board/src/lib.rs to T1-T4's files (R2); nothing else changes. — why: R2: each task declares its own module; the overlap scheduler reads file lists. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C16 — T5 adds pub async fn canvas_get_tool(&self, a: WindowArgs) and canvas_command_tool(&self, a: CanvasCommandArgs), following the db.rs pattern. Its tests pass project = a tempdir and AppPaths { root: tempdir }, so is_running is false and nothing is written under the repository. — why: The tool methods are private to the router, and project_dir falls back to the cwd. — costs if wrong: Two thin wrappers.
- Ruling: P7-C17 — Drop crates/app/src/workspace/control.rs from T5's files and git add; if a change turns out to be needed, T5 lists it then. — why: A no-op entry only creates false overlap with P1.T6 and P8.T1. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C18 — Start the server as target/debug/sawit-mcp (cargo build -p sawit-mcp), or as a symlink named sawit-mcp to target/debug/sawit. Set SAWIT_HOME=$S/home (sawit-mcp honours it through AppPaths::default_macos), project=$S/proj, and SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS=1 under C5. Launch and stop the app per Plan 1's live rulings (--foreground, open_page, /bin/kill $PID). — why: main.rs:94-97 dispatches on argv[0], not on a subcommand. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C19 — Use params {name: <id of the first board.templates entry>}. Plan 3's place_template and Plan 8 Task 4's board.template calls need the same parameter. — why: The engine reads `name` and refuses a call without it. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C20 — Use `cargo test -p sawit-board -- ops:: summary::` and `cargo test -p sawit-board -- excalidraw:: store::`; libtest takes several filters. — why: cargo test accepts one TESTNAME positional. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C21 — P7.T3 changes that test's op to one that is never supported (e.g. "teleport"). — why: Keeps the test covering what its name says. — costs if wrong: One line.
- Ruling: P7-C22 — The fixture's free line has exactly 2 points. The test expects 2 connectors (one bound at both ends, one free with no caps) and 1 ink (the freedraw). — why: An assertion that accepts either outcome can't catch a wrong line mapping. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P7-C23 — Accept the synchronous render, because the spec mandates synchronous answers and typical diagrams take under 1 ms. T3 notes the bound at the op; no Answer::Later. — why: Spec 'Agent wire contract'; Plan 8 deletes Answer::Later. — costs if wrong: A rare UI stall of up to 5 s on a pathological diagram.
- Ruling: P7-S1 — Split. T2 ships get/add/update/delete/clear/replace/commands and then depends on P7.T1, P1.T2, P1.T6 and P2.T1 only. export_svg/export_png and their two tests become a follow-up task (e.g. P7.T8: files crates/board/src/ops.rs; depends on P7.T2 and P6.T3; P7.T7 depends on it). — why: Every function keeps its owner, and Plan 7's board and MCP work can run beside Plans 2-6 instead of after P6.T3. — costs if wrong: One more small task and review.
- Ruling: P7-X1 — (from Plan 8 C15) P7.T5 makes sawit-mcp's canvas() helper (crates/mcp/src/app.rs) turn a reply whose `ok` is false into `Err(error)`, on the app path and on the headless path; the control socket keeps answering with the op value — without it, failed canvas ops reach agents as successes after the cutover — costs a few lines in P7.T5.
- Ruling: P7-X2 — P7.T8 (new, from S1) has its own brief, task-8-brief.md in this workspace, and runs after P7.T2 and P6.T3; P7.T7, P8.T1, P8.T2 and P8.T4 depend on it — the split keeps P7.T2-T6 off Plan 6's chain — costs one more small review.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T1) ⚠️ apply passes no color, style or author, so shapes and stickies take the toolbar's sticky colour, ink the marker colour, connectors the toolbar's routing (Elbow by default) and stickies the Preferences author (FigCraft items.rs:104,108,228,279, connectors.rs:49) → fix: pin fixed values in crates/board/src/skeleton.rs: color = BoardTool::default().sticky (yellow) on every board.shape (:588) and board.sticky (:599), author:false on board.sticky, color = BoardTool::default().marker (#1E1E1E) on board.ink (:724), style:'straight' on board.connector (:765); set_colors still applies backgroundColor and strokeColor afterwards; one new test applies one list to a fresh board and to one whose session.tool.board (sticky pink, routing curved, marker red) and prefs.user_name differ and asserts equal documents and straight connectors; text is left alone (its default fill follows the page, not the toolbar), and Excalidraw's transparent default for agent shapes is not brought back (filled board shapes are what the spec's board.shape mapping draws) — today's canvas_add_elements never reads the user's toolbar (convertToExcalidrawElements' fixed defaults, web/canvas/src/api.ts:386), P7.T5 answers one MCP call from the app's session or from a fresh headless one, and Plan 3's flyouts set exactly tool.sticky {color} and tool.connector {style}, so unpinned the same call draws differently by path and by the user's last pick; straight rather than the engine's Elbow because Elbow draws a free 2-point line or arrow as a Z (FigCraft derived.rs:327 short_elbow), bending agents' diagonal lines and every migrated Excalidraw diagonal arrow, while straight keeps today's edge-to-edge arrows (api.ts:293 routeArrows) and the spec's 2-point line; canvas_add_elements' description can't carry the defaults before P8.T2 anyway (Plan 7: MCP descriptions change in Plan 8) — costs if wrong: agents' shapes, stickies and connectors ignore the user's colour and style picks, and connectors are straight where FigCraft's own tool defaults to elbow; about 15 lines and one test now, undone by dropping the pinned params (elbow is a one-word change).
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T1) PLAN-CONFLICT: skeleton.rs:199-200,231-235 fills a text layer from strokeColor and ignores backgroundColor, against the literal spec colour rule and P7-C12 (fill for other nodes) → dismiss: the code stays, and this amends P7-C12 and the spec's Skeleton colour rule for text: strokeColor is the text's fill (its glyph colour; transparent means no fill), backgroundColor is ignored, and text gets no stroke; P7.T2's update gets the same through set_colors; P7.T3 puts a Mermaid text's SVG fill into strokeColor, as pele.ts textColor (:633-636) does at :422, never into backgroundColor (its fill-to-backgroundColor line is for shapes), and P7.T4 passes an Excalidraw text's strokeColor through as its colour — the spec's Skeleton follows today's MCP schema, Excalidraw's, where a text's colour is strokeColor and its backgroundColor is never drawn, so the literal rule would draw ported Mermaid text black with a coloured outline (against D10's same result as today) and every migrated Excalidraw text as hollow outlines, since its backgroundColor defaults to transparent (against D5 and D9); text_is_coloured_by_stroke_color_and_has_no_background pins the code — costs if wrong: nothing in code; skeletons and update can't outline text (canvas_command node.set {stroke} still can), and backgroundColor on text does nothing, as in Excalidraw today.

- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T3) ⚠️ On-screen parity with today (D10) can't be judged from the code: the Skeleton carries no font size, label colour, dash or start cap (loose text at 12 px, labels in default ink, dashed edges solid) and bound edges are straight engine-routed connectors → dismiss: no P7.T3 change beyond the fix round it already owes for the review's Important 1 and 2;
