- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T4) ⚠️ the Err contract has two consumers outside this diff: open and open_with_issues answer Err(Format|Io) naming canvas.excalidraw for an old canvas the importer rejects and write nothing (store.rs:108-112, test :657); P7.T5's offline canvas_get must fall back to canvas_file_summary on it (P7-C6), and BoardView shows its file-error state (mod.rs:134) with no in-app way to start a blank canvas → fix: only for an old canvas with no content; the Err stands for one whose content the importer cannot read, and its consumers are carried. (1) Fix round: a canvas.excalidraw that is empty or only whitespace (after a byte order mark) opens as a new blank board with no notes and writes neither file, in the migration only; read_file stays strict, so replace {path} (ops.rs:466) keeps refusing an empty file as canvas_load does today (crates/mcp/src/app.rs:741); every other unreadable old canvas stays an Err (test :657 unchanged). (2) CARRY to P7.T5, on its native offline path (P7-C5): when store::open fails and the canvas file (store::board_file) is absent, the error is the import of the old canvas (store.rs:89-112), so canvas_get answers canvas_file_summary (P7-C6); when the canvas file is there, its own error is the answer (a tool error, P7-X1), never the old canvas's elements; every other offline op answers the open error and writes nothing, never a blank board; tests in crates/mcp/tests/tools.rs: with only a canvas.excalidraw of {"type": "excalidraw"}, canvas_get gives 0 elements and canvas_command board.sticky is an error naming canvas.excalidraw that leaves the folder as it was; with a corrupt canvas.figcraft beside the P7.T4 fixture as canvas.excalidraw, canvas_get is an error, not the fixture's elements. (3) CARRY to P7.T6: the project canvas needs nothing new: the Plan 1 error state (main 89baf55 crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:138, :497-501) shows the reason naming the file, copyable, never saves, and ops answer it (not_opened :226-235); BoardView::import of an .excalidraw the importer rejects shows that state and creates no sibling .figcraft, tested as corrupt_file_shows_error_and_leaves_file (:773) is, and an empty one opens blank as the migration now does (reusing the fix round's helper); no start-a-blank-canvas action is added — why: an error for content that cannot be read is the Plan 1 precedent for a corrupt canvas.figcraft (test :773), and a blank board in its place would be saved as canvas.figcraft at the first edit and stop every later open from importing the old canvas (report decision 2); the detail names the file and the reason, so fixing or moving it is the way out, and the spec asks for no other; the line is content: an empty or whitespace-only file holds nothing a later importer could recover and today opens blank (web/canvas/src/main.tsx:319-324 reads blank text as no scene; crates/app/src/canvas.rs:580), so D5's "same behaviour as today" holds for it, while JSON the importer cannot read (a newer format, say) might hold a board; P7-C6 limits the fallback to an .excalidraw the importer rejects, and only an absent canvas file tells P7.T5 that the error is that one: the old canvas stays on disk after every migration, so a fallback on any error would show agents a months-old board whenever canvas.figcraft is corrupt — costs if wrong: about 5 lines and one test in the fix round P7.T4 runs anyway for Important 1, and a few lines and two tests in P7.T5; without the P7.T5 carry an agent reads the old canvas as the current one, or an offline write puts a blank canvas.figcraft in place of the old canvas.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T4) ⚠️ on-screen parity of a migrated board (D5, D9) cannot be judged from code: a skeleton has no rotation, opacity, dash, stroke width, font size, label colour or group, and the implementer's render of a real-canvas copy (134 elements, 0 notes) is not reproducible from the diff → dismiss: no P7.T4 change beyond the fix round it owes for the review's Important 1 (arrowheads); CARRY to P7.T7 and P8.T4. (1) P7.T7 Step 1 (unattended, R-LIVE, R-LAUNCH) migrates, besides the P7.T4 fixture, a COPY of the one real old board on this Mac, ~/Library/Application Support/sawit/canvas.excalidraw (166,806 bytes on 2026-10-30; 134 live elements: 39 rectangles (31 rounded), 3 diamonds, 50 texts (48 of them labels: 42 on shapes, 6 on arrows), 42 arrows, 30 of which carry the 10-19 sampled points canvas_add_mermaid writes), placed at $S/proj/.nyawit/canvas.excalidraw; expected get while the board is unchanged: 31 roundedRectangle, 8 rectangle, 3 diamond, 2 text and 42 connector (6 with text, 38 joined at both ends, 4 at their end only), and the copy's checksum unchanged; P7.T7 screenshots it and writes the differences into the plan file in words (R5); the copy and its screenshots stay in the scratchpad, are deleted afterwards and are never committed. (2) If the lane's base still has the web canvas, the flag-off run that the P7.T3 carry already makes is done in a fresh scratch project and also draws, with canvas_add_mermaid, "erDiagram / CUSTOMER ||--o{ ORDER : places" and "flowchart LR / A --- B / B <--> C" (both render with Pele: no marker on A --- B, markers at both ends of B <--> C, bars and a crow foot on the ER line) beside that carry's source (c), the classDiagram; P7.T7 screenshots the legacy canvas, stops the app once canvas.excalidraw is on disk, relaunches with SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS=1 on that project so the file migrates, then screenshots and gets it: after the P7.T4 fix round A --- B is a connector with no caps and the class heads stay at Animal, while crow feet, bars and the composition diamond come in as a triangle end cap, B <--> C and the ER line keep only their end head, dashes come in solid and curves straight (recorded, not failed); without the web canvas this pair goes to the hand-check list. (3) Under "Hand checks (pending — needs the user)", P7.T7 extends the P7.T3 carry's item (choose the Skeleton fields to add) with the import's losses: text sizes, label colours, marker kinds and a head at each end, plus rotation, opacity, fill style and groups for boards drawn by hand; P8.T4's real-board walk-through (cross-plan ruling: a copy, never the original) asks the user which of them matter — why: each loss is the spec Skeleton's (spec :236-249 has none of these fields; P7.T4 names them in excalidraw.rs:22-26 and report decision 10), so only a spec extension the user chooses can close them, as already ruled for Mermaid (the P7.T3 delegate ruling on parity); arrowhead meaning is the one loss the Skeleton can express, and the review's Important 1 already sends it to the fix loop; jq over the real board (read-only) shows what the check will meet: no rotation, opacity, group, dash or hatch fill, and no head-less or start-only arrow (so heads need the fixture and the Mermaid pair), while its two standalone headings at fontSize 36 and 24 come in at FigCraft's default 12 px (doc text.rs:285), its 30 sampled-curve edges come in straight and 8 of its 48 labels lose their colour: the losses the user will see first — costs if wrong: nothing in code; until the user chooses, migrated boards lose text sizes, curves, label colours and marker kinds on screen (and dashes, widths, rotation, opacity and groups where a board has them), while the untouched .excalidraw keeps them all; adding fields later is the follow-up the P7.T3 ruling sized (150-250 lines with tests), best before P8.T1 deletes the web canvas that shows the original look.
- Ruling: P7-X3 (controller; overrides the Plan 1 final review delegate's "no change" on M1) — P7.T8 adds a write-target guard: (1) ops::refused (the command op, and command.batch steps at any depth) refuses a path-taking engine command — file.export, file.exportSvg and file.exportFramesPdf with `path`; file.exportLayers and data.fill with `dir`; any other id at rev 0971d09 that writes to a caller-given path — when its target is a .figcraft or .excalidraw file or one of their .bak, .lossy.bak or .tmp siblings (for a `dir`, when the files it would write match those names), answering {ok:false, error:"<id> would overwrite a canvas file"}; (2) sawit-mcp's canvas_export (crates/mcp/src/app.rs) refuses the same targets for output_path on the app and headless paths. Tests: an export aimed at .nyawit/canvas.figcraft is refused and the file is byte-identical afterwards; the same export to a .png/.svg path still writes. P7.T8's files gain crates/mcp/src/app.rs — why: the spec's never-lose-data intent (D5, Review Focus) and a reasonable user's expectation; offline (P7.T5's headless path) nothing holds another copy, so one mistaken agent export destroys a board, and the guard keeps every legitimate export — costs: ~15 lines and two tests; an agent can no longer write image bytes to a path named *.figcraft.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T5) ⚠️ headless saver vs P7.T6's R-D14 API: run_and_save (crates/mcp/src/app.rs:511-525) saves without BoardDoc::refuse_dropping_images and decides 'changed' and the .bak flag from doc() instead of document_itself(), and the T5 and T6 reports each hand it to the other → dismiss: no P7.T5 change now; owner FINAL.P7 (CARRY): with P7.T5 and P7.T6 both on main, run_and_save compares board.document_itself() before and after the op instead of doc(), and only when it changed calls board.refuse_dropping_images(file) before to_bytes (its Err, the engine's sentence, is the tool error and nothing is written), with the .bak flag from store::is_empty of the document itself; RED-first tests in crates/mcp: (1) an offline canvas_command board.sticky on a canvas.figcraft whose images were left in the file (record it with DocState::with_images_left_in_file as P7.T6's app test does, or open a real past-budget archive) is a tool error carrying the refusal sentence and the file is byte-identical, while canvas_get on it still answers its layers; (2) an offline canvas_command version.preview of a canvas.figcraft that holds a saved version writes nothing (inode and mtime unchanged, no .bak, as offline_get_and_other_reads_write_nothing checks); FINAL.P7's review lists it as an Important cross-lane seam finding unless main already has it, and its fix wave makes it — lane 2's base a474bcc has neither BoardDoc::refuse_dropping_images nor document_itself, and neither loads versions nor records images left in a file (all P7.T6: lane-3 fdb071e crates/board/src/doc.rs:88-160, not on main), so no test can go RED then GREEN on lane 2 and its fixers can't rebase; nothing fails to compile when both land, so no integrator sees it; FINAL.P7 is the first node with both on main (it depends on P7.T5, P7.T6, P7.T8 and P7.T7) and the program's last task waits for it; until then the path is behind SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS (P7-C5), and R9's .lossy.bak, written at open for a file with images left in it (store.rs:77-86, kept by P7.T6), holds the original — costs if wrong: if FINAL.P7 misses it, an offline edit of a project canvas holding images past FigCraft's load budget drops them from the file (only the .lossy.bak keeps them), and an offline version.preview rewrites an unchanged canvas and, for an empty version, replaces an older .bak with the current file (keep_backup_before_emptying); the fix is about 5 lines and two tests in app.rs.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T5) ⚠️ P7-X3 order: canvas_command and canvas_commands put file.export, file.exportSvg, file.exportFramesPdf, file.exportLayers and data.fill (path or dir) in agents' hands on the app and headless paths before P7.T8's write-target guard exists → dismiss: no P7.T5 change; the guard stays P7.T8's under P7-X3, and P7.T8 also adds (CARRY) one offline test through sawit-mcp's canvas_command_tool (native on, tempdir project and AppPaths root, no app): after board.sticky, canvas_command file.exportSvg {path: <project>/.nyawit/canvas.figcraft} is a tool error saying file.exportSvg would overwrite a canvas file, the file is byte-identical and canvas_get still answers the sticky; if canvas_command_tool isn't on P7.T8's base yet, FINAL.P7 adds that test; FINAL.P7 checks that the guard sits in ops::refused, where both of T5's paths reach it — T5's headless runner and the app's control branch both answer through ops::run (lane-2 app.rs:511-513; main crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:245-247 via control.rs:327-328), whose command op checks ops::refused before it runs anything (main ops.rs:572-573, :639), so P7-X3's guard covers canvas_command on both paths without T5 code; reproduced on lane-2's built sawit-mcp (scratch SAWIT_HOME and project, no app): canvas_commands lists all five ids, and canvas_command file.exportSvg aimed at .nyawit/canvas.figcraft answered ok:true, replaced the 1,024-byte board with 879 bytes of SVG, left no .bak, and canvas_get then failed with 'not a valid FigCraft file', which the carried test pins; the order already holds: the hole needs SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS (P7-C5), the flag removal (Plan 8 Task 1) and the program's last task (Plan 8 Task 4) both depend on P7.T8, and nothing is pushed during the run — costs if wrong: if P7.T8's guard misses a path or the order is broken by hand, one mistaken offline export destroys a project board with no copy; the carried test is about 15 lines.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T5) PLAN-CONFLICT canvas_commands takes an unrequested filter and without it answers each command's id and label only (crates/mcp/src/app.rs:266-272,460-478,981-991; test :1425), against the brief's canvas_commands {project?} -> op commands through ok(pretty(..)) → dismiss: the code stays and the brief is amended to canvas_commands {filter?, project?} -> op commands: without filter every listed command's {id, label}; with filter (a case-insensitive part of an id or label; blank is none) the matching commands with their params; the commands op and its wire shape {ok, commands:[{id, label, params}]} are unchanged; CARRY to P8.T2: the final canvas_commands and canvas_command descriptions and the canvas instruction line keep filter and say that the parameters come with a filter — reproduced on lane-2's built sawit-mcp (scratch SAWIT_HOME and project, no app): 558 commands; the whole list with params is 106,024 bytes even as compact JSON (pretty is larger), over the 100,000-byte clip every tool answer goes through (lib.rs:67,113-127), and it ends inside command 532, data.fill, so the commands the engine registers last never reach an agent: positions 534-558, the 18 board.* ids (board.sticky to board.template and board.templates) and the board tool.* ids; id and label alone are 28,404 bytes, whole; filter board. answers the 18 with params in 4,208 bytes; spec D9 names canvas_commands but fixes no parameters or output shape, and its purpose (the full engine becomes reachable) is what the literal tool defeats; the socket's reply has no cap (app_control.rs:310-322) — costs if wrong: if the controller rules the other way, delete CanvasCommandsArgs.filter and command_list (about 30 lines and one test) and pass the reply through pretty; agents then get a list cut inside data.fill with every board command missing.
- Ruling: (controller) FINAL.P7 MUST list as an Important finding — unless main already has it — the headless-saver seam from the delegate ruling "(delegate, P7.T5) ⚠️ headless saver vs P7.T6's R-D14 API": run_and_save (crates/mcp/src/app.rs) compares document_itself() before/after, calls refuse_dropping_images(file) before to_bytes when changed, and takes the .bak flag from the document itself; its fix wave makes it with that ruling's two RED-first tests — P8.T1 removes the SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS gate, after which the offline path is live; P8.T4 (and so the push) waits for FINAL.P7 — costs: one fix-wave item.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T6) ⚠️ P7.T5's headless save (lane-2 1cd4cc4 crates/mcp/src/app.rs:511-526, run_and_save) opens with store::open, compares doc() and saves to_bytes through the Writer without refuse_dropping_images, so once both land an offline canvas op saves over a .figcraft whose images the loader left out and drops them, against P7-C1 (2); it should also compare document_itself() (report Concern 2) → dismiss: no P7.T6 change and no second owner: this is the seam already ruled in "(delegate, P7.T5) ⚠️ headless saver vs P7.T6's R-D14 API" (owner FINAL.P7: document_itself() before and after, refuse_dropping_images(file) before to_bytes, the .bak flag from the document itself, two RED-first tests in crates/mcp) and in the controller's ruling that FINAL.P7 must list it as an Important finding unless main has it; this ruling adds one constraint: P7.T6's fix round keeps BoardDoc::refuse_dropping_images(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<(), BoardError> and BoardDoc::document_itself(&self) -> Result<Arc<Document>, BoardError> public with these signatures, because FINAL.P7's fix calls them — why: read at 1cd4cc4 and fdb071e, the gap is real but not T6's: T6's own in-place saver compares the document itself and refuses (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:424-428) and publishes both calls for the headless saver (crates/board/src/doc.rs); lane 2's base a474bcc has neither, a fixer works on its own lane and an integrator only rebases, and FINAL.P7 is the first node with both on main (it depends on P7.T5, P7.T6, P7.T7 and P7.T8) — costs if wrong: none beyond those two rulings'; if T6's fix round renamed either call, FINAL.P7's fix wave adapts one call site.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T6) ⚠️ P7-C9's one-view-per-file routing (crates/app/src/workspace.rs open_board_file :602-640, board_tab :643-651, open_canvas_of's reuse :2087-2090) has no test that drives it; only the pure canvas_project is tested (:2737), and no test can build a Workspace → dismiss: no P7.T6 change; CARRY to P7.T7 as a live check after Step 1 (R-LIVE, R-LAUNCH, unattended over the control socket): with copies of the Task 4 fixture at $S/proj/x.excalidraw (no x.figcraft beside it) and $S/proj/.nyawit/canvas.excalidraw, send {"cmd":"open","path":...} for $S/proj/x.excalidraw, $S/proj/x.figcraft, $S/proj/.nyawit/canvas.excalidraw and $S/proj/.nyawit/canvas.figcraft in that order (absolute paths under the canonical path of the run's scratch folder), then {"cmd":"open_page","page":"canvas"}, then {"cmd":"state"}; expected: exactly one tab of kind "file" (path $S/proj/x.figcraft, title x.figcraft), exactly one tab of kind "canvas", and no other tab for either file; write the answer into the plan file (R5), and report a second tab for one file as a finding — why: read at fdb071e, every direction reaches one view: both names bind to one .figcraft (store::bound_file), a project's canvas file goes to open_canvas_of (canvas_project :2719-2729), which reuses the canvas\0<root> tab and then any tab whose BoardView::path() is that file, and any other file reuses its tab through board_tab; the paths compare equal because cli::absolute canonicalises (crates/core/src/cli.rs:242-264) and project roots are canonical; Workspace::new is called only from crates/app/src/cli.rs and workspace.rs has no #[gpui_kit::test], so driving the routing in a test needs a Workspace harness no task owns, while the control socket's open runs the same open_file path (crates/app/src/cli.rs:554-573) — costs if wrong: until P7.T7 runs, a routing regression would give one file two views, each saving over the other's edits (P7-C9's hazard); the check is about ten socket messages.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T6) ⚠️ FigCraft's own save also refuses to overwrite a file changed on disk since it was opened or saved (refuse_overwriting_changes, engine cmd/file/save.rs:117; stamped at open, cmd/file/open.rs:87 and :105), but P7-C1 names only the images refusal, so under D14 a .figcraft changed outside while open in a Sawit tab is last-writer-wins and the next autosave silently replaces the outside change → fix, in P7.T6's fix round beside M2's write_atomic (crates/board/src/store.rs:217,306-320; crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:177-178): every in-place save of a BoardView gets FigCraft's guard (#1330): BoardView::open stamps the file (length and modified time from std::fs::metadata, as FigCraft's library::stat, engine assets/library.rs:552; it and DocState.on_disk are pub(crate) at 0971d09, so Sawit keeps its own) before it reads it, as FigCraft stamps before reading; the view's Writer refuses a write when it holds a stamp for the path and the file's stamp now differs (no stamp, or no file, writes as now), records the written file's stamp after each of its own writes so the canvas's own saves never trip it, and makes the refusal the save's error, which the autosave toasts (mod.rs:467-473) and a flush logs, in FigCraft's sentence with Sawit's way out: '<stem> wasn't saved: the file changed on disk since it was opened or saved here (another app or window wrote to it), and saving would replace those changes. Close its tab and open it again to load them, or save it under another name to keep this version as well.'; Writer::save(path, bytes, empty) keeps working unstamped for P7.T5's headless path; RED-first tests: in store.rs a stamped path rewritten from outside is not written and keeps the outside bytes, a writer's consecutive saves of its own path all go through, an unstamped path writes; in the app a canvas whose .figcraft is rewritten from outside, then edited and flushed, leaves the outside bytes, of two views of one file the second to save is refused, and an imported or migrated board (no file at open) still writes at its first flush; note for P6.T3: a Save As whose target is the canvas's own bound file saves through the view (a flush), never a second writer, or the guard refuses the view's next save; FINAL.P7 may give P7.T5's headless saver the same stamp, taken at its store::open, which also covers T5's deferred minor on two sawit-mcp processes losing an update — why: P7-C1 rules that R-D14 'keeps what it can and refuses what it can't, as FigCraft's own file.save does … exactly the engine's own save rule', and FigCraft's in-place save and auto-save run both refusals back to back (FileSnapshot::save, save.rs:91-92; test auto_save_leaves_changes_made_on_disk_alone, engine save_disk_changes_tests.rs:68), of which P7-C1 carried one (its pre-flight read save.rs:41-115); D14's 'the engine supports this for free' includes the guard; Sawit's other tabs for file-tree files never silently replace an outside change (the editor polls the file's mtime every 2 s, reloads a clean buffer and warns a dirty one, crates/app/src/editor/mod.rs:172-260), so T6's 'two windows can still each open the same file, as with editors' (report decision 13) isn't as with editors: two windows' canvases of one file each save over the other's edits, the hazard P7-C9 was ruled to stop; nothing else keeps the outside version (the .bak is made only before an emptying save, the .lossy.bak only at an open with notes); the controller's P7-X3 guards a canvas file against silent loss on the same never-lose-data grounds; D5's mechanics stay (300 ms, writer thread, newest of the canvas's own saves wins, tmp + rename, .bak before emptying), and the guard only refuses where today's canvas (crates/app/src/canvas.rs:530-540) silently lost an outside write — costs if wrong: about 40-60 lines and 5 tests in store.rs and mod.rs, removable as one change; a file whose modified time changes with the same bytes (touch, a sync tool) makes its tab refuse until it is reopened, as FigCraft does, and until P6.T3 lands Save As reopening is the only way out and drops the canvas's newer edits; a later follow-up could reload a canvas with no unsaved edits, as the editor does.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T6) ⚠️ every in-place autosave now encodes the whole version history and branches on the UI thread (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:429, then doc.rs to_bytes, save_bytes_with_history), while FigCraft encodes off its UI thread (FileSnapshot::encode, engine cmd/file/save.rs:75), so a file with a long history may stall edits → dismiss: no P7.T6 change; measured now rather than later (release build of a scratch timer of exactly T6's save_bytes_with_history(doc, None, versions, []) call, figcraft-format 0971d09 with Sawit's Cargo.lock, load average 3-4.5 from the lanes): FigCraft's example ridgeline-app (349 nodes, 0.28 MB of document JSON) saves in 2.3 ms alone, +12 ms with 20 versions as read from a file and +64 ms with 100; versions made in the session (Version::new snapshots, such as an agent's file.saveVersion, serialized again on each save) +21 / +107 ms; ridgeline-studio-dashboard (269 nodes) +7 / +37 ms read, +13 / +64 ms made; CARRY to P8.T4 (release, quiet machine, R-LOAD), after Step 3: run <the run's scratch folder>/p7t6-delegate/savecost/savecost <the budget board's canvas.figcraft> 20 100 (it builds the versions itself and prints the save time alone and with 20 and 100 versions, read from a file and made in the session; rebuild it with cargo build --release --offline in that folder if the binary is gone), write the numbers into the plan file (R5), and add to the spec's Risks, as Step 3 does for a missed budget, the follow-up these numbers already call for at FigCraft's 100-version cap: in-place saves encode on store.rs's writer thread (hand it the document itself, versions and branches, as FigCraft's FileSnapshot does), which also carries the P1.T7 thumbnail follow-up — why: the UI-thread encode is Plan 1's design (P1.T5), which the P1.T7 delegate ruling kept because moving it changes the newest-save-wins core, and T6 adds only what P7-C1 (1) rules; FigCraft keeps 20 autosave checkpoints (version.autosave, every 30 minutes) and at most 100 versions (MAX_VERSIONS, figcraft-format lib.rs:58), so a usual file adds about one 60 Hz frame at the spec's 500-node size, inside its 50 ms re-render budget, while one near the cap or a design several times larger adds 50-100 ms or more at each autosave; nothing is lost either way — costs if wrong: until the follow-up, a FigCraft file with a long history or a large design hitches for tens of milliseconds up to a few tenths of a second about 300 ms after each pause in editing, several times more in debug builds (figcraft-format and miniz_oxide aren't at opt-level 3 in dev, Cargo.toml:34-73); the follow-up is about 30-50 lines and two tests in store.rs and mod.rs.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T6) ⚠️ until P6.T3 lands Save As (⇧⌘S still says 'Coming soon'), edits to a file with images left in it can't be kept, while the refusal toast says 'Save it under another name' (report Concern 1) → dismiss: no change and no hand check, because the order already holds: P8.T1 depends on P6.T3 directly and through P7.T8 (.superpowers/sdd/native-canvas-analysis/dag.json; P7-X2), so Save As lands before the cutover, and until then the native canvas is behind SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS (D6) and nothing is pushed; T6 pins that a save to any other path isn't refused (crates/board/src/store.rs:582-584 at fdb071e), and P6.T3 writes Save As with to_bytes to the chosen path (the P6.T1 delegate ruling), so it keeps such edits as a copy, as FigCraft's sentence promises; the disk-change refusal ruled above for P7.T6 offers the same way out and relies on the same order — why: the refusal needs a file holding images past FigCraft's load budget (20,000 images or 4 GiB, figcraft-format lib.rs:66), which only FigCraft writes, and the window is a flagged build before P6.T3 lands, in which the file and the .lossy.bak made when it opened keep everything that was in it — costs if wrong: none beyond that window; if the graph changed so that P8.T1 no longer waited for P6.T3, the cutover would ship both refusals with reopening as the only way out, which drops a refused canvas's edits, and the item would join P8.T4's hand checks.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T8) ⚠️ WRITES_TO_PATH (crates/board/src/ops.rs:97-104) completeness at rev 0971d09, and `path` as the parameter of variable.export, component.playground and codeConnect.export, can't be verified from the diff → dismiss: no change. The list is complete and the parameter names are right. I grepped the engine checkout at 0971d09. Every engine write goes through file_access::write (automation/file_access.rs:287). Its callers are cmd/file/load.rs:31's write and component.playground (assets/playground.rs:75-78, string(p, "path")). load.rs's write is called by: file.export (cmd/file/export.rs:106, plus its fig and penpot branches :125 and :136, all from `path`); file.exportSvg (cmd/file.rs:133, which is file.export with format svg); file.exportFramesPdf (:395); file.exportLayers (:348, writing `dir` + jobs_for's `<layer><suffix>.<ext>`, :274-277, where ext is png|jpg|svg|pdf|webp|xml, so it is never a canvas name); file.save and file.saveAs (save.rs:93 and :186, both HANDLED_BY_SAWIT); variable.export (cmd/variables_io.rs:54-56, string(p, "path")); and codeConnect.export (dev/code_connect.rs:613 and :632, text(cmd, p, "path")). data.fill writes through file.export into `<dir>/<stem>.<format>`, and it checks format against png|jpg|webp|svg|pdf (content/datafill.rs:47-50, :200-201). The only other writes are plugin installs into the app's install_dir (extensions/plugin_directory.rs:153-182), which nothing in Sawit's crates/ sets, and the writers in figcraft-mcp and ui-egui, which Sawit doesn't depend on (root Cargo.toml:21-27). The lane's built sawit-mcp (target/debug, 4c1f01e) gave a tool error for canvas_command file.exportSvg aimed at .nyawit/canvas.figcraft and left the file byte-identical. The one way past the check is a {"$step"|"$ref"} reference used as a batch step's `path` (engine automation/batch.rs:101-160): ops::refused reads the unresolved value. But no engine command answers with the canvas's path, because Sawit's DocState has none (crates/board/src/doc.rs:105). So an agent only gets there by typing the canvas path into an earlier step on purpose. That is outside P7-X3's mistaken-export case, and the shell can do it anyway — costs if wrong: a writer I missed could overwrite a board offline. the_guarded_writers_are_the_audited_engine_commands (ops.rs:1591) catches only renames, so a FigCraft rev bump has to redo this grep (rg -n 'file_access::write|cmd::file::write|super::file::write' crates/engine/src).
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T8) ⚠️ a_headless_export_of_text_has_glyphs can't go RED on this Mac, so the font-scan call isn't pinned by a test → fix: the Mac isn't the problem. The assertion is too weak to fail. Rewrite the test so it fails without the scan. I measured with the lane's own built sawit-mcp (4c1f01e) on the headless path, with a scratch SAWIT_HOME and no app. A sticky reading 'Hello world' exports 5,317 bytes with the scan. With FIGCRAFT_NO_SYSTEM_FONTS=1 it exports 879 bytes, byte-identical to a sticky with no text, and its text path is d="". Both exports have 2 <path elements, so paths >= 2 (crates/mcp/src/app.rs:1656) holds either way. Sawit embeds no font: CRAFT_FONTS_DIR is unset, so figcraft-text's build.rs:35 leaves CRAFT_FONTS empty. Every other scan call in sawit-board is inside a #[cfg(test)] module (design/typography.rs:315 and :338, input.rs:1546, overlay.rs:2386). So in sawit-mcp's test process, app.rs:505 is the only scan, and without it the text has no face. The fixer should assert that the text's glyph path has data: the SVG has no d="" path, and it is longer than an export of the same sticky with empty text. Then show the test RED with app.rs:505 commented out, and GREEN with it back — why: Plan 2 ruling (5) asks for a test that a headless export of a text layer has glyphs, and this test passes with no glyphs at all — costs if wrong: one fix round of about 10 test lines. After P8.T1 registers embedded Inter in headless(), text has glyphs without the scan, and the test then guards register() as much as the scan. That still meets (5), which calls the scan 'harmless if headless text already finds a font'.
- Ruling: (delegate, P7.T8) ⚠️ the carried offline canvas_command_tool test uses the native(), board_file() and canvas_command_tool helpers from the base, and the diff doesn't show them → dismiss: no change. They exist, unchanged, on both the lane base 8e63845 and main df633b9 (crates/mcp/src/app.rs): canvas_command_tool at :361; native(dir) at :1111, which calls with_native_canvas(true) on a Sawit whose AppPaths root is under dir; board_file(project) at :1123, which gives <project>/.nyawit/canvas.figcraft; write_board at :1128; said at :1146. The test is an_export_never_overwrites_the_canvas_file (lane app.rs:1570), and it does what the P7.T5 carry asks: native on, tempdir project and home, no app. file.exportSvg aimed at .nyawit/canvas.figcraft is a tool error reading 'file.exportSvg would overwrite a canvas file', the file stays byte-identical, and canvas_get still answers the sticky. I reproduced it on the lane's built sawit-mcp over stdio (scratch SAWIT_HOME, SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS=1): same error with isError true, the file's shasum unchanged, and canvas_get listing the sticky — costs if wrong: nothing past a compile error, which the gates catch.
- Ruling: (controller, 2026-10-31) FINAL.P7 MUST list as Important findings — unless main already has them — and its fix wave fixes, these two halves of one data-loss gap carried from FINAL.P4's load-bearing residual ruling (Plan 4 ledger, "Final review: Ruling: (delegate) The eye, padlock, drop and page actions …"): (1) the layers panel's page_action, toggle_visible, toggle_locked and drop_rows end an open canvas text edit (text.edit.end) before their command, through one helper, as select_layers already does (crates/app/src/board/layers_panel.rs), with that ruling's GPUI test (two pages, a character typed in a text on the first, select the second page's row, then a Move-tool click on a layer there: the character survives and ds.interaction is None); (2) the command op (crates/board/src/ops.rs command arm, and command.batch steps) ends an open text edit before page.select or page.delete — any command that clears ds.text_edit without ending its interaction — with a test that an agent's page.select during a user's text edit keeps the typed character — why: typing, then switching page and clicking a layer silently loses the text, autosave writes the loss and no undo step holds it (spec never-lose-data intent, D5); Plan 4 had no second fix wave, and FINAL.P7 is the next reviewed fix wave before P8.T4 — costs: two small guards and two tests in FINAL.P7's wave.
- Ruling: (controller) R-TEXTEDIT in the program ledger's Cross-plan rulings binds this plan's final fix wave: one BoardView helper ends an open canvas text edit before any non-text, non-clipboard command; FINAL.P6 creates it with its C1 fix, FINAL.P7 routes the layers panel and the command op through it.
- Final review: parked — On-screen parity of Mermaid and migrated boards (text sizes, dashes, widths, curves, label colours, marker kinds, fonts) (declined to judge: a spec Skeleton extension only the user can choose, P7.T3/P7.T4 delegate rulings; only the staleness of the hand checks that carry it was judged, I-4) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the spec gives the Skeleton today's MCP fields and no others (Agent wire contract: type, id, x, y, width, height, label.text, text, start.id, end.id, strokeColor, backgroundColor, roundness, plus sticky), so font size, label colour, dash, width, cap kinds and routes are a spec extension, which the P7.T3 (progress.md:140) and P7.T4 (:149) rulings rightly leave to the user; the staleness is fixed on main: e52cf5f rewrote hand checks 1 and 2 (plan :505-548) to compare with a build of 3c35377 in a checkout of its own (or excalidraw.com for check 2), with a SAWIT_HOME per run and a fresh board copy per native run (re-review: I-4 ADDRESSED), and P7.T7's notes put each difference in words (plan :400-456) — costs if wrong: until the user chooses, Mermaid and migrated boards draw 12 px text, ink labels, solid straight edges and one filled end cap where today's canvas draws Pele's sizes, colours, dashes and curves; the .excalidraw always stays byte-identical, but the first open of each of the user's old boards in a build from main writes canvas.figcraft beside it at the first autosave (P7-C8), and a later parity fix reaches that board only if its canvas.figcraft is moved aside, losing what was drawn since, so hand check 2 should be answered before the user opens a real board in a build from main.
- Final review: parked — Default fill of agent shapes with no backgroundColor: the engine's sticky yellow where today's Excalidraw agent shapes are transparent (declined to judge: the P7.T1 delegate ruled it under the spec's board.shape mapping; a look for the user's parity hand check) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the spec maps rectangle, ellipse and diamond to board.shape with a color, and its 'Transparent means no fill' is for a backgroundColor that is given, so the colour of one left out is the implementation's choice; the P7.T1 ruling (progress.md:121) pinned the engine's default sticky colour (crates/board/src/skeleton.rs:48-52, default_fill) so that one call draws the same in the app and headless whatever the user last picked, and an agent that wants no fill sends backgroundColor 'transparent'; no hand check shows it, though (Mermaid nodes and migrated shapes come with fills of their own), so the controller should add it to the parity choices it relays with hand check 1 — costs if wrong: every agent rectangle, ellipse and diamond without a backgroundColor is filled sticky-yellow where today's are outlines, and canvas_add_elements' text doesn't say so (Plan 8 Task 2's deferred minor, crates/mcp/src/app.rs:211); no fill by default is a few lines in skeleton.rs (a default for board.shape apart from the stickies' yellow) and one test.
- Final review: parked — Opening an .excalidraw from the Files tab writes its sibling <stem>.figcraft at the first autosave even with no edit (declined to judge: the plan's own Task 6 test and ruling P7-C8 decide it, D14) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: D14 makes the sibling the file an imported .excalidraw saves to, P7-C8 (progress.md:94) has an imported or migrated board with layers start with no saved baseline so that its first autosave or flush writes it (D5's 'imports it once'), and the plan's excalidraw_file_opens_bound_to_sibling expects the .figcraft after a flush with no edit (plan :319); main does exactly that and never writes the .excalidraw (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:277-297 and :329-336, test :1582) — costs if wrong: a look at an .excalidraw leaves an untracked <stem>.figcraft beside it, which from then on opens in its place without a word, even after the .excalidraw is edited elsewhere (review m-3); saving only after an edit is one condition in with_board for an import, at the price of importing again on every unedited open.
- Final review: parked — canvas_command runs ids the menu policy hides; only canvas_commands omits them (declined to judge: the Task 2 reading of P7-C11, accepted by its review and by Plan 1's parked M1 ruling: the hidden list binds menus and keys) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the spec's hidden list is a menu list that is blocked from the keyboard too (Menu policy, D7), D9 makes the full engine reachable through canvas_command, and P7-C11's why ('agents can call what it shows') holds, since listing and refusing share one predicate (crates/board/src/ops.rs:678-700, :781); I checked the hidden families in Sawit's embedding and none does harm: what opens, closes or switches documents is refused or rolled back (HANDLED_BY_SAWIT :73, OPENS_DOCUMENTS :90, Documents :817), the ones that ask the UI for a window end in handle_ui_requests' log arm (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:546; help.page asks for open.help, engine cmd/view.rs:449), file.rename only retitles the document (engine cmd/file.rs:193-196), the collab, library and plugin-directory commands need a link the engine never opens and Sawit never installs (engine sharing/live.rs:5-8; no figcraft-collab-net in main's Cargo.lock), plugins run in figcraft-plugin's sandbox with no file system or network, and path writes are P7-X3's guarded list — costs if wrong: an agent can do what the user can't reach from menus or keys (save a version, retitle the file, set default properties); refusing the hidden ids in ops::refused is a few lines, and P1.T6's tests that use file.saveVersion as a setup step would need another.
- Final review: parked — On the app path every canvas tool opens and activates the canvas tab (reads too) and acts on the window's active project's canvas rather than the project the request matched (declined to judge: the old canvas tools' behaviour, as today, outside Plan 7's diff) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, and the second half doesn't hold as written: a request that names a project picks the window holding it and makes that project the active one before the command runs (crates/app/src/cli.rs:577-599, 'Commands act on the project asked for'), so open_canvas acts on the matched project's canvas (crates/app/src/workspace.rs:2020-2023, workspace/control.rs:321-324); opening and activating the tab, and the frontmost window's active project when no project is named, are as before the program (0f5df5e control.rs:319-322, workspace.rs:1949-1958, cli.rs:596-598), and the spec asks only that the eight tools keep working — costs if wrong: an agent's read still brings the canvas forward (showing the centre over a maximized terminal) and switches the window's active project; with no project named, the app path draws on the frontmost window's active project where the headless path draws on the agent's working folder (crates/mcp/src/lib.rs:186-193), and a subfolder named as the project is its project's canvas in the app but gets a .nyawit/ of its own offline (T5-7's optional half); each is a few lines in sawit-mcp or control.rs once the user picks the rule.
- Final review: parked — canvas_get answers are clipped at 100 KB for boards of several hundred layers (declined to judge: the old canvas_get pretty-printed and clipped the same way; not a Plan 7 regression) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: every sawit-mcp tool's text goes through clip at MAX_OUTPUT, 100 000 bytes, which ends a cut text with a note of how much was shown (crates/mcp/src/lib.rs:67, :99-122), as before the program (0f5df5e lib.rs:67, app.rs:657-668), the spec sets no size for get's reply, and a native Summary carries fewer keys than the old offline summary (summarize_element, crates/mcp/src/app.rs), so at least as many layers fit — costs if wrong: on a board of more than about 500 top-level layers an agent gets a cut list, though the note tells it so; printing get without indentation, or paging it, is a few lines in canvas_get_tool.
- Final review: Ruling: (delegate) An agent op arriving during a held pointer drag joins the drag's gesture, and an Escape mid-drag cancels both (declined to judge: same family as R-TEXTEDIT, but the ruling names text edits and the window is a held mouse button) — load-bearing, and worse than the review says: a gesture's previews re-apply to the snapshot taken when it began (engine session/undo.rs:48-88, 'later preview calls re-apply edits to this snapshot'), a command run meanwhile changes only the current document and makes no undo step (exec.rs:225, undo.rs:278), the Move tool previews on every drag event past the slop (canvas/tools/drag.rs:235; resize :488 and :517, scale :617, shape drawing tools/draw.rs:41, the eraser boards/board/tools.rs:159), and the release of a press on a layer that didn't move cancels the gesture (tools/draw.rs:158-159); so an agent's add, mermaid, update, delete, clear, replace or command that lands while the user holds the button on the canvas, a click on a layer included, is taken back by the next move or by the release after it answered ok:true, which breaks the wire contract ('Failures look like {ok:false, error}') and the spec's Goal that the canvas tools keep working; BoardView::run_op ends only a Design-panel scrub and ops::run only a text edit first (crates/app/src/board/mod.rs:428-443, crates/board/src/ops.rs:203-209), so a held pointer is the last open path of the mechanism Plan 5's final review I1 fixed for a typed field value ('a drag's preview would take it back', crates/app/src/board/events.rs:55-59); smallest change: ops::run answers {ok:false, error:'<op>: finish the drag on the canvas first'} to add, update, delete, clear, replace, mermaid and command while board.session.tool.busy(), before it ends the text edit or runs anything (reads, exports and the pointer, key and type input still go through), as the engine's own tool.place refuses mid-drag ('finish the drag first', engine canvas/tools/keys.rs:79-81) and Sawit's context bar already reads busy() (crates/app/src/board/context_bar.rs:101); one sawit-board test: pointer down on a sticky and a move past the slop, then add answers ok:false and the page is unchanged; pointer up, then add answers ok:true and its layer survives a following drag (down, move, up); headless is unaffected (each call is a fresh session) — costs if wrong: about 10 lines and one test, and an agent retries for as long as a button is held; holding the op in BoardView until the release (Answer::Later, as browser ops answer) would spare the retry for about 30 lines.
- Final review: parked — Network fetches through canvas_command, the program carry 'no fetch without a user action' (declined to judge: checked, not a finding: font.download is refused unless the user turned downloading on in a Craft app's own window, Sawit never calls allow_downloads, and no collab-net crate is linked) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly, and the carry is closed: re-checked at rev 0971d09 and main e52cf5f: font.download is refused unless the font store's shared setting is on (engine fonts/download.rs:116-119), which only allow_downloads turns on, from the user's own click in a Missing fonts window (:76-84), and git grep finds no allow_downloads in Sawit's crates; the engine never opens a socket (sharing/live.rs:5-8: networking comes only through a link that figcraft-collab-net installs, and main's Cargo.lock has no figcraft-collab-net, so collab, library and plugin-directory commands have nothing to send through), figcraft-plugin runs plugins in a sandbox with no file system, network or clock, and nothing Sawit links from FigCraft or craft-libs spawns a process outside tests; craft-font-store's ureq is linked but reached only after that check — costs if wrong: an agent can start a font download only once the user has turned downloading on in another Craft app, whose setting is shared; the controller can mark the program ledger's carried item (figcraft pulls ureq/rustls for font download) done.
- Final review: parked — Gate results at main f0a61bd, not re-run (declined to judge: read-only review, no build; the fix wave should run the full gates on main from clean workspace fingerprints, task-7-report.md:296-305) — Ruling: (delegate) superseded since the review: the fix wave touched every tracked file under crates/ in lane 3 before building, and its logs show all four workspace crates compiled and checked from lane 3's own sources (scratchpad final-p7-fix/warmup.log, clippy.log, workspace-tests.log); at the final tree, committed unchanged as e52cf5f, cargo fmt --all -- --check printed nothing, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings was clean but for the known block v0.1.6 note, and cargo test --workspace passed 1309 with 9 ignored and 0 failed (final-fmt.log, final-clippy.log, final-workspace-tests.log, which the re-review read); main was fast-forwarded to that commit, e52cf5f, its HEAD now — costs if wrong: a failure hidden by stale fingerprints shows at FINISH STEPS 2-3, which re-run the full gates on the merged main before anything merges; nothing is pushed in between.
- Final review: parked — UI-thread encode of version history on each in-place autosave (declined to judge: ruled for P8.T4 to measure, P7.T6 delegate ruling) — Ruling: (delegate) set aside rightly: the P7.T6 ruling (progress.md:176) measured it under build load (a 349-node file: +12 ms with 20 versions read from a file, +64 ms with 100, +107 ms with 100 made in the session) and carried a release measurement to P8.T4, which hasn't run and has it as item (4) of the controller's P8.T4 CHECKLIST (Plan 8 progress.md:102), with the timer still built at scratchpad/p7t6-delegate/savecost/savecost; FigCraft caps a file at 100 versions — costs if wrong: an autosave of a file with a long history stalls the canvas for up to about 110 ms until in-place saves encode on store.rs's writer thread, the follow-up P8.T4 writes into the spec's Risks if its numbers call for it.
- Ruling: (controller) FINAL.P7's load-bearing residual (agent ops during a held drag) → routed to FINAL.P8's fix wave; see Plan 8 ledger.

## Plan 8 (cutover)

- Ruling: P8-C1 — In crates/app/Cargo.toml, keep wry, objc2-web-kit, objc2-app-kit and block2. Remove only include_dir, brotli-decompressor and the canvas-bundle comment. Step 4's rg adds --glob '!crates/app/src/browser.rs' --glob '!crates/app/src/browser/**', and accepts the wry dependency line and the objc2-web-kit comment in crates/app/Cargo.toml. Review Focus 3 becomes 'no include_dir, brotli or CanvasView; wry only in the browser'. Read the spec's 'the webview (wry) ... deleted' as the canvas's webview, and amend Plan 8 as the browser-preview-1 plan already requires. — why: The spec predates the browser merge, and its Risks section itself expects the browser to need wry. Deleting wry fails cargo build. This is the smallest change that still removes the canvas webview. — costs if wrong: None. The canvas stops using wry either way; the size report should say that wry stays for the browser.
- Ruling: P8-C2 — Keep Answer::Later, CONTROL_TIMEOUT and the Later branch in cli.rs. Reword them for the browser: the Answer doc to 'now, or once the browser page replies'; the CONTROL_TIMEOUT doc to 'a browser op'; the handle_control doc to 'browser ops'; the errors to 'The browser closed before answering.' and 'The browser page didn't answer in time. Is the Sawit window visible?'. — why: The plan's 'if unused' conditions resolve to keep. Only the canvas wording is stale. — costs if wrong: Wording only.
- Ruling: P8-C3 — Delete only sync_canvas's webview half: the canvases loop with set_visible and set_maximized. Keep the fill-window reset as sync_canvas_maximized, called from render where sync_canvas was. Rewrite its doc comment (this is 'the old :2014' comment: sync_canvas's doc at f16c833), and fix sync_browser's [Self::sync_canvas] doc link. maximize_still_works_without_webview also checks that after maximizing, toggling the sidebar or activating another tab makes canvas_maximized false and shows the tab bar. If a Workspace can't be built in a test, test a small pure toggle/reset helper with #[test], or add the check to P8.T4's hand-check. — why: Review Focus 4 requires the tab bar to come back. Only this reset clears canvas_maximized when the layout changes in other ways. — costs if wrong: About ten kept lines.
- Ruling: P8-C4 — Move page_key into workspace.rs's mod tests, and keep no_two_shortcuts_share_a_keystroke. — why: page_key stays in use, but only from tests. Under clippy --all-targets -D warnings it is dead code in the bin target. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C5 — After git rm -r crates/app/canvas web/canvas crates/app/src/canvas.rs, commit with `git add -u crates Cargo.toml Cargo.lock`. The git rm has already staged web/canvas. — why: Reproduced: once web/ is gone, the pathspec 'web' matches nothing and git add aborts. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C6 — Task 1 Step 4's expected result also allows the MCP tool and argument descriptions (Task 2's test is their gate) and the ensure() test fixture in crates/core/src/nyawit.rs. With C1 and C7, the pattern becomes `wry|include_dir|brotli|CanvasView|canvas_passthrough|web/canvas|pele\.ts|Excalidraw|excalidraw`, with the browser globs added. — why: The plan puts the MCP wording after Task 1, and the fixture is unrelated test data. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C7 — Task 1 also: sets canvas_open from the live boards; deletes CanvasTab entirely, and P1.T6's native_flag_opens_board_view (superseded by canvas_opens_native_board); makes P7.T6's .figcraft/.excalidraw open_file branch unconditional; re-points every crate::canvas:: path (BoardView's EventEmitter, board/top_bar.rs) to crate::board::; and rewords the doc comments at crates/core/src/app_control.rs:201 and crates/core/src/mermaid.rs:18-19. Add those files to Task 1's file list. — why: Each one either stops compiling once the flag and the module go, or leaves a doc comment that points at deleted code. — costs if wrong: Small.
- Ruling: P8-C8 — If a Workspace can't be built in a test, skip the three GPUI tests, as Plan 1 C8 did; Task 1's red/green then comes from the gates. Before the budget run, Task 4 Step 3 adds: with no SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS, open_page canvas; state shows an active tab of kind 'canvas'; and {"cmd":"canvas","op":{"op":"get"}} answers ok:true at once, well under the old 30 s. Task 4 Step 5 adds '⇧⌘↩ maximizes, and ⌘B or switching tabs brings the tab bar back'. A GPUI test that does build a Workspace must point the canvas at a temp project, never the real app root. — why: No existing test builds a Workspace. Workspace::new and open_canvas read the real AppPaths, so a test could touch the user's own canvas file. — costs if wrong: None. The checks still run, live. — Amended by the controller: the '⇧⌘↩ maximizes …' Step 5 check is driven over the control socket where possible; what needs hands goes on the hand-check list (R-LIVE).
- Ruling: P8-C9 — Change app_open_page's description, not PAGES, to 'canvas (whiteboard and design canvas)'. Reword the canvas_get, canvas_add_elements, canvas_add_mermaid and canvas_load descriptions too. canvas_tools_describe_the_native_canvas serialises every tool's description and input_schema, plus INSTRUCTIONS, and asserts that no 'Excalidraw' appears outside canvas_load (whose lowercase .excalidraw mentions are file extensions). The canvas_* instruction line says that the tools also work on the saved file when the app is closed. — why: The test forces the full rename only if it reads the input schemas, and PAGES feeds variant()'s error message. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C10 — Add a short '## Credits' section to README.md with the FigCraft line and 'see NOTICE'. Reword the docs/media/canvas.png alt text, and leave the demo GIF caption alone. Relabel the MCP row 'Canvas', and say that the tools work on the saved file when the app is closed. — why: This matches D16's 'README credits' and the result Task 3 Step 1 expects from rg. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C11 — After FigCraft's NOTICE text, append FigCraft's LICENSE-MIT verbatim (the copyright line and permission notice), under a line saying that Sawit uses FigCraft under the MIT licence. Shipping NOTICE inside Sawit.app is a follow-up outside this plan. — why: D16's purpose is licence compliance. MIT's condition is that the permission notice ships with copies, and FigCraft's NOTICE doesn't contain it. — costs if wrong: About 20 lines of text, trivial to drop later.
- Ruling: P8-C12 — Rely on Plan 1 C3/C4 (the log dependency and a RUST_LOG-gated logger in main.rs); if they weren't applied, Task 4 stops and reports it. Launch with `SAWIT_HOME=$S/home RUST_LOG=sawit=debug target/release/sawit --foreground "$S/proj" 2>"$S/render.log" &` and record PID=$!. Bring the window forward with {"cmd":"open_page","page":"canvas","project":"$S/proj"}. Add a stop step, /bin/kill $PID, before Step 6. — why: An app launched from a terminal relaunches itself with stderr sent to /dev/null, and the memory recipe says to stop only your own instance, with /bin/kill. — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C13 — Run {op:'command', command:'board.templates'}, then board.template {name:<id>} 10 times, cycling through the ids. Then repeat select.all and edit.duplicate until node.find {limit:0} reports total ≥ 500, and record both the node total and get's top-level count. Hide the sidebar and terminal with the layout command, and record the canvas's device-pixel size next to the numbers. Synthetic scrolling can't reach the window, so ask the user to pan the board during Step 5 and record whether the frames keep up. — why: This keeps the spec's budget definition (500 nodes), and covers its pan item with the only input that reaches the window. — costs if wrong: None. — Amended by the controller: the pan-the-board step goes on the plan's hand-check list (program rule R-LIVE) instead of waiting for the user mid-run.
- Ruling: P8-C14 — Add Step 7: `git add docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-29-native-canvas-8-cutover.md`, plus docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-native-canvas-design.md if a Risks note was added. Commit with 'docs: record the native canvas budget, release size and final checks'. — why: Lanes integrate only commits. Plan 1 Task 0 committed its baseline the same way (e865229). — costs if wrong: None.
- Ruling: P8-C15 — sawit-mcp's canvas() helper (crates/mcp/src/app.rs) turns a reply whose ok is false into Err(error), for both the app path and P7.T5's headless path. The control socket keeps answering with the op value, as P1.T6 specifies. Check this at Plan 7's pre-flight; no change is needed if P7.T5 already does it. — why: It keeps canvas_* failures as MCP tool errors, as they were with the web canvas (D9: existing agents keep working). — costs if wrong: A few lines in P7.T5. If the socket did the mapping instead, P7.T5's offline path would need the same mapping anyway. — Assigned by the controller: P7.T5 implements this mapping on both paths (Plan 7 ruling X1).

Nothing is pushed until cutover'; spec :293), and every canvas_* tool body goes through Sawit::canvas (app.rs:847-1005 at 1a3e59e; canvas_get through canvas_get_stored :378), so once canvas() has no gate they are exact for all of them; the removal is already P8.T1's and reaches its dispatch: Plan 7 ruling P7-C5 ('Plan 8 Task 1 removes the gate and adds crates/mcp/src/{lib.rs,app.rs} to its files'), the program ledger's cross-plan ruling 'Plan 7 C5 / Plan 8 C7', and P8.T1's dag.json entry, which its implementer prompt tells it to read ('crates/mcp/src/lib.rs (added: Plan 7 C5 gate removal)'; dag.md :127, :178); P8.T1 is in flight (run wf_6ecd7f0b-036, opus, lane 2), its transcript reads P7-C5, and lane 2's uncommitted tree (05:28-05:37, read only) already does it: no SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS or native_canvas anywhere under crates/, Sawit is {paths, tool_router} (lib.rs:94-97), new reads no environment, canvas() runs headless whenever no app is running (app.rs:332-338), offline canvas_get is canvas_get_stored alone (:347-352), the flag-off test is deleted and both native() helpers are a plain Sawit::new; cargo test -p sawit-mcp re-runs on the combined code when the second of P8.T1 and P8.T2 integrates (integrator step 3; both touch crates/mcp) and again in P8.T4 Step 6 (cargo test --workspace); the binary path (the sawit-mcp symlink, invoked_as_mcp, AppPaths::default_macos) reads no flag, so a live MCP step would find nothing the tests miss; until P8.T1 lands, main already makes the same claim in README's Canvas row (README.md:481, P8.T3 under P8-C10), and nothing is pushed before P8.T4 — costs if wrong: if P8.T1 kept the gate, a default sawit-mcp would answer every canvas tool except canvas_get with 'the app isn't running' while Sawit is closed, and canvas_get would read the legacy canvas.excalidraw instead of canvas.figcraft, against three texts (instruction line, launch_app, README); - Ruling: (delegate, P8.T1) ⚠️ Review Focus 2 and 4 live, and the two GPUI tests skipped under P8-C8 (canvas_opens_native_board, canvas_ops_answer_now) → dismiss: no P8.T1 gap, nothing enters the fix loop; report, TDD evidence); an app test can't pin the call: register() runs once per process (OnceLock, fonts.rs:36), and the sawit test binary reaches it from the family-picker test and from each test that runs bind_app_keys, which calls workspace::init and so board::init (board/mod.rs:2848-2850, workspace.rs:145-147), so a test that board::init registers Inter passes with the line deleted whenever another test registered first; P2.T7 saw [] before the embed), and in the app process board::init is the only caller of register() (headless(), mcp app.rs:506, runs in sawit-mcp's own process), so a non-empty list pins board::init's call on the shipped binary; the hand check stays for the look — costs if wrong: if the call were lost, Inter text on a Mac without Inter would lose bold, italic and weights on the canvas while the document keeps them (the P2.T7 gap); P8.T4's socket check catches that on the release build before anything is pushed, and the fix is the one line in board::init.
- Ruling: (delegate, P8.T1) ⚠️ the P8.T2 delegate's guard (git grep -n -e SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS -e native_canvas <rev> -- crates) prints nothing at 21afee5, but after the rebase onto main it also matches P8.T2's test name canvas_tools_describe_the_native_canvas, so P8.T4's check should use -w → dismiss: no P8.T1 gap, nothing enters the fix loop; the carried guard (rulings.md:42) is re-worded for P8.T4 as git grep -n -w -e SAWIT_NATIVE_CANVAS -e native_canvas -e with_native_canvas <rev> -- crates, which must print nothing; the carry's other two checks stay as worded — why: reproduced on a trial merge (git merge-tree --write-tree main 21afee5: exit 0, tree 863ae7f; it touches no checkout): the plain guard prints exactly one line, crates/mcp/src/lib.rs:432, fn canvas_tools_describe_the_native_canvas (a substring, not the flag), and the re-worded guard prints nothing there and at 21afee5; -w alone, as the review suggests, would also stop catching the gate's setter, because native_canvas inside with_native_canvas isn't a whole word: on main, before P8.T1, it drops with_native_canvas at crates/mcp/src/lib.rs:232 and its calls at crates/mcp/src/app.rs:1130 and crates/mcp/tests/tools.rs:227 and :508; with the setter as a third pattern, the guard finds all 19 of the gate's lines on main (both fields, both env reads, the setter and its calls, the branches and the doc lines) and drops only P8.T2's test name (main lib.rs:444) — costs if wrong: none in code; worded as carried, P8.T4 would report a false hit or waive the guard, and with -w alone it would miss a leftover with_native_canvas call.

- Ruling: (controller, 2026-10-31) FINAL.P8 MUST list as an Important finding — unless main already has it — and its fix wave fixes the load-bearing residual from Plan 7's final review (Plan 7 ledger, "Final review: Ruling: (delegate) An agent op arriving during a held pointer drag …"): ops::run answers {ok:false, error:"<op>: finish the drag on the canvas first"} to add, update, delete, clear, replace, mermaid and command while board.session.tool.busy(), before it ends a text edit or runs anything (reads, exports and the pointer, key and type ops still go through), as the engine's own tool.place refuses mid-drag; one sawit-board test: pointer down on a sticky and a move past the slop, then add answers ok:false and the page is unchanged; pointer up, then add answers ok:true and its layer survives a following drag — why: an agent change made while the user holds the mouse button is taken back by the next move or the release after it answered ok:true, breaking the wire contract; after the cutover the native canvas is the only canvas, and FINAL.P8 is the last reviewed fix wave — costs: ~10 lines and one test; an agent retries while a button is held.
- Ruling: (delegate, P8.T4) ⚠️ R-LOAD names P8.T4 the authoritative budget measurement on a quiet machine, but its series ran at a 1-min load of 3.3-10.8 between disk-watchdog passes (plan :232-238) → dismiss: the numbers stand as P8.T4's budget record and R-LOAD's authoritative measurement for its three verdicts; no re-take in the fix loop and no spec change — why: load only lengthens render times, so both passes hold a fortiori (edit p95 40.5 ms; idle 0.2-1.7 % in ps and 0.3-0.7 % in top with no render line while sampling); the miss would need a 7.6-10.7x slowdown to flip, while each series started with 70-95 % of the CPU idle (top) and FigCraft's Retained draws a full frame through render_view in up to 8 rayon bands (render retained.rs:182, lib.rs:123-128), so others' 0.4-2.4 busy cores can stretch a render by tens of percent, not several times; under the same load the empty page took p95 15.4 ms and the effect-free board 64.7 ms, which puts 76-89 % of a settle on the shadows whatever the load; the 100 % renders repeat at 506-569 ms in two series at 1-min loads 6.3-8.8 (scratch live-P8.T4 series-cycles-1.txt, diag-board-1.txt); the notes record the load beside every series and say the machine wasn't quiet (plan :232-238 and the tables' vm.loadavg column), the labelling R-LOAD asked of P1.T7; the controller already logged these numbers and backed the watchdog off (program ledger, 2026-10-31 10:00); a re-take after the run's loops stop can't happen inside the fix loop, and the machine is attended and busy with the user's own work (a cf dev / vite dev server in site/ at 10:07, 1-min load 3.3, 7.9 GB free), so later isn't sure to be quieter; the magnitudes are upper bounds, which Important 2's fix already says of these numbers — costs if wrong: the spec's Risks magnitudes (537, 382 and 65 ms) may overstate a quiet machine's by tens of percent, and the effect-free 65 ms might come in at or under 50 ms, which would make the follow-up's 'then tiles' step unneeded; the follow-up measures before it tunes, and a quiet re-take of Step 3 with the kept binary and tools (scratchpad live-P8.T4/bin/sawit, tools/build_board.py, tools/series.py) takes about 20 min whenever the controller wants one, e.g. in FINISH STEPS.

so the record has no settle time for a 500-node board as boards are laid out, which is what the spec's budget is for (spec :257-263), what the report asks the user to weigh before releasing (Concern 1) and what the Risks follow-up will be sized on; Important 2's caveat gives the direction but no size and can be read either way; the pile's own numbers put a shadow near 2.7 ms at 100 % ((529.6 − 60.1) ms over the ~173 in view), and a laid-out board shows one template's 5-15 stickies at 100 % and all of them small at fit, so it may settle near the 50 ms budget rather than 7-11x over, which would change the verdict's headline; the series adds evidence of the kind the implementer already added (the 501-node page, the fit and edit series) and replaces nothing, so P8-C13 stands; the release binary (scratchpad live-P8.T4/bin/sawit, e52cf5f, checksum-matched) and the socket tools are kept, so it costs one launch — costs if wrong: one relaunch and about 30 min in the fix round, plus a table row and two sentences the controller can drop; if the laid-out board misses as badly, the record says so with a number instead of a caveat.

## After the program (2026-11-01)

- Ruling: a refusal the user caused speaks plain words, and a key with nothing to act on is quiet — the engine's `Disabled` refusal is its own `BoardError::Unavailable`, whose message (what an agent's `{ok:false,error}` carries) stays the engine's; `BoardError::for_user` drops the advice to agents ("(pass `ids`)", ": pass `path`", the "invalid parameters for `x`:" frame) for every chrome and menu toast (`BoardView::show_error`), and `BoardError::for_key` makes a key's `Unavailable` refusal and a key's command given what it can't use ("select an instance") show nothing, but for the boards-only refusal (SMOKE-3 keeps its toast) — the user's screenshot: ⌥⌘G with nothing selected toasted "command `object.frameSelection` is not available right now: nothing is selected (pass `ids`)"; FigCraft's own keyboard and Figma do nothing then (as the P2.T1 digit ruling already had it) — costs if wrong: a key whose refusal the user needed to read stays silent; its menu item still says why.
- Ruling: menu items that don't apply now are greyed out, and left out of the Actions palette (`sawit_board::menu::applies`): what acts on the selection with nothing selected (`NEEDS_SELECTION`, pinned against the engine's own refusals by `needs_selection_is_what_the_engine_refuses_with_nothing_selected`), Undo and Redo with no step, Delete and heal outside vector edit mode, the dialog items P6-C18 accepted as visible (`NEEDS_INPUT`: text.link, vector.offset, object.moveToPage, object.batchRename, file.colorProfile, pref.nudge) and file.revert; their keys do nothing — supersedes P6-C18's "errors already toast" and P2.T4's ⌘K-while-editing toast for text.link: the user asked for disabled items instead of agent-worded toasts — costs if wrong: until the dialogs come, a user looking for Create link sees it greyed out with no word of why.
- Ruling: every canvas command the keymap editor offers has a default key but the allowlist in `shortcuts::tests::LEFT_UNBOUND` (the dialog items, file.revert, bool.flatten which is Flatten itself, the five Preferences settings and the two Outlines options) — Sawit's keys (`shortcuts::SAWIT_KEYS`) sit beside FigCraft's in `shortcuts::defaults`, which the sheet, the key bindings, the key op and the menus' labels all read; where Sawit keeps FigCraft's Mac key (⌃⌘M, ⌥⌘O, ⌘J, ⇧⌘J) the command takes FigCraft's Windows key with ⌃ for Ctrl; tests prove none is FigCraft's, the app's or another command's key — the user asked for every unset command to get a key — costs if wrong: a later app-wide key on one of these combinations is shadowed on the canvas; `sawits_canvas_keys_take_no_key_of_the_apps` fails first.
- Ruling: the toolbar offers every kind of layer the engine's tools make on the file on show: a board gains Text on a path, Frame and Slice (its Section button becomes the Frame group's, Frame shown first, as on a design file, which keeps the board's toolbar inside an 800 pt window), the six shape tools with Place image…, and Pen and Pencil; a design file gains Text on a path and Place image…; a group of several tools of one kind shows the one used last with a ^ flyout of them all and their keys (`Flyout::Tools`, FigCraft's own toolbar's way), while Move/Hand and the board's ink stay one button each — the user asked for the frame and "all the object list" on the board's toolbar; `every_drawing_tool_makes_a_layer_on_both_kinds_of_file` proves none only errors — costs if wrong: a wider board toolbar, which scrolls sideways in a narrow canvas (P3.T4).
